# Gracehush

You are an expert React Native + Expo engineer helping build Gracehush, a production-quality AI-powered Christian audio prayer journal.

You write clean, simple, maintainable code. You prefer clarity to unnecessary abstraction, because this app is built feature by feature and every piece should be easy to read, explain, and maintain.

Think like a senior mobile developer. Implement like someone shipping a real app that people will trust with their most private prayers.

---

## Scope & Source of Truth

- This file governs the **Gracehush** repo (this folder) only. A parent-folder `AGENTS.md` (for example the one in the folder above this repo, for the Talebrim reader app) belongs to a different product with a different stack. Ignore it here.
- `prompts/AGENTS.md` is a verbatim copy of this file for the step prompts. Edit the root file first, then copy it over. The two must never differ.
- Order of authority: the user's current message, then this file, then the step prompt (`prompts/NN-*.md`) for the details of that step.
- A step prompt may change a decision on purpose. It then says so and updates this file (see "AGENTS.md updates" in `prompts/TEMPLATE.md`), replacing the rule it supersedes instead of adding a second one. If a prompt conflicts with a decision recorded here and does not say it is changing it, treat the prompt as stale: follow this file and flag the conflict in your report.

---

## Instructional Memory

Standing instructions from the user. They stay in force until the user changes them, and they outrank a step prompt that says otherwise. Add each new one here the moment the user gives it.

- **The iOS developer account comes last (2026-10-17).** The paid Apple Developer Program account is the last thing to set up, after the app is built, unless the user asks for it earlier. Until then the iPhone is tested in Expo Go.
  - Do not plan, prepare or run anything that needs that account: EAS iOS builds, `eas.json`, EAS environment variables, device registration, Apple credentials, the Sign in with Apple App ID capability, the Google iOS OAuth client, the Clerk iOS app entry and Apple SSO connection. The prepared steps wait in `prompts/35-ios-developer-account.md`.
  - Code for iOS-only features is still written when a step asks for it (for example the Apple button). What only an iOS development build can check is reported as "not verified on iOS" and waits for step 35. That includes Apple and Google sign-in on the iPhone, track-player lock-screen playback, RevenueCat purchases, and anything else a step logs that way. Android development builds are not affected.
  - Where a step prompt asks for an iPhone or EAS build, do the Android part and report the iPhone part as not verified. If a later step adds a native module that Expo Go does not include, the app may stop launching in Expo Go: say so at that step and ask whether to guard the module or move testing to the Android development build. Do not decide silently.
- **Publishing never exposes a secret or an identifying value (2026-10-22, repeated 2026-10-23).** The GitHub repository is public. No committed file (code, AGENTS.md, a prompt, a test, a commit message) holds a key, a token, the contents of a `.env.local`, the Supabase project ref, the Clerk instance domain, the length of a key, a local path, a personal email address, the Google billing account id, a card's digits or the Google Cloud project id. The rules and the audit are under "Committing and publishing" in Linting and Validation. Commit and push only when the user asks.

---

## Project Overview

Gracehush is a calm, private "audio sanctuary." Users speak or type a raw prayer or journal entry. The app then:

- transcribes the voice entry
- names and validates the user's emotion, with no toxic positivity
- selects one fitting, verified Bible passage (Berean Standard Bible)
- generates a ~60-second personalized spoken devotional with lock-screen playback
- creates three affirmations (morning, afternoon, evening) delivered as local notifications
- tracks active and answered prayers over time
- works offline first and syncs when the device reconnects

Primary audience: practicing Christians aged 25–45, likely female-leaning, who are busy and carrying emotional weight (anxiety, stress, grief, weariness). They often pray out loud in the car or on walks. The app must feel safe, warm, dignified, and private.

Brand mood: **"Warm Strength."** Grounded, mature, feminist-friendly. It should never feel childish, bubblegum pink, or clinical.

## Tech Stack

Use the following stack:

**Client**

- Expo SDK 58 with React Native 0.88 and React 19.3 (New Architecture) and the React Compiler. The project moved up from SDK 57 and React Native 0.86 on 2026-10-24, because the App Store's Expo Go updated itself to SDK 58 and an iPhone can only run the newest Expo Go (see the SDK 58 upgrade entry at the end of the log). The package versions named in the notes below are the ones installed at the step that added them: the upgrade moved every Expo package to its SDK 58 version. The shipped app is a development build, because track-player and other native modules require it. No dev client exists yet (no `expo-dev-client`, no `eas.json`), so until a step needs one, UI work is verified in Expo Go SDK 58 and on the Android emulator (see Linting and Validation). From step 05, Apple and Google sign-in need a development build, while email sign-in and every other screen still run in Expo Go (the Clerk native modules are skipped there). In Expo Go, Google signs in through Clerk's browser flow instead (see Authentication Rules), and Apple shows the calm "isn't available" message.
- React Native + TypeScript
- React Navigation (`NavigationContainer`, native stack, bottom tabs) via `src/navigation/RootNavigator.tsx` and `MainTabs.tsx`. Installed: `@react-navigation/native` and `@react-navigation/native-stack`. Installed in step 08: `@react-navigation/bottom-tabs` 7.21.0. **Expo Router is not used**: never create an `app/` folder or `_layout.tsx`.
- NativeWind / Tailwind CSS for styling
- Zustand for global client state, with `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage` for persisted stores. Installed in step 07: `zustand` 5.0.15 and `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage` 2.2.0 (the SDK 57 pin). Install AsyncStorage with `npx expo install` only: the registry's latest (3.x) is not guaranteed to match Expo Go's native module. Never use the deprecated `AsyncStorage` from `react-native`.
- `@clerk/expo` (the current name of `@clerk/clerk-expo`) for authentication: our own UI on its custom-flow hooks, with `expo-secure-store` for the token cache, `expo-apple-authentication` and `expo-crypto` for Apple (iOS only), and `@clerk/expo-google-signin` for Google. Installed in step 05: `@clerk/expo` 4.13.0, `@clerk/expo-google-signin` 1.1.0, `expo-secure-store` 57.0.4, `expo-apple-authentication` 57.0.2, `expo-crypto` 57.0.4. `expo-auth-session` 57.0.16 was added after step 05, with approval, for the Expo Go Google fallback.
- `@supabase/supabase-js` (added in step 17) with the Clerk session token injected per request through its `accessToken` option: Supabase Third-Party Auth with Clerk, no JWT template. Installed in step 17: `@supabase/supabase-js` 2.118.0 with `npx expo install` (its own `postgrest-js`, `auth-js`, `realtime-js`, `storage-js`, `functions-js`, `phoenix` and `iceberg-js` came with it; nothing else was added)
- `react-native-purchases` (RevenueCat) for subscriptions and paywall purchases
- `expo-audio` for AAC voice recording (`expo-av` is not part of SDK 57; see SDK note below). Installed in step 07: `expo-audio` 57.0.5, with its required peer `expo-asset` 57.0.20 as a direct dependency (native builds need it, and `expo-doctor` fails without it).
- `react-native-track-player` for devotional playback and lock-screen controls
- `expo-notifications` for local, on-device affirmation scheduling. Installed in step 07: `expo-notifications` 57.0.23. It cannot be imported in Expo Go on Android (see the SDK note below).
- `expo-haptics` for small taps through `src/services/haptics.ts` (see "Haptics" under UI Quality Bar). Installed in the step 11 follow-up, with the user's approval: `expo-haptics` 57.0.3 (`npx expo install`; Expo Go includes it, and it has no config plugin).
- Date and time pickers: `@expo/ui/community/datetime-picker` on iOS, `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` (imperative API) on Android. See Date & Time Pickers.
- Local SQLite cache (`expo-sqlite`, or WatermelonDB if it is already in `package.json`) for offline-first entries

**Backend**

- Supabase PostgreSQL 15 with Row Level Security and `pgvector`
- Supabase Storage for raw audio and TTS output
- Supabase Edge Functions (Deno): `POST /process-entry` (built in step 19) and `POST /confirm-scripture` (step 20). Their only dependencies are `@supabase/supabase-js` 2.118.0 (the version the app uses) and `jose` 6.2.13 (Clerk token verification), both pinned in each function's own `deno.json` (`supabase/functions/process-entry/deno.json` and `supabase/functions/confirm-scripture/deno.json`); the app's `package.json` is untouched
- Clerk as the identity provider for Supabase RLS

**External services (server-side only)**

- Google Gemini, paid and active since 2026-10-23 (how it is connected, what it costs and what to do when it fails: "Gemini connection and credit" under Backend / Edge Function Rules; the model is the `GEMINI_MODEL` secret; `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` since step 19's real calls, because `gemini-3.8-flash` took 30 to 90 s per call on a free key; on the paid tier since 2026-10-23 it takes 3 to 7 s, so the choice is open again for step 32), called through the Interactions API in two stages (step 21): a crisis screening first, which also transcribes a spoken entry (`GEMINI_SCREENING_MODEL` may name a model of its own for it, and `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL` a second model that every second attempt of both stages goes to while the first is busy), then, only for an entry the screening called clear, one structured reply with the emotion, scripture location, devotional, closing prayer, affirmations and a prayer title and summary, written from the screened words alone
- HelloAO Free Use Bible API (bible.helloao.org, no key): the Berean Standard Bible text, read by `process-entry` and `confirm-scripture` (step 20) in the API's simplified chapter format
- Google Cloud Text-to-Speech (step 22): the spoken devotional, as a lossless WAV (`LINEAR16`; the MP3 this API writes is fixed at 32 kbps and was heard as faintly robotic, so the WAV was chosen on 2026-10-24: see the vendor note), in a Chirp 3 HD voice named by the `TTS_VOICE_NAME` secret (Aoede at a speaking rate of 0.88, chosen by the user after listening on 2026-10-24). This API rejects API keys (checked on 2026-10-23), so the function signs in as a service account (`GOOGLE_TTS_CREDENTIALS_B64`). Speech is optional: with no credentials set the function makes the reflection as text and nothing else changes. See the Cloud Text-to-Speech vendor note

Do not introduce new major libraries unless there is a strong reason.

### SDK and Vendor Version Notes

Before touching these areas, check `package.json`, `app.json`, and the current vendor docs:

- **expo-av** is not part of SDK 57 (it is missing from `bundledNativeModules.json`), so voice recording uses `expo-audio` (decided in the step 07 review). Its config plugin defaults to `enableBackgroundPlayback: true`, which adds the iOS `audio` background mode and an Android media-playback foreground service, so always set `enableBackgroundRecording` and `enableBackgroundPlayback` explicitly. Step 07 set both to `false` in `app.json`, so a build has no `audio` background mode and no media-playback foreground service. Step 23 decides background recording and step 26 decides playback.
- **expo-notifications** (57.0.x): its config plugin has no `enableRemoteNotifications` option (that is newer than SDK 57) and always adds the iOS `aps-environment` entitlement. Remote push does not run in Expo Go on Android, and the package cannot even be imported there: Expo Go 57.0.9 did not register `ExpoTopicSubscriptionModule` (not rechecked on Expo Go 58, so the guard stays), so the import throws `Cannot find native module` at startup (found in step 07, with the user's approval to guard it). `src/services/notifications.ts` therefore skips the import in Expo Go on Android only (`Platform.OS === "android" && isRunningInExpoGo()`) and answers `"undetermined"` without a prompt. A development build and iOS always load it, so a real failure there is never hidden. The iPhone in Expo Go and the Android development build (step 05b) are where notification permission, channels and scheduling can be verified. On Android 13+ the channel must exist before the permission request, and a never-asked permission is reported as `denied` with `canAskAgain: true`, not `undetermined`, so decide from `canAskAgain` as well as `status`. Use the `AndroidImportance` enum, never numbers (its values changed).
- **Zustand `persist`** (5.x): when the storage read throws or the saved JSON is corrupt, the store never becomes hydrated (`hasHydrated()` stays false and `onFinishHydration` never fires), so a splash gate waits forever. Persisted stores use `src/store/safeStorage.ts`, which turns a failed read into "nothing saved".
- **Gemini API** (read from ai.google.dev on 2026-10-22 in the step 19 review; these pages change quickly, so read them again before editing `gemini.ts`). The structured-output, audio and model pages now document only the **Interactions API** (`POST https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/interactions`, key in the `x-goog-api-key` header), which Google calls generally available and recommended for new projects; `generateContent` is called legacy but fully supported. `process-entry` uses Interactions, and `supabase/functions/process-entry/gemini.ts` is the only file that knows the wire format. A request holds `model`, `system_instruction`, `input` (a string, or a text part and an `{ "type": "audio", "data": <base64>, "mime_type": "audio/m4a" }` part), `response_format: { "type": "text", "mime_type": "application/json", "schema": … }`, `generation_config: { "thinking_level", "max_output_tokens" }` and `"store": false`; the reply is a timeline of steps, and the text is in the `model_output` step's `content`. **`store` defaults to true, which keeps the request and the reply on Google's servers for 55 days (1 day on the free tier). A private prayer is never stored, so every request sets it to false, and a test checks.** Gemini 3 and later take no `temperature`, `top_p` or `top_k` (the 3.8 Flash migration list says to remove them), so none is sent. `thinking_level` is `low`, `medium` or `high` (default `medium`; `minimal` is an error on 3.8 Flash), and the function sends `low` unless `GEMINI_THINKING_LEVEL` says otherwise. Never hardcode a model name. The step 19 review chose `gemini-3.8-flash` (stable on 2026-10-22; the 2.5 models are restricted to accounts that already used them), and the real calls of 2026-10-23 overturned that (see "Real calls" at the end of this note): the value in use is `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`. The schema may only use what the docs list (types, a `"null"` inside a type array, `enum`, `required`, `items`, `maxItems`); `minLength` and `maxLength` are ignored by the server, so every limit is checked again in `output.ts`. The supported audio list has `audio/m4a` and not `audio/mp4` or `audio/x-m4a`, so the function always sends `audio/m4a`; audio costs 32 tokens a second on paper (measured 25 a second on `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`: a 199.8 s clip was 5,565 input tokens, about 570 of them text); the whole request may be 20 MB, which is why a recording is capped at 12 MiB (base64 adds a third, so 15 MiB would be exactly 20 MiB). A blocked request fails with HTTP 400 and an error code of `safety`, `jailbreak`, `prohibited_content` or `blocklist`; the default safety threshold on Gemini 2.5 and later is off. Price on 2026-10-22 for `gemini-3.8-flash`: $0.75 per million input tokens and $3.75 per million output tokens (thinking included) through 2026-12-31, then $1.50 and $7.50; the free tier costs nothing. `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` (pricing page, 2026-10-23): $0.25 per million input tokens (text, image, video), $0.50 audio, $1.50 output (thinking included), with a free tier; `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` $0.30 and $2.50; `gemini-3.5-flash` is not on the page. **Real calls (step 19, 2026-10-23, a free-tier key made in AI Studio).** (1) That key starts with `AQ.`, not `AIza`: since 2026-05-28 every AI Studio key is an "auth key" (ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/api-key, which also says an unrestricted classic key is rejected). It works in the `x-goog-api-key` header on the Interactions API (HTTP 200), although forum threads from June to August 2026 report 401 `ACCESS_TOKEN_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` for other accounts, and nothing in the function depends on the prefix. (2) **`gemini-3.8-flash` was unusable on this key: 63 to 77 s for a full reflection and 32 to 88 s for the one-word request "ready", through Interactions and through `generateContent` alike, while the network to Google took 0.3 to 0.5 s; the function stops a call after 45 s, so every request would have failed with a 503.** `gemini-3.7-flash` answered 503 "currently experiencing high demand", `gemini-3.5-flash` answered 503 or 429 in 3 of 4 cases, and `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` answered every case in 1.4 to 4.2 s on the first attempt (a written entry: 548 to 571 tokens in, 620 to 658 out, 92 to 144 thinking, about $0.0013; the 3 min 20 s recording: 5,565 in, 900 to 930 out, 1,350 to 1,915 thinking, about $0.0065). The key lists 46 Gemini models. A new model may simply be queued: time it again with a billed key before choosing it (step 25) and in the step 32 comparison. (3) The reply is `{ status: "completed", steps: [{ type: "thought", signature }, { type: "model_output", content: [{ type: "text", text }] }], usage: { total_tokens, total_input_tokens, input_tokens_by_modality, total_cached_tokens, total_output_tokens, total_tool_use_tokens, total_thought_tokens }, service_tier: "standard", model, created, updated, object }`. `total_tokens` is input plus output plus thinking: the thinking count is separate from the output count and billed as output. `thinking_level: low` still spends 90 to 150 thinking tokens on a written entry and 1,350 to 1,915 on a three-minute recording. (4) Interactions errors name themselves in lowercase (`{ error: { code: "service_unavailable", message } }`, no `status`), so `gemini.ts` keeps an error word in the log reason when it is ALL_CAPS or snake_case, and never the message. (5) The model writes both "Psalm" and "Psalms" for one book (step 20 must accept both), and capitalises "You/Your" for God in some prayers and not in others (step 32).
- **Gemini safety settings and the crisis screening** (step 21; the docs at ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/safety-settings and api-errors were read on 2026-10-23, and every claim below was run against the real API). The Interactions API takes `safety_settings: [{ "type", "threshold" }]`, in lowercase, and not the `generateContent` `safetySettings` that the step 21 prompt text names. The five types are `harassment`, `hate_speech`, `sexually_explicit`, `dangerous_content` and `jailbreak`, and the thresholds are `off`, `block_only_high`, `block_medium_and_above` and `block_low_and_above`. The default is `off` for Gemini 2.5 and later, so the screening states `off` for all five and changes nothing. The real API accepted exactly those five types (each alone, and all together) and answered anything else (`self_harm` as a type, `none` as a threshold) with HTTP 400 `{ error: { code: "invalid_request", message } }`, which is a plain failure and not a block. A block is HTTP 400 with the error code `safety`, `jailbreak`, `prohibited_content` or `blocklist` (the last three with a message that starts `Input blocked:`), and a background interaction fails with the same code in its `errors` list. This API has no `promptFeedback` and no `finishReason`, so `isBlockedBody` in `gemini.ts` is the whole check; every error body seen on 2026-10-23 was wrapped in `error` (a bad key as an array). The docs name no `seed`, and their troubleshooting page says to keep `temperature`, `top_p` and `top_k` at their defaults on Gemini 3.x (a lower temperature can cause looping), so the prompt's "temperature 0" was not applied and the screening is not repeatable: its safety comes from the three layers and from failing safe. The REST example sends an `Api-Revision: 2026-05-20` header; the function sends none, and every call of 2026-10-23 worked without it. Google's terms say an application that uses less restrictive safety settings may be subject to review. **Measured on 2026-10-23** on a free-tier key with `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` and thinking low (see the Step 21 log entry for the full numbers): a screening call costs about 420 tokens in, under 100 out and 100 to 200 thinking, which is about $0.0003 on paper; it took 1 to 4 s for a clear written entry (two of fifteen took about 10 s) and 4 to 23 s for one it flagged (it thinks longer about risk); and the free-tier model sometimes answers `503 service_unavailable` after about 20 s, which is a screening with no answer, so nothing is made and the person can try again. A billed key is expected to be steadier: time it again before step 25.
- **Gemini under heavy demand, and how the function retries** (step 21 follow-up, 2026-10-23; Google's troubleshooting, API errors, rate limits and priority inference pages read that day, last updated 2026-09-20 to 2026-10-15). The first hosted run of the step 21 check (it ended about 14:56 UTC) failed every case that needed the model with `503 AI_UNAVAILABLE` after 25 to 44 s, while the crisis that a phrase decided passed in 1.3 s. Probes from this machine minutes later, with the same free key and the screening's real request: `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` answered HTTP 503 `{ error: { code: "service_unavailable" } }`, its message saying the model "is currently experiencing high demand, spikes in demand are usually temporary", in 3 of 5 calls (after 3.3, 4.7 and 19.4 s), gave no answer within 90 s once and answered once in 17.8 s (a few minutes earlier the same request took 1.0 to 13.0 s), while in the same minutes `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` answered 5 of 5 (0.8 to 19.4 s) and `gemini-flash-lite-latest` 5 of 5 (0.8 to 20.4 s). So the free tier queues, one model under demand fails while another answers, and nothing in the function was broken. Google's pages: 503 `service_unavailable` is "temporarily overloaded or down", and 503 and 429 (`rate_limit_exceeded`, `too_many_requests`) are retried "with exponential backoff" (for example 2, 4 and 8 s, with random jitter and a cap on the retries; Google's Python SDK retries a transient error up to four times), while 429 `quota_exceeded` is the daily quota. The rate limits page does not say that a paid tier gets capacity first ("Specified rate limits are not guaranteed and actual capacity may vary"), so a billed key alone is not known to stop 503s, and the current pages do not mention switching to another model. Priority inference (`service_tier: "priority"` on the Interactions API; both Flash-Lite models are listed) costs 75 to 100% more, has 0.3x the standard rate limits, and over them is downgraded to standard processing instead of failing with a 503 or 429; it is paid and was not tried. **What the function does since (`gemini.ts`):** at most four attempts per call; after a busy, failed or timed-out attempt a pause of about 2, 4 and then 8 s (a quarter either way), and never a pause that leaves under 8 s for another attempt; a reply that fails the output rules is asked for once more at once (two at most, since the service works); with `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL` set every second attempt goes to that model, with the same request otherwise. Our own timeout is logged as `gemini_timeout` and a dropped connection as `gemini_network`. The crisis screening ends 60 s after the request started (`screenTiming.deadlineMs`), so the reflection always keeps 30 s of the 90.
- **Gemini API terms** (read from Google's Gemini API Additional Terms of Service in the step 16 follow-up, 2026-10-22): on *unpaid* services (Google AI Studio and the API's unpaid quota) Google may use what is submitted to improve its products and human reviewers may read it, and on *paid* services it does not (a project with an active Cloud Billing account counts as paid). A real user's prayer therefore goes only to a billed project; test data may use the free tier. Re-read on 2026-10-22 for step 19 (effective 2026-03-23, updated 2026-04-28): the substance is unchanged, and the unpaid-services text now says outright "Do not submit sensitive, confidential, or personal information to the Unpaid Services." So step 19 sends made-up text only, with a free-tier key, and **the hosted `GEMINI_API_KEY` must belong to a project with Cloud Billing before step 25 sends a real person's prayer** (billing was set up on 2026-10-23: see the Google billing entry in the log). Cloud Text-to-Speech's data logging was checked in the step 22 review (2026-10-23): Google's data-logging page says it does not log any customer Cloud TTS text or audio data, and it says nothing about training; the paid Google Cloud terms apply, so the Cloud Billing account must be active before a real person's devotional is sent to it (an account still on the free trial is what Google's billing page calls ineligible for the paid Gemini tier). The terms page may have changed since this was written.
- **HelloAO Free Use Bible API** (read from bible.helloao.org on 2026-10-23 in the step 20 review; the data's Last-Modified date is 2026-10-12). No key, no account, no published usage limit, and caching is allowed (the home page says so); AO Lab is a nonprofit and the site has a Donate link. It is static JSON on S3 behind CloudFront (`Cache-Control: max-age=86400`): a chapter is 5 to 32 KB, took 0.2 to 0.8 s from Lagos, and a chapter that does not exist is a plain 404. The BSB's `books.json` says `publicDomain: true`, `attribution: not-required`, `commercialUse: allowed`, and lists 66 books, 1,189 chapters and 31,086 verses. **The code reads `GET /api/BSB/{ID}/{chapter}.simple.json`, the Simplified Format (documented at /docs/reference/translations/simplified.html): each verse is one string, lines of poetry are separated by `\n`, and footnotes are held apart.** The standard format is not used: its verse parts (`string`, `{noteId}`, `{lineBreak}`, `{text, poem}` and one `{text, descriptive}`) have no spaces between them, the `wordsOfJesus` and inline-heading parts that its docs describe do not occur in the BSB, and rebuilding a verse by hand puts a lone closing quote on its own line. The simplified text has the same letters and marks as the standard text in all 31,086 verses, but 363 of them (1.2%) have spacing defects: a word glued to the sentence before it ("good.His loving devotion", "poor?”declares the LORD", "forever.Selah", a speaker label in the Song of Solomon) or a closing mark on its own line or after a space ("heel.\n”", "offspring. ”"). `tidyVerse` in `_shared/bible/helloao.ts` repairs only whitespace, and `_shared/bible/sweep_check.ts` proves on the whole translation that no letter or mark changes and that no defect is left. **`numberOfVerses` is the number of verses present, not the highest verse number:** 15 chapters leave numbers out (Matthew 17:21, 18:11 and 23:14; Mark 7:16, 9:44 and 9:46, 11:26 and 15:28; Luke 17:36 and 23:17; John 5:4; Acts 8:37, 15:34, 24:7 and 28:29; Romans 16:24), so a verse exists when the chapter lists it. A USFM id can start with a digit (`1SA` to `3JN`), `books.json` gives the Song of Solomon the id `SNG`, the name "Song" and the commonName "Song of Solomon" (the code uses the commonName), and the book of Psalms is written "Psalm" in a reference. The longest verse is 400 characters (median 114). Real check on 2026-10-23 with `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` and a `book_id` enum of the 66 ids, on made-up entries: 14 of 14 chosen passages exist in the BSB and fit their entry, and the follow-up request for another passage gave a real one 5 times in 5 (1.1 to 1.6 s, about $0.0003). A second function needs its own `deno.json` with the same two pins and its own `[functions.<name>] verify_jwt = false` block in `supabase/config.toml`. Deno scopes `deno test` to the folder of the `--config` file, so `deno test … supabase/functions` runs only `process-entry`'s tests: the test command names every function folder and `_shared`. **Read from the hosted functions (step 20 logs, 2026-10-23):** the first read of a chapter on an instance took 34 ms and a later read 4 ms (a cache hit would read 0), so the Bible is a negligible part of a request; the model and the database are the rest.
- **Google Cloud Text-to-Speech** (step 22; read from docs.cloud.google.com/text-to-speech and cloud.google.com/text-to-speech/pricing on 2026-10-23, and every claim about signing in was run against the real endpoints with made-up credentials). **It rejects API keys.** `texttospeech.googleapis.com` answered 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED`, "API keys are not supported by this API. Expected OAuth2 access token or other authentication credentials that assert a principal", to the project's Gemini key in the `x-goog-api-key` header and in `?key=` alike, and the current pages document only service accounts, application default credentials and OAuth tokens (the REST reference names the scope `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform`). So the function signs in as a service account: it signs a JWT with the account's private key (RS256, with `jose`, no new dependency), posts it to `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token`, and calls the API with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (`_shared/tts/googleAuth.ts`). A made-up account got HTTP 400 `invalid_grant` from the token endpoint (a well-formed request about an unknown account) and a made-up token got 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED` with the reason `ACCESS_TOKEN_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`. The account's key file is one secret, `GOOGLE_TTS_CREDENTIALS_B64`: the JSON reduced to `type`, `client_email` and `private_key`, as one line of base64 (a Windows PowerShell 5.1 recipe is in prompt 22 and was run: the function's parser returns the identical key). It is a secret that can spend money: it never enters the repo, a chat, a screenshot or a log, and the downloaded file is deleted once the secret is set. **The request:** `POST https://texttospeech.googleapis.com/v1/text:synthesize` with `{ input: { markup }, voice: { languageCode, name }, audioConfig: { audioEncoding: "LINEAR16", speakingRate } }` (MP3 until 2026-10-24: see below). `markup` is the Chirp 3 HD field that reads the tags `[pause short]`, `[pause]` and `[pause long]` ("pause tags will only work in the markup field"); `speakingRate` runs from 0.25 to 2.0; pitch is not sent (the Chirp 3 HD page lists none); a voice is named `<locale>-Chirp3-HD-<Name>` (the page lists 14 female voices: Achernar, Aoede, Autonoe, Callirrhoe, Despina, Erinome, Gacrux, Kore, Laomedeia, Leda, Pulcherrima, Sulafat, Vindemiatrix and Zephyr; and 16 male); the answer is `{ audioContent }`, base64. The Chirp 3 HD page lists MP3 for non-streaming requests (its "batch" row), and the first real calls on 2026-10-24 confirmed it: HTTP 200 for every file, an MP3 of MPEG-2 Layer III, 24,000 Hz, mono, 32 kbps constant (about 4 KB a second), with no ID3 tag and no Xing or Info frame. **That MP3 is not used any more.** At 32 kbps the voice was heard as faintly robotic (the user, 2026-10-24, on a real reflection), and the rate is fixed: the v1 enum has only `MP3` (32 kbps), the v1beta1 enum adds `MP3_64_KBPS` but the API refused it for these voices ("This voice currently only supports the following audio encodings: LINEAR16, MP3, OGG_OPUS, MULAW, ALAW"), `sampleRateHertz: 48000` still gives 32 kbps, and `OGG_OPUS` is a dead end (the bucket does not take it and iPhones do not play it). The function asks for **`LINEAR16`**, a plain WAV (a 44-byte header: RIFF, a 16-byte `fmt ` chunk, `data`; PCM, mono, 24,000 Hz, 16-bit, so 48,000 bytes a second: a two-minute devotional is about 5.5 MB, twelve times the MP3), and the user listened to the lossless file and chose it. The function reads the length from the header (`wavDuration.ts`, exact to the sample; it walks the chunks and counts the bytes that are there when the size field is a placeholder or past the end), and the `journal_media` bucket accepts `audio/wav` since the migration of 2026-10-24. Parsing Google's 6.5 MB reply and decoding the 4.9 MB file took 17 ms in all, so the step costs no CPU worth counting; the upload is the larger file. **Limits** (quotas page): 5,000 bytes a request (the function stops at 4,800) and 200 Chirp 3 requests a minute for a project. **Price** (2026-10-23): the first 1 million characters a month are free for Chirp 3 HD, then $30 per million; characters are counted with spaces and newlines, SSML tags are counted too (the pause tags are assumed to count), and "You must enable billing to use Text-to-Speech", even for the free part. A devotional script is about 1,300 to 1,700 characters, so about $0.04 to $0.05 after the free million (about 600 devotionals a month are free). **Data:** Google's data-logging page says "Google does not log any customer Cloud TTS text or audio data" and is silent on training; the Google Cloud terms apply to the paid account. **Considered and not chosen:** Gemini-TTS inside the same API (`gemini-2.5-flash-tts`, `gemini-2.5-pro-tts`, `gemini-3.1-flash-tts-preview`; MP3 for non-streaming requests; a style prompt instead of a speaking rate; the tags `[short pause]`, `[medium pause]`, `[long pause]`; 4,000 bytes of text and 4,000 of prompt; $10 to $20 per million audio tokens at 25 tokens a second, so about $0.025 for 100 s of Flash audio; no free allowance). It is cheaper and steerable, but it is a language model, so a verse or a prayer could be changed or skipped, and several of its voices are Preview. Chirp 3 HD stays unless the audition disappoints, and switching is a follow-up (other request fields and tags). On 2026-10-24 the user found the first real devotional short of feeling as well as faintly robotic; the robotic part was the MP3 (above), and the feeling was left as it is. Gemini-TTS was probed that day and parked: it needs the Agent Platform API (`aiplatform.googleapis.com`) switched on in the project (the call answered 403 `SERVICE_DISABLED`); its request is `input.text` with `input.prompt`, `voice.name` a bare name such as `Aoede` and `voice.modelName` (`gemini-2.5-flash-tts`, `gemini-2.5-pro-tts` or `gemini-3.1-flash-tts-preview`); the docs of 2026-10-22 say freeform text is "spoken exactly as written" and that some style tags are vocalized instead of acted on; and the real price page of 2026-10-24 lists Gemini 2.5 Flash TTS at $0.50 per million text tokens in and $10 per million audio tokens out, 2.5 Pro and 3.1 Flash at $1 and $20, with 25 audio tokens a second and no free usage (about $0.025 to $0.05 a devotional). The user chose the lossless Chirp file instead, which reads scripture with no language model between the text and the voice. The Gemini API's own TTS models (`gemini-3.8-flash-tts` and the others the key lists) were not used either: they return WAV, raw PCM, mu-law or A-law and no MP3 (an objection that fell on 2026-10-24, when the devotional became a WAV anyway; what still rules them out is that they are language models reading the text, as above). **Voice and speaking rate: Aoede at 0.88** (`TTS_VOICE_NAME=en-US-Chirp3-HD-Aoede` and `TTS_SPEAKING_RATE=0.88`; a voice name is not a secret), chosen by the user on 2026-10-24 after listening to the audition's eight files (Aoede, Leda, Sulafat and Achernar at 0.88 and 0.95; the Step 22 entry has their lengths). Both are in the local `.env.local`, and the hosted project gets them with `secrets set` at the deploy. The code's `DEFAULT_SPEAKING_RATE` stays 0.92 for a project that sets no rate: the chosen rate travels in the secret. The price page was read again on 2026-10-23 in the step 22 build and still says: Chirp 3 HD 0 to 1 million characters free a month, then $30 per million, billing must be enabled, and spaces, newlines and tags are counted. **Supabase limits** are unchanged (150 s, 2 s of CPU, 256 MB, a 5 MB server-side bundle): the speech step is network time, not CPU.
- **Supabase Edge Functions** (read from supabase.com/docs/guides/functions on 2026-10-22, step 19 review). Free-plan limits: 150 s of wall clock (a request still unanswered then gets a 504), 2 s of CPU per request (waiting on the network is free), 256 MB of memory, a 5 MB bundle when it is bundled on Supabase's side (20 MB when bundled locally with Docker), 100 secrets, and no secret name that starts with `SUPABASE_`. The platform provides `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_DB_URL`, `SUPABASE_JWKS`, `SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEYS` and `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEYS` (JSON objects keyed by name; the key named `default` is the one the dashboard shows) and, for older projects, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`; `_shared/admin.ts` reads the first and falls back to the last. The platform's `verify_jwt` is a "legacy auth verification check" that only understands Supabase's legacy HS256 tokens (supabase.com/docs/guides/troubleshooting/edge-function-401-error-response), so a Clerk RS256 token gets the platform's own 401 "Invalid JWT" before any code runs: it needs `verify_jwt = false` (`supabase/config.toml`), and `_shared/auth.ts` verifies the token instead. A new-format key (`sb_publishable_…`, `sb_secret_…`) goes in the `apikey` header, and supabase-js 2.118 never sends one to a function as a bearer token. A secret changed in the dashboard or with the CLI is read by the running function at once; a change to the code needs a deploy. **There is no Docker on this machine**, so `supabase start` and `functions serve` do not work: the function is tested with Deno through `npx`, and deployed with `supabase functions deploy process-entry --project-ref <ref> --use-api` (the bundle is built on Supabase's side). Every Deno command run from the repo root needs `--no-lock --node-modules-dir=none` (and `--config supabase/functions/process-entry/deno.json` for the import map): with the app's `package.json` at the root, Deno otherwise looks for `node_modules` and cannot resolve an `npm:` import (reproduced here with Deno 2.9.6). Deno 2.9's linter also refuses an inline `npm:` specifier, which is why the two versions live in `deno.json`. **Hosted facts (step 19, deployed 2026-10-23 by the user with `functions deploy process-entry --project-ref <ref> --use-api`).** The CLI uploaded the 19 files that `index.ts` imports (the function and `_shared`, no tests), and a local `deno bundle` of the same entry is 107 modules and 0.83 MB, far under the 5 MB limit. `verify_jwt = false` in `supabase/config.toml` was honoured with no flag, so the same command keeps the platform check off on later deploys (an anonymous POST with no token and one with `Bearer aaa.bbb.ccc` both got the function's own 401 body, not the platform's). `secrets list` showed `CLERK_ISSUER`, `GEMINI_API_KEY` and `GEMINI_MODEL` beside the platform's `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_DB_URL`, `SUPABASE_JWKS`, `SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEYS`, `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEYS`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` and `SUPABASE_URL`, so this project has both key formats (a terminal copy of that list showed some lines twice: a redraw, since a secret name is unique). Supabase runs a function in the region nearest the caller (`x-sb-edge-region: eu-west-3` for a phone in Lagos) while the database is in us-east-1, which cost 0.5 to 2.3 s of `dbMs` per request that touches the database (an `x-region` request header can pin a region). A cold request took 2.1 s against 0.8 s warm; auth takes 0 to 1 ms once the Clerk key set is cached (66 to 140 ms on a cold worker); the dashboard's own log lines `booted (time: 35ms)` and `shutdown` are the platform's, not ours. `supabase login` keeps its token outside the repo, and the CLI creates `supabase/.temp/` (ignored); the implementer never uses that token.
- **jose 6.2.13** (run under Deno in the step 19 review). `jwtVerify` checks `exp` and `nbf` only when they are present, so a token with no expiry verifies unless `requiredClaims: ["exp", "sub"]` is passed (`_shared/auth.ts` passes it). `clockTolerance` is in seconds. `createRemoteJWKSet` fetches the key set once and caches it, refetching only for an unknown key id and not more often than its `cooldownDuration`. A key set that cannot be fetched is not a claim error: the function answers 500, never 401, because a 401 makes the app drop a good session. Clerk's Frontend API URL is the token's `iss` and serves `/.well-known/jwks.json` (RS256, `cache-control: public, max-age=14400`); this project's development instance is `https://<dev-instance>.clerk.accounts.dev` (its real name is left out of this public file on purpose: a publishable key is `pk_test_` plus that domain in base64), read from the publishable key and confirmed by its key set and by the Third-Party Auth entry of step 17. The dev panel's Supabase check shows the real token's issuer host, which must equal `CLERK_ISSUER`. `sts` is `pending` for a session with an unfinished task, `azp` may be missing on a native app's token, and the Clerk Supabase integration adds the top-level `role: "authenticated"`.
- **Clerk ↔ Supabase** (decided in the step 17 review, 2026-10-22): Supabase **Third-Party Auth with Clerk**. The app sends Clerk's normal session token (`getToken()` with no template) as the bearer token; the Clerk dashboard's Supabase integration adds the claim `role: "authenticated"` to it, and Supabase checks the token against Clerk's public keys, so no JWT secret is shared and no "supabase" JWT template exists or may be created (Clerk and Supabase deprecated that route on 1 April 2025). The database sees the Clerk user id as `auth.jwt() ->> 'sub'`, a text, so policies use `public.requesting_user_id()` and never `auth.uid()`, which casts to a uuid and fails. Do not switch integrations without asking.
- **Expo Router is installed but unused.** The app navigates with React Navigation. Since SDK 56 the Expo CLI refuses `@react-navigation/*` imports while `expo-router` is installed, so the root `.env` holds the non-secret flag `EXPO_ROUTER_DISABLE_RN_NAVIGATION_CHECK=1`. Keep that file committed (only `.env*.local` is gitignored) and do not uninstall `expo-router` without asking. If it is ever removed, delete the flag. SDK 58's CLI no longer has this guard (the flag is not read anywhere in `@expo/cli` 58.1.15) and `expo-router` 58 no longer depends on `@react-navigation/*`, so the flag is now unneeded but harmless, and removing `expo-router` stays a decision to ask about.
- **Date and time pickers.** `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` 9.x deprecates `onChange` in favor of `onValueChange` / `onDismiss`. On the user's iPhone its iOS view never reported changes under the New Architecture (upstream issue #965), so iOS uses Expo UI's picker. Its Android _component_ re-opens the dialog on every value change, so Android uses the imperative `DateTimePickerAndroid.open`. See Date & Time Pickers.
- **expo-symbols on Android** scales the glyph with the system font size but not its box, which clips icons at large font sizes. Render icons through `src/components/Icon.tsx`.
- **@react-navigation/bottom-tabs** (7.21, read from its source in the step 08 review): a custom `tabBar` is rendered in normal flow below the scenes, so a scene already ends where the bar begins. Never add the bar's height to a screen's scroll padding. `useBottomTabBarHeight()` returns the default bar's estimate unless a custom bar reports its own height through `BottomTabBarHeightCallbackContext`, so this app does not use it. The scene background option is `sceneStyle`. A custom bar must emit `tabPress` (with `canPreventDefault`) before it navigates, as the default bar does, so `useScrollToTop` keeps working. On iOS under the New Architecture, React Native maps `accessibilityRole="tab"` to no trait and speaks no role (the legacy code that spoke it does not run on Fabric), so a tab's label has to say "tab" and its position (React Navigation's default bar does this on iOS only); on Android the role itself is announced, so the label is just the name.
- **A percentage offset on an absolutely positioned child resolves against the parent's content box** (React Native 0.86, found in step 08 on the Android emulator): the parent's padding and borders are not part of 100%. `FloatingInputDock` sits above the tab bar with `bottom: "100%"`, and while it was a child of the padded, bordered bar it ended at the bar's content height, so it overlapped the bar by the bottom inset plus the border (the mic ended 5dp below the bar's top instead of 20dp above it). It now sits in an outer view with no padding or border, beside the visible bar. Do the same for anything else that must follow a bar's real height (for example a mini player), or measure with `onLayout`.
- **`expo lint` runs the React Compiler rules as errors** (eslint-plugin-react-hooks 7.1.1, the `recommended` preset through eslint-config-expo; checked in the step 09 review by linting candidate code through stdin). These fail: `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` (a `setState` directly in an effect body, or in a local function the effect calls before its first `await`) and `react-hooks/refs` (reading or writing `ref.current` during render, which includes the common `useRef(new Animated.Value(1)).current`: create an animated value with `useState(() => new Animated.Value(1))`, as `GracehushTabBar` does). These pass: `setState` inside a promise callback, an event-listener callback, an event handler or a `useFocusEffect` callback; a request counter in a ref used only in callbacks and effects; `useState(() => new Date())`; a previous value kept in state and compared during render; an effect that only sets state from a timer callback; `ListHeaderComponent` passed as an element; a `useRef` written and read only inside responder handlers (a scrubber's grab), a zustand action called from an effect, and a module-level `setInterval` and `AppState` listener in a store file (checked through stdin in the step 12 review, and by `npm run lint` on the built `playerStore.ts`, `ProgressScrubber.tsx` and `NowPlayingScreen.tsx`). `new Date()` in render also passes lint but is impure, and the compiler memoizes render, so keep a clock in state and refresh it on focus and on `AppState` `active`. Define components at module level (`react-hooks/static-components`).
- **`FlatList` clones `ListEmptyComponent` with a `style` prop and an `onLayout`, and wraps `ListHeaderComponent` in a plain `View`** (React Native 0.86.3 `VirtualizedList.render`, read in the step 09 review). An empty state that must center below the header is therefore an element whose root is a `View` that takes that `style`, with `flex: 1`, inside a content container with `flexGrow: 1`. A component used there has to forward `style` to its root, or the layout is silently ignored.
- **React Navigation 7 `navigate(name, params)` reuses the route that is already on top** (`StackRouter` NAVIGATE: "If the route matches the current one, then navigate to it") and replaces its params, so a double tap on a card opens one screen and needs no guard (checked in the step 09 review). A second tap on a different card swaps the params, which is why the dock keeps its `opening` ref guard: its `mode` must not change. **It does not go back to a route that is deeper in the stack: it pushes a second copy** (checked in the step 11 review by running the repo's own `StackRouter`, `@react-navigation/routers` 7.6.5: from `[MainTabs, Reflection]`, `navigate("MainTabs", { screen: "Journal" })` gives `[MainTabs, Reflection, MainTabs]`, and Android back then shows Reflection again). To return to a route that is already in the stack use `popTo(name, params)`, which gives `[MainTabs]` there (after New Entry's `replace` too); the tab navigator below consumes the new `screen` param when its route's params change (`useNavigationBuilder`, `@react-navigation/core` 7.24), and when no such route is below, `popTo` replaces the current one (a notification opening Reflection first, step 28). `navigate(name, params, { pop: true })` is the other way. Reflection's "Return to Journal" uses `popTo`.
- **A `Pressable` is accessible by default and groups its children into one accessibility element** (React Native's `accessible` prop), so a button inside a pressable card cannot be reached on its own with VoiceOver. Step 11's `MiniPlayerCard` makes the card `accessible={false}` (it still takes touches) and puts the labels on the play button and the "Open full player" link. Do the same for any tappable card that holds a control, and never put an accessible element inside another. (Step 11 emulator check, `uiautomator dump --compressed`: on Android that card is still listed as one clickable node with a label derived from its children's text, "Peace in the Midst, 1 min • Personalized devotional", and the play button, the spoken time and "Open full player" stay separate nodes. How iOS lists it was not verified.)
- **native-stack, `beforeRemove` and `usePreventRemove`** (`@react-navigation/native-stack` 7.21 and `@react-navigation/core`, read in the step 10 review). Every action that removes a route fires `beforeRemove` for it, `replace` included (`shouldPreventRemove` runs for each route that is missing from the next state), so a guard that blocks every removal also blocks the screen's own `navigation.replace(...)`. `usePreventRemove(prevent, callback)` (exported from `@react-navigation/native`) is the one that stops a native iOS dismissal such as swiping a modal down and still calls the screen back (`gestureEnabled: false` also stops the swipe, but silently): it registers the route in `PreventRemoveContext`, and native-stack then passes `preventNativeDismiss` to the screen. A bare `addListener("beforeRemove")` with `preventDefault()` only works for JS-driven back (Android): on iOS the screen is already gone natively and the library logs "removed natively but didn't get removed from JS state". The callback receives `data.action`, and dispatching it again passes the guard (it carries the routes already asked), so do that after the user confirms. To leave on purpose while the guard is on (a `replace` to the next screen, a Close button), set a ref first and re-dispatch `data.action` from the callback when the ref is set. `prevent` is a plain boolean prop, so it cannot be switched off and used in the same tick. The pattern passes the lint rules below.
- **Expo CLI rewrites `tsconfig.json` and deletes the generated, gitignored `expo-env.d.ts`** once typed routes are off, which drops the `*.css` module declaration from typecheck. Ambient declarations live in committed files under `src/types/`.
- **Expo Go runs one SDK at a time on an iPhone.** The App Store updates it by itself to the newest SDK, an older Expo Go cannot be installed on an iPhone, and a project on an older SDK then shows "Project is incompatible with this version of Expo Go". That happened on 2026-10-24: SDK 58 reached the App Store and the SDK 57 project stopped opening on the user's iPhone (see the SDK 58 upgrade entry at the end of the log). Use the Expo Go that matches the project's SDK, SDK 58 now (on Android, the `Expo-Go-58.x` APK from the `expo/expo-go-releases` GitHub releases; 58.0.5 is the newest APK there). Each new SDK, about every three months, will repeat this for an iPhone in Expo Go, until the development build of step 35 exists.
- **React Native 0.87 and later type refs and lists strictly** (found in the SDK 58 upgrade, 2026-10-24; the types are in `react-native/types_generated`). `Text`, `TextInput` and `ScrollView` are function components now, so `useRef<Text>`, `useRef<TextInput>` and `useRef<ScrollView>` no longer type the instance: use the exported `TextInstance`, `TextInputInstance` and `ScrollViewInstance` (and `RefObject<TextInputInstance | null>` for a prop). `useRef<FlatList<T>>` is unchanged. `ListHeaderComponent` and `ListEmptyComponent` no longer accept `null`: pass `element ?? undefined`. SDK 58 also removes `InteractionManager`, the `Touchable` root export, the `NativeMethods` types, the `Modal` `animated` prop and deep imports from `react-native/Libraries`: none is used in `src`, and none may be added.
- **NativeWind's Metro resolver redirects every `react-native` import, node_modules included, to its own wrapper module** (react-native-css 3.1.0-rc.0, `dist/commonjs/metro/resolver.js`: `nativeResolver` skips only its own files and `react-native/index.js`; the wrapper is `dist/module/components/index.cjs`, a written-out list of getters). The wrapper forwards every React Native export but `AssetRegistry` (checked on the iPhone on 2026-10-24: 97 keys on `react-native`, exactly one missing from the wrapper), and expo-asset 58.0.16 and later read `AssetRegistry` from `react-native` (its changelog, #51302; expo-asset 57 took a deep import), so on SDK 58 every `Asset.fromModule` threw `Cannot read property 'getAssetByID' of undefined`, `useFonts` rejected, the splash gate opened on the error and every text drew in the system font, while images were untouched (they go through React Native's `resolveAssetSource`). `metro.config.js` therefore wraps the resolver that `withNativewind` installs: a `react-native` request whose `originModulePath` is inside `node_modules/expo-asset/` goes to `context.resolveRequest` (Expo's chain, the real `react-native`) and everything else to NativeWind, so app code keeps the wrapper and its `className` support. Remove the bypass when react-native-css forwards `AssetRegistry`. A `metro.config.js` change needs a dev server restart (`npx expo start -c`). The failure was silent because the gate in `App.tsx` opens on a font error by design (a font that will not load must not hold the app); since this fix it logs `[fonts] <message>` in development.
- **Expo Go on Android scopes permissions per project** (found in step 10 on the emulator, Expo Go 57.0.9). `getRecordingPermissionsAsync` keeps answering "not granted" (our `undetermined`) after the microphone is turned on in Android Settings or with `adb shell pm grant`, until Expo Go's own confirmation ("You've already granted permission to another Expo experience. Allow gracehush to also use it?") has been answered through a request. So in Expo Go the "turn it on in Settings and come back" re-check cannot be seen to succeed after an OS-level grant. The AppState re-check and the state change (card gone, orb back, no restart) were verified with a temporary relaxed condition (reverted); a development build reports the OS answer directly. After `adb shell pm revoke` Expo Go's process is killed, and the next orb tap goes straight to the denied card (Android treats the request as permanently denied).
- **`flex-none` does not reset `flexBasis` in react-native-css, and `basis-auto` compiles to nothing** (NativeWind 5.0.0-rc.0, read from the compiled bundle in step 10): `flex-none` becomes `flexGrow: 0, flexShrink: 0` only, so the `flexBasis: "0%"` that a `flex-1` component class sets survives, and there is no class that resets it. The compact `SegmentedControl` item (`segmented__item` plus `min-h-0 flex-none px-5 py-1`) still sizes to its content on the Android emulator (26dp visual height, 29.8 at font scale 1.3); why the 0% does not collapse it was not established, and iOS was not checked. Check a `flex-1` class overridden by `flex-none` on a device before relying on it.
- **An iOS `fullScreenModal` is `UIModalPresentationFullScreen`** (react-native-screens `RNSScreen.mm`), which UIKit never dismisses with a swipe; only `modal`, `pageSheet` and `formSheet` can be. NewEntry is a `fullScreenModal`, so only X, Cancel and Android back need the leave guard there.
- **A `View` with no visible styling is flattened away on Android, and when it later gains a background its re-created native view has lost its `borderRadius`** (React Native 0.86 Fabric, Expo Go 57.0.9, found in step 11 on the emulator and not seen on iOS, which was not checked). `SegmentedControl`'s items are transparent until selected: the item selected at mount is a pill, but every item selected afterwards was a square white or plum block that could poke out of the rounded track (the Reflection tabs and, since step 10, New Entry's Speak/Write toggle). Tested on the Reflection tabs, one workaround per tab: `collapsable={false}` and `overflow: "hidden"` on the item `View` each keep the pill round; an inline `borderRadius: 22` on the item, an explicit transparent background on the unselected items, and removing the pressed opacity from the `Pressable` do not. Filter chips are not affected because they always have a background. A control whose background or border appears on a state change needs `collapsable={false}` on that `View`. **Fixed for `SegmentedControl` in the step 11 follow-up (2026-10-20)**, with the user's approval (the step 11 prompt had forbidden touching it): its item `View` carries `collapsable={false}`, and the selected pill is round after every change (checked on the emulator on the Reflection tabs and on New Entry's toggle, in both directions, with the item bounds unchanged). Whether iOS ever had the bug was not checked.
- **`useReduceMotion` is one app-wide answer, asked for when its file loads** (step 11 follow-up, 2026-10-20, with the user's approval). Before, each component asked the OS after it mounted and started at `false`, so the answer came 40 to 500 ms late (measured in step 11: `FadeIn` logged `reduceMotion=false` and then `true` 43, 65, 209 and 486 ms apart on the emulator), and a component that animates on mount started its animation under Reduce Motion and stopped it a moment later: a 150 ms fade showed partway, and `Skeleton` and `usePulse` started briefly too. Now the file asks once at import and follows `reduceMotionChanged`, and the hook reads that answer with `useSyncExternalStore`, so a component sees the real value on its first render (the signature did not change, so no consumer was edited, and the OS is asked once for the whole app instead of once per mounted component). Checked on the emulator with temporary logs (reverted): after a cold start with Reduce Motion on, the OS answered 1.5 s after the file loaded and the first component subscribed 11 s after it, already seeing `true`; every new `FadeIn` logged `true` and never `false` first; turning the setting off and on again reached mounted components live; with animations on the fade ran to completion. A component that mounted before the OS answered would still start at `false`, which the splash gate (fonts, Clerk, the onboarding store) makes unlikely at start-up.
- **The app is light only, so `userInterfaceStyle` is `light`** (step 11 follow-up, 2026-10-20; found on the user's iPhone: with `automatic` and the phone in dark mode, the keyboard was dark under the cream UI, and switches and system alerts would be too). `app.json` says `"light"` (the merged config reads it), and the three text inputs also pass `keyboardAppearance="light"` (iOS only, and it works in Expo Go whatever the manifest does). The status bar is fixed `dark`. A night theme is a feature for after V1 ("Sweet Sleep" is a bedtime devotional). This resolves the step 08 open item about `userInterfaceStyle`. Not verified on the iPhone: whether Expo Go applies the project's `userInterfaceStyle`.
- **A layout that must follow the keyboard** (`WriteSection`, step 11 follow-up): on iOS `Keyboard` fires `keyboardWillShow` and `keyboardWillHide` before the keyboard moves, and `LayoutAnimation.configureNext({ duration, update: { duration, type: "keyboard" } })` with the event's duration moves the next layout change on the keyboard's own curve, as `KeyboardAvoidingView` does (read in React Native 0.86: it configures `LayoutAnimation` on iOS only, with that type). Android has only the `Did` events, so there the layout follows once the keyboard has moved, and `LayoutAnimation` is not used. `Keyboard.isVisible()` seeds the state when the view mounts with the keyboard already up, and Reduce Motion skips the animation. Verified on the Android emulator only (the heading collapses and returns and the card stays whole); the iOS animation was not verified.
- **A drag on a `View` inside a vertical `ScrollView` needs more than responder props on Android** (found in step 12 on the emulator, with the Now Playing scrubber and `ProgressScrubber`). The scrubber is a 44dp-tall `View` with `onStartShouldSetResponder`, `onMoveShouldSetResponder`, `onResponderGrant`, `onResponderMove`, `onResponderRelease` and `onResponderTerminate` (not a `PanResponder`, which is created once and holds stale callbacks), `onResponderTerminationRequest` returning false, and a 16dp thumb plus the 8dp track as `pointerEvents="none"` children, so `locationX` is relative to the 44dp view. `onResponderTerminationRequest` does not stop Android's `ScrollView`: once the finger drifts about 8dp vertically (the touch slop) the `ScrollView` takes the touch, React Native sends `onResponderTerminate` and the page scrolls. Measured at 320dp wide with font scale 1.3, a drag that drifted 53px (24dp) upward froze the thumb at 0:36 while the finger went on to about 0:48, and the page scrolled 44dp. So `NowPlayingScreen` sets `scrollEnabled={false}` on its `ScrollView` while a finger is on either scrubber (`onSeekStart` sets it, `onSeekEnd` clears it, and `onResponderTerminate` also calls `onSeekEnd`, so the state always ends): with it, a drag that drifted 406px (147dp) upward kept following the finger and the page did not move. A release or a terminate seeks to the last value, so a drag makes one `seekTo` (counted with a temporary log: one per tap, one per drag). The value follows the finger beyond the ends and clamps there (0:00 and 1:15 both held). Measured at 390dp: the view 350.3×43.7, the track 7.9 tall 18.1 from the top, the thumb 15.9×16.3 centered on the track and overhanging it by 8dp at each end (inside the 20dp screen padding), the elapsed and total labels 6.2dp under the track.
- **`opacity` on a group whose children overlap draws each child with the alpha on its own on Android** (React Native 0.86, found in step 12): the dimmed volume row (`opacity-40` while the ambient sound is Off) let the fill's end show through the thumb as a darker half-disc. `needsOffscreenAlphaCompositing` on the dimmed `View` composites the group first and fixes it (an Android-only prop, ignored on iOS). Use it on any dimmed group whose children overlap.
- **An Android `modal` stack screen without `animation` does not slide up** (react-native-screens 4.26, read from `FragmentTransactionKt.kt` and the resource `rns_default_enter_in`, then recorded on the API 37 emulator): on API 33+ the default is a fade with a 10% slide in from the right. `animation: "slide_from_bottom"` slides it up like a sheet (the resource is `rns_slide_in_from_bottom`, and the screen below stays still). Both were recorded with `screenrecord` and read frame by frame (see "Verifying on this machine"). iOS swipes a `modal` down by itself, so the option only matters on Android there; it was not verified on iOS.
- **`AccessibilityInfo.setAccessibilityFocus` is deprecated** (React Native 0.86.3, `AccessibilityInfo.js`: "Use `sendAccessibilityEvent` with eventType `focus` instead"). Use `AccessibilityInfo.sendAccessibilityEvent(ref.current, "focus")` with the host ref of a `useRef<Text>` (NativeWind's wrapper forwards the ref: it was set, and the call moved TalkBack's focus), and on a pushed screen call it after a delay, because the transition is still running at mount: `CrisisSupportPanel` waits 500 ms. On the Android emulator with TalkBack (step 13) the headline took focus in 3 of 3 opens at 500 ms made with TalkBack already running (and in one open each at 1,000, 1,500 and 3,000 ms, in a reverted Android-only experiment), while the very first open right after TalkBack was switched on ended on "Go back": TalkBack's own focus on the first element came later, while its text-to-speech engine was still starting. A re-run of the effect on a stable screen (Fast Refresh) always moved the focus onto the headline. No retry was added. Not verified on iOS.
- **`Linking.canOpenURL` is not used for `tel:` and `sms:`** (without declared query schemes it answers false for links that would open). `Linking.openURL` inside `try/catch` is, and its rejection means no app can open the link. The SMS prefill separator is `?` on Android and `&` on iOS (`sms:741741?body=HOME`): on Android, Google Messages opened a thread to 741741 with "HOME" in the compose box and sent nothing, `sms:988` opened an empty thread, and `tel:` opened the Google Dialer with the number typed and no call placed (iOS not verified). With the dialer and Contacts disabled, `Linking.openURL("tel:988")` rejects and the fallback sheet shows. `expo-web-browser` 57.0.3 on Android opens a Custom Tab through a proxy activity whose return path relaunches `getLaunchIntentForPackage` (`BrowserProxyActivity.returnToMainApp`), so in Expo Go closing the tab shows Expo Go's own launcher and not the app (the app and the screen it was on are one `am start` away); in a development or release build the launch intent is the app's own activity (not verified there). The library's defaults were kept.
- **A `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")` is UTC midnight**, which prints the day before in the Americas and Samoa: "2026-10-20" printed "October 19, 2026" in America/Los_Angeles, America/New_York and Pacific/Pago_Pago (step 13 ran nine zones). A calendar date is parsed with `formatCalendarDate` (`utils/format.ts`), which builds a local date from its parts.
- **`accessibilityHint` is the node's tooltip text on Android** (React Native 0.86.3, `ReactAccessibilityDelegate.kt`: `info.tooltipText = accessibilityHint`), so `uiautomator dump` does not show it (its `hint` attribute stays empty) and the label is not extended. How TalkBack voices it was not heard.
- **A development `console.warn` shows as a LogBox toast** at the bottom of the screen ("[crisis] a link could not be opened") and covers whatever is under it ("More ways to get help" sat under it); a tap on its body opens the full "Console Warning". Dismiss it with its x before tapping near the bottom. Release builds have no toast.
- **A `Text` in Fraunces italic that is sized by its own measure loses its last words on Android** (React Native 0.86 Fabric, Expo Go 57.0.9, found in step 14 on the emulator at 390dp and 320dp): the scripture reference "Proverbs 17:17" in a row's `Text` of natural width showed only "Proverbs", while its node reported the whole string and a one-line height (the second line is wrapped and clipped). It happened in both a bare `Text` that is a child of a `flex-wrap` row and a `Text` in a shrinkable group; a five-row experiment with the same string showed Fraunces regular and DM Sans fine in the same structures, and the italic fine in a plain row group, so it is not about the structure alone. A `Text` whose width comes from the layout (`flex-1`) is not affected: `PrayerCard`'s reference is a group with `min-w-[120px] flex-1` around a `flex-1` text, and the Journal card's is `flex-1` too. Give any italic text a width from the layout, never from its own measure. Not checked on iOS.
- **`rounded` (no size) compiles to 8** (NativeWind 5.0.0-rc.0 with this `@theme`'s `--radius: 8px`, read from the compiled bundle in step 14), so the Answered note box uses it for the 8dp radius.
- **`SegmentedControl` stacks by measuring its labels** (step 14, `onTextLayout`, kept per window width and font scale): at 320dp with normal text the three Reflection tabs stack, because each tab is 82dp wide and "Closing Prayer" is 89.8dp (before step 14 it wrapped to "Closing" and "Prayer" inside its tab, so the prompt's expectation that three tabs fit at 320dp and normal text was wrong). At 390dp and normal text they are three in a row (102.6×44.1 each in a 52.0 track), at 360dp and 320dp with font scale 1.3 they are three full-width 44dp rows, and the two Prayers tabs stay a row at 360dp ×1.3 (156dp tabs, 113dp of content) and at 320dp ×1.3 and stack at 320dp ×2.0 (280×48 each). A stacked track is `rounded-hero` (24): a full pill radius on a 100 to 140dp tall track pushes the selected pill's corners outside it. One frame of the row shows before the stack at a large size (the log showed `stacked=false` and then `stacked=true` for the same label).
- **Android `SymbolView` paints its glyph one async step after it mounts** (expo-symbols 57.0.3, read in `build/SymbolView.js` in step 16): every instance renders an empty box of its size first, then calls `loadAsync(...)` and sets `loaded` when the promise resolves, so the first frame of any screen has empty icon boxes whatever the font cache holds. On the loaded emulator the Paywall's X glyph appeared 0.59s after "Restore purchases" in a screen recording (frames at 2.23s and 2.82s, read with `ffprobe -show_entries frame=pts_time`), while the button was in the accessibility tree and enabled from the first frame. The app cannot skip that first render (a hand-drawn X would break the rule that every icon goes through `Icon`), so "the X is there from the first frame" is true of the button and not of its glyph. iOS draws a native symbol view and was not checked.
- **A `flex-1` column beside a natural-width column wraps first** (React Native 0.86, found in step 16 on the plan cards): `flex-1` gives the column a basis of 0, so Yoga never shrinks the price beside it and the title wraps instead. "Monthly Access" (151dp in 20sp Fraunces Medium) broke onto two lines at 360dp beside a one-line price ("$9.99 / month", 101dp), growing the card from 85 to 113dp; stacking the price over its period (49dp wide, as the HTML draws it) fixed it. When a title must stay on one line, narrow the other column.
- **A hot update that applies half an edit leaves Metro with a stale module graph** (step 16): an edit that used `Platform` before its import was added logged `ReferenceError: Property 'Platform' doesn't exist`, and afterwards one module showed the new code while two others kept the old one. Add the import first and the use second, and after any multi-file temporary experiment run `am force-stop host.exp.exponent` and relaunch with `am start -a android.intent.action.VIEW -d exp://127.0.0.1:8082 host.exp.exponent` (about 20s) instead of trusting Fast Refresh.
- **`accessibilityRole="radiogroup"` is not a node in the Android accessibility tree** (step 16, `uiautomator dump`): the container is not focusable, so only the options appear, as `android.widget.RadioButton` nodes with `checked` and `disabled`. TalkBack's Tab order on the Paywall was Close, Restore purchases, the two plans, the button and the two links. What TalkBack says for the group, and VoiceOver's "radio button, checked", were not heard.
- **A wrapped `Text` breaks greedily on Android unless it is told to balance, and iOS has no balanced mode** (React Native 0.86.3, step 16 follow-up, 2026-10-22). `textBreakStrategy` (Android API 23+, default `highQuality`) takes `'balanced'`. `lineBreakStrategyIOS` takes `'none'`, `'standard'`, `'hangul-word'` or `'push-out'` and maps to `NSLineBreakStrategy` on the paragraph style; Apple's documentation says push-out avoids an orphan word on a paragraph's last line, which is not the same as balancing the lines, and it was not run on an iPhone here. `PrimaryButton`'s label sets both. On the API 37 emulator at 320dp wide and font scale 1.3 the paid label "Subscribe for $59.99/year" now breaks as "Subscribe for" over "$59.99/year"; before, it broke after the slash and left "year" alone. The Text node is the same 214.2×48.0dp box either way: React Native sizes the node to the available width and not to the longest line, and `text-center` centers the lines inside it. At 360dp and font scale 1.3 the label is one line (244.7dp of text in a 246dp slot), so a longer price wraps there. A word joiner (U+2060) before the period was rejected, because an invisible character inside an accessibility label cannot be checked on iOS here, and so was "per year" for "/year": it is 25.5dp wider (215.1 against 189.6dp, from the DM Sans file) and would wrap labels that fit today.
- **supabase-js 2.118 and postgrest-js 2.118** (read from the packages and run in the step 17 review, 2026-10-22). With the `accessToken` option `supabase.auth` throws, because Clerk owns the session. The client calls the token callback when it is created and again before every request, and when the callback returns `null` it sends its own API key as the bearer token, so the app creates the client on first use (`getSupabase()` is `null` while signed out) and the callback throws `NoSessionError` instead of returning `null`. postgrest-js retries a failed read 3 times, 1, 2 and 4 seconds apart: one offline read took **7.0 seconds and 4 attempts** (a write is never retried), so the client is created with `db: { retry: false }` (0.0 seconds, 1 attempt). A rejected fetch, a failed Clerk token request included, comes back as `status` 0 with the error's name and message; an HTTP failure comes back as its status and `code` (`PGRST301` to `PGRST303` for a refused token, `42501` for row level security or a missing grant). A hand-written `Database` type must use `type` aliases for Row, Insert and Update: with an `interface` the table collapses to `never` (tsc: "not assignable to parameter of type 'never'"). Publishable keys (`sb_publishable_…`) go in the `apikey` header only, never as a bearer token. Supabase's Expo quickstart installs `react-native-url-polyfill`, which SDK 57 does not need: its runtime installs `URL` and `URLSearchParams` (`node_modules/expo/src/winter/runtime.native.ts`). The packages have a `react-native` export condition and require no Node built-in module. `time` columns arrive as `"HH:mm:ss"`. `process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_*` is read by its full name (`process.env[name]` is empty in the app). Postgres ignores a column-level REVOKE while a table-wide GRANT exists, so the profile table revokes everything and grants back only the columns the app may write (`supabase/schema.sql`). Read in `SupabaseClient.ts` and `lib/fetch.ts` during step 17: the client calls the token callback once when it is created, and if that rejects (for example Clerk cannot mint a token offline) it only logs `console.warn('Failed to set initial Realtime auth token:', e)`, which is a development LogBox toast and nothing else (nothing is sent, nothing breaks). `omitApiKeyAsBearer` is set only on the fetch that Edge Functions use (`functionsFetch`): the REST, storage and realtime fetch still falls back to the API key as the bearer token when the callback returns `null`, which is why this app's callback throws `NoSessionError` and never returns `null`.

---

## Development Philosophy

Build feature by feature.

For every feature:

1. Understand the user request.
2. Check this file before coding.
3. Keep the implementation simple.
4. Avoid overengineering.
5. Prefer readable code to clever code.
6. Build the smallest useful version first.
7. Refactor only when repetition or complexity appears.
8. Never trade user privacy, scripture accuracy, or crisis safety for speed.

---

## Decision Making & Clarifications

If something is unclear or could be improved:

- Proactively suggest better approaches.
- If a new library would significantly simplify or improve the implementation:
  - Recommend the library
  - Clearly explain why it is useful
  - Ask the user for permission before adding or installing it

Example:

> "The ambient soundscape needs a second audio channel that plays alongside track-player. `expo-audio` can loop it cleanly. Do you want me to add it?"

Do not install or use new libraries without user approval. That includes packages already in `package.json` that the stack above does not mention (for example `@expo/ui`): name the package and the reason before first use. Approved so far: the Expo UI date-time picker for iOS (step 03), and in step 05 `@clerk/expo`, `@clerk/expo-google-signin`, `expo-secure-store`, `expo-apple-authentication`, `expo-crypto` and `expo-constants` (to detect Expo Go), then `expo-auth-session` (the Expo Go Google fallback), and in step 07 `zustand`, `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage`, `expo-notifications`, `expo-audio` and its required peer `expo-asset`, and in step 08 `@react-navigation/bottom-tabs`, and in the step 11 follow-up `expo-haptics`, and in step 13 `expo-web-browser` (already installed, named by the step prompt: the in-app browser for the crisis web links), and in step 17 `@supabase/supabase-js` (named by the step prompt; it is the only package that step added), and in step 19 `jose` 6.2.13 (and `@supabase/supabase-js` 2.118.0 again) for the Edge Function only: Deno imports pinned in `supabase/functions/process-entry/deno.json`, named by the step prompt, with the app's `package.json` and lockfile untouched. Deno 2.9.6 and the Supabase CLI 2.121.0 run through `npx --yes` and install nothing.

Always ask before:

- installing, upgrading, or removing any dependency (including `expo-router`)
- creating or changing a database migration
- changing RLS or storage policies
- deploying an Edge Function
- changing the auth integration (JWT template vs. Third-Party Auth)
- changing RevenueCat products, entitlements, or pricing copy

---

## Architecture Guidelines

Use this structure unless there is a strong reason to change it:

```txt
gracehush/
├── App.tsx                     # App = ClerkProvider (SecureStore tokenCache). AppShell = splash gate (fonts, Clerk isLoaded and the hydration of the onboarding and profile prefs stores), SafeAreaProvider, SupabaseAuthBridge (renders nothing), NavigationContainer, StatusBar. App stops with a clear message when a Supabase value in `.env.local` is wrong, and, in a release build only, when one is missing. Providers only, no screen UI
├── index.ts                    # registerRootComponent(App)
├── app.json                    # Base config: bundle ID / package, background audio modes, plugins, permissions copy
├── app.config.ts               # Extends app.json: puts the Google client IDs and iOS URL scheme from process.env into expo.extra
├── .env                        # Non-secret tooling flag EXPO_ROUTER_DISABLE_RN_NAVIGATION_CHECK=1 (keep committed; SDK 58's CLI no longer reads it). Never put real keys here
├── .env.local                  # Real EXPO_PUBLIC_* keys (Clerk, Google client IDs, and from step 17 the Supabase URL and publishable key), added in step 05. Gitignored by `.env*.local`
├── .env.example                # The same key names with empty values. Committed
├── global.css                  # @theme tokens, type scale, BEM utilities (NativeWind v5, no tailwind.config.js)
├── assets/
│   ├── fonts/
│   ├── images/
│   └── audio/                  # Ambient loops (soft-rain.mp3, gentle-piano.mp3 …)
├── docs/
│   ├── legal-brief.md          # Facts for the Privacy Policy and the Terms of Use, for a lawyer: not legal text (2026-10-22). The questions in it are the owner's
│   ├── privacy-policy.md       # DRAFT of the page behind `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL`, for a lawyer to review and the owner to host: every unconfirmed fact is a TODO, it is not shipped in the app, and the DRAFT block at its top is deleted before it is published
│   └── terms-of-use.md         # DRAFT of the page behind `TERMS_URL`, the same
├── src/
```
