- Every interactive element has an `accessibilityRole` and a label that includes its state, for example "Morning reminder, 8:00 AM, change time". Step counters read "Step 1 of 3".
- The screen holds up at 320dp wide and at font scale 1.3: content scrolls, footers stay pinned, nothing is clipped (icons included), and every touch target is at least 44.
- React Native's default `flexShrink` is 0, so text beside a chip, badge, pill, icon or button must be `flex-1` or `shrink`, or it pushes that element out. A row that cannot fit on one line at 320dp or font scale 1.3 wraps (`flex-wrap`, with a `minWidth` on the text block that is the longest word at the current `fontScale`) instead of squeezing a word into a mid-word break. Yoga breaks lines using the flex basis clamped by min/max size, so `flex-1` plus `minWidth` does trigger the wrap (read in the step 09 review). To know whether a row fits, measure its strings with the real font files (the advance widths in the `.ttf`), for example the Journal greeting row in step 09: its chip is 166dp wide and "afternoon," at 28dp is 136dp. Two more rules from step 15 (Profile, at 320dp and font scale 1.3): a slider that shares a row with fixed icons and a readout gets `min-w-[96px] flex-1` on its wrapper and the row wraps, so the readout drops under it instead of squeezing the track (it was 73dp wide and is 150dp now); and a clock time keeps its digits and its AM or PM on one line with a non-breaking space (`copy.profile.reminders.summary`: "Afternoon 3:00 / PM" otherwise).

---

## Date & Time Pickers

Decided in step 03 and reused by every later picker (Profile reminders, the Prayers answered date).

- **iOS:** a bottom sheet (React Native `Modal`, 24 top radius) holding `{ DateTimePicker }` from `@expo/ui/community/datetime-picker` with `display="spinner"` and `themeVariant="light"`, plus a primary `Done` button. Use the named export, because the default export trips `import/no-named-as-default`. Changes apply live through `onValueChange`, so the chip behind the sheet updates as the wheel settles.
- **Android:** the native dialog through the imperative `DateTimePickerAndroid.open({ value, mode, is24Hour: false, onValueChange })` from `@react-native-community/datetimepicker`.
- Never render the community picker as a component on either platform: on iOS its change events do not arrive, and on Android it re-opens the dialog whenever `value` changes.
- Keep each time as one `"HH:mm"` 24-hour string in state, derive the picker's `Date` from it (a fixed date, only the time of day matters), and display it as `h:mm AM/PM`.
- **A date** (the Prayers answered day, step 14) is kept as one `"YYYY-MM-DD"` string in local time (`utils/prayerDates.ts`: `toCalendarDate`, `parseCalendarDate`), never built with `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")`, and shown with `formatCalendarDate`. Android: `DateTimePickerAndroid.open({ mode: "date", minimumDate, maximumDate, onValueChange })`. iOS: **inside a `BottomSheet` the spinner is shown inline under its row** (the Expo UI `DateTimePicker`, `display="spinner"`, `themeVariant="light"`, `minimumDate`, `maximumDate`) and not in a second `Modal`, because a Modal opened from inside the sheet's Modal is the fragile case on iOS (decided in the step 14 review; built in step 14 and verified on the Android emulator, where the dialog greys out the days before the prayer was created and after today; the iOS inline spinner inside the sheet is not verified).
- **The reminder times on Profile** (step 15) are edited through the same `ReminderTimePicker` as onboarding's Rhythm step (extracted in step 15: the iOS `Modal` sheet with the Expo UI spinner and `Done`, and the imperative Android dialog). The sheet is opened by the screen itself, never from inside a `BottomSheet` (the reason the Prayers date row has an inline spinner instead).
- Never build a custom wheel.

---

## Bottom Sheets

Decided in step 04. Every sheet (the email sheet, Mark Answered, Filter) is a `BottomSheet`: build new ones on it instead of a new `Modal`. The Rhythm time sheet keeps its own `Modal` because its picker is verified on the iPhone, and only shares the blush background.

- **Look:** blush surface, `radii.sheet` top corners, a 1px petal top border, `shadows.sheet`, a centered 48×6 petal handle, padding 24 plus the bottom safe-area inset. The scrim is `ink` at 40%, closes the sheet, and is labelled "Close" (`copy.common.close`). Android back closes it too.
- **Behavior:** React Native `Modal` (`transparent`, `slide`) with `statusBarTranslucent` and `navigationBarTranslucent`, so the scrim covers the system bars on Android. `KeyboardAvoidingView` uses `padding` on both platforms (checked on the Android emulator, which is edge-to-edge). The sheet reserves the top safe-area inset, so it never climbs under the status bar. Children sit in a `ScrollView` with `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"`, so content taller than the space above the keyboard scrolls.
- **Inputs inside a sheet:** do not use `autoFocus`. On Android it focuses the field before the dialog window has focus, so the keyboard stays closed, and React Native then ignores a second `focus()` on that field. Call `ref.current?.focus()` about 300 ms after the sheet opens or its step changes (see `EmailSheet`).
- **Flows inside a sheet:** closing resets everything (step, typed values, timers), and a request that finishes after the sheet closed is ignored.
- **Code boxes:** one TextInput laid over the boxes at `opacity: 0.01`. Never 0, because Android hides a fully transparent view from screen readers, and never a transparent text color, because Android ignores it.

---

## Copy & Tone Rules

- Gentle, validating, and never preachy. Validate feelings before offering scripture.
- No toxic positivity ("Just trust God and be happy!").
- No guilt or shame around streaks, missed days, or upgrading. Use "5 days of reflection," never "You broke your streak."
- No pressure tactics on the Paywall: no fake countdowns, no fake scarcity.
- All user-facing strings live in `src/constants/copy.ts`, grouped by screen (`copy.onboarding`, …), including accessibility labels built by functions (for example `changeTime(slot, time)`). No prose inline in components. A `// TEMP` placeholder is the only exception, together with dev-only UI (the `DevToolsPanel`, step 08), which is never shown to users and keeps its strings inline.
- Strings that render uppercase (eyebrows, taglines, counters) are stored in sentence case. The `uppercase` style does the casing.
- Every string uses typographic punctuation: curly apostrophes (’) and curly double quotes, never the straight ones (decided in the step 11 follow-up, 2026-10-20, from the serif headlines "you’re" and "that’s" on the user's iPhone: 39 straight apostrophes in `copy.ts` and 18 in the mock data became ’). Text the app does not write is left as it comes: the user's own words, and scripture, which keeps the BSB's own marks (`quoteVerse`). AI-written text should arrive with curly apostrophes as well, so step 32's Gemini prompt asks for them; the client does not rewrite it.
- Copy says only what the app does today, or what a named step will make true. Do not promise a notification, a save or a sync that no step builds. The design's "We'll notify you when it's ready" became "Your reflection will be waiting in your Journal" in step 10, because no step sends a "ready" notification. "Saved locally" and "Your words are safe" are mock until steps 24 and 25 make them true, so they carry `// TODO(24)` markers.

---

## Authentication Rules (Clerk ↔ Supabase)

Use Clerk for authentication. Do not build home-grown auth (our own passwords, tokens, or sessions).

- Sign-in methods: Apple, Google, and email through Clerk. Email is passwordless: a 6-digit one-time code, with no password field anywhere.
- One Sign In screen serves new and returning users. There is no separate Sign Up screen. Email uses Clerk's sign-in-or-up custom flow (step 05): `signIn.create({ identifier, signUpIfMissing: true })` sends a code whether or not the account exists, and a new user is transferred to sign-up (`signUp.create({ transfer: true })`) only after the code is verified. Never try sign-in and fall back to sign-up on `form_identifier_not_found`: it reveals which emails have accounts and fails when strict user enumeration protection is on.
- Sign in with Apple is iOS-only (native flow), so the Apple button is hidden on Android. Google is native on both platforms in a development build. Neither native flow works in Expo Go (Clerk's Apple and Google guides), so in Expo Go only, `continueWithGoogle` signs in through Clerk's browser flow (`useSSO` with `oauth_google`, which uses Clerk's own Google credentials on a development instance) and `continueWithApple` returns `NOT_SUPPORTED`. Do not add a browser-based Apple flow.
- `ClerkProvider` wraps the app in `App.tsx` with the SecureStore `tokenCache` from `@clerk/expo/token-cache`. Never cache a token yourself. Real keys live in `.env.local`, never in `.env`.
- The UI is ours, driven by Clerk's custom-flow hooks. Do not use Clerk's prebuilt components or hosted pages.
- The Supabase client is `getSupabase()` in `src/services/supabaseClient.ts` (step 17): one client for the app, created on first use, whose `accessToken` option reads Clerk's `getToken()` through the session that `SupabaseAuthBridge` (`src/components/SupabaseAuthBridge.tsx`) sets after sign-in. It is `null` while nobody is signed in or the Supabase keys are missing, so no request for user data can leave anonymously. Never call `supabase.auth.*` (Clerk owns the session) and never send a column the app has no grant on (the typed `Database` blocks it at compile time).
- The integration is **Supabase Third-Party Auth with Clerk** (decided in the step 17 review): `getToken()` with no template, and the Clerk dashboard's Supabase integration adds `role: "authenticated"`. There is no JWT template and no shared JWT secret. Ask before changing it.
- RLS uses `requesting_user_id()` (the JWT `sub` claim, the Clerk id as text: `auth.uid()` is never used). Every user-owned table must have RLS enabled with both `USING` and `WITH CHECK`. The app writes only the columns it is granted: `supabase/schema.sql` revokes everything from `anon` and `authenticated` and grants back per column, and `subscription_tier` is never one of them (only the service role changes it).
- On sign-out: clear Zustand stores, wipe the local SQLite cache, cancel Gracehush notifications, and reset track-player. Exception: the onboarding store (`gracehush.onboarding`, device-level) is kept, so a signed-out user never sees Welcome again. A second exception from step 17: the profile prefs store (`gracehush.profilePrefs`) keeps each account's offline copy of its sign-up date, its plan and its three settings (Daily Affirmations, ambient sound and volume), as last read from `users_profile`, plus the setting edits not yet sent (`unsent`), keyed by Clerk user id (a switch that turned itself back on after a sign-out would be worse than keeping it; an unsent setting waits for its own account to sign in again); account deletion removes that account's entry, and `signOutCleanup()` cancels the profile sync debounce and drops only a reminder edit that never reached the server (the reminder times are device-wide), so it can never be sent to the next account that signs in on the phone. `signOutCleanup()` in `src/services/account.ts` (step 15) is the one place this runs: Profile's Sign Out, the delete-account flow and the dev panel's two sign-out buttons call it before Clerk's `signOut()`, and each later step adds its own line to it (today it resets the player store, and the other steps are `TODO` lines).

---

## Backend / Edge Function Rules

Use Supabase Edge Functions for:

- all AI calls (Gemini)
- Bible text hydration (HelloAO)
- TTS generation
- writes that need the service role
- subscription status sync (RevenueCat webhook)

Never expose secrets in the mobile app.

**Client-safe env vars (EXPO*PUBLIC* only):** Supabase URL (`EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`) and publishable key (`EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `sb_publishable_…`; the secret and service_role keys never), Clerk publishable key, Google OAuth client IDs and iOS URL scheme, RevenueCat public SDK keys.
**Server-only secrets (Edge Function env):** `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_MODEL`, `GEMINI_SCREENING_MODEL` (optional, step 21: the crisis screening's own model, `GEMINI_MODEL` when empty), `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL` (optional, step 21 follow-up: every second attempt of the screening and of the reflection goes to it; it screens entries too, so it must pass the screening evaluation first), `GEMINI_THINKING_LEVEL` (optional), `CLERK_ISSUER`, `CLERK_AUTHORIZED_PARTIES` (optional), `GOOGLE_TTS_CREDENTIALS_B64` (optional, step 22: the Text-to-Speech service account's key file as one line of base64; with it and `TTS_VOICE_NAME` both unset, speech is off), `TTS_VOICE_NAME` (optional, step 22: a Chirp 3 HD voice such as `en-US-Chirp3-HD-<Name>`), `TTS_SPEAKING_RATE` (optional, step 22: 0.25 to 2.0) and, from later steps, `REVENUECAT_WEBHOOK_SECRET`. The platform provides `SUPABASE_URL` and the secret key (`SUPABASE_SECRET_KEYS`, or the older `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`): never set by hand, and never in the app.

### Gemini connection and credit (active since 2026-10-23)

The Gemini credit is paid and active, so no step has any billing to set up. Every step that calls a model (25, 28, 29, 31, 32 and any later one) uses it as written here.

- **Status.** The project's Gemini key is on the paid tier (AI Studio shows Tier 1, Prepay plan) with **$20 of credits** bought on 2026-10-23 and **auto-reload off**. Google's paid-services terms apply (see the Gemini API terms note), so a real person's prayer may be sent to it, through the Edge Function only (step 25 onwards). Verified on 2026-10-23: calls served on three models; the timings and the screening evaluation are in the Google billing log entry.
- **The key.** One secret, `GEMINI_API_KEY` (an `AQ.` auth key), server-side only: in `supabase/functions/.env.local` (git ignores it) and in the hosted project's secrets. It never goes in the app, in an `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` variable, a committed file, a log, a test or a chat, and nobody prints it. The credit belongs to the billing account, so any key of the project spends it. The user sets the hosted secrets and deploys (`npx --yes supabase@2.121.0 secrets set --env-file supabase/functions/.env.local --project-ref <project-ref>`, then `functions deploy <name> --project-ref <project-ref> --use-api`); the implementer never holds a management token. `secrets set --env-file` sends every line of the file, so no secret in it may be empty.
- **The models.** `GEMINI_MODEL=gemini-3.1-flash-lite` in the local file and on the hosted project; `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL=gemini-3.5-flash-lite` on the hosted project and, since 2026-10-24, in the local file too (so that `live_check.ts` runs as production does, and `secrets set --env-file` sends the same value again); `GEMINI_SCREENING_MODEL` and `GEMINI_THINKING_LEVEL` empty (the screening uses `GEMINI_MODEL`, and thinking is `low`). No model name is written in code. Changing a model is the user's decision (step 32 compares them; the paid-tier numbers are in the log entry), and a new screening or fallback model must first pass `run_eval.ts`.
- **The only code that calls Google** is `supabase/functions/process-entry/gemini.ts` (the Interactions API, the key in the `x-goog-api-key` header, `store: false`, retries with backoff). A new function or step that needs a model imports it and the screening in `_shared/safety/`: never a second client, and never a call from the app, which only sends its Clerk session token to an Edge Function (step 25).
- **Check the connection** (made-up text only). The quickest check is `npx --yes deno@2.9.6 run --no-lock --node-modules-dir=none --config supabase/functions/process-entry/deno.json --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read --env-file=supabase/functions/.env.local supabase/functions/process-entry/live_check.ts --shape`: it sends two requests and must print `screening: HTTP 200` (1 to 3 s). `live_check.ts` without `--shape` runs the whole flow on made-up entries, and the screening evaluation is `run_eval.ts` (see Linting and Validation).
- **When a call fails.** HTTP 402 `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, "Your prepayment credits are depleted", means the balance is $0 and every key of the billing account has stopped: `gemini.ts` logs `gemini_http_402:RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, never retries it, and the entry fails with `AI_UNAVAILABLE`. Stop, say so, and tell the user to buy credits (aistudio.google.com/billing, **Buy credits**); do not loop. A 429 or a 5xx (busy) is retried by `gemini.ts` with backoff. A 400 is a bad request body, and a 401 or 403 a wrong or revoked key (the user makes a new one and updates both places).
- **What it costs.** At the prices of 2026-10-23 a crisis screening costs about $0.0003, a written reflection about $0.0013 (`gemini-3.1-flash-lite`) to $0.0026 (`gemini-3.8-flash` until 2026-12-31), a three-minute spoken entry about $0.0065 and the 30-case evaluation about $0.01. So $20 is thousands of reflections, and a loop or a large batch is the only way to spend it fast: tell the user the cost before anything that would spend more than about $1.
- **What it does not pay for.** Cloud Text-to-Speech (step 22) is paid by the Cloud Billing account (a Paid account holding the user's $30 credit; its first million characters each month are free), not by the Gemini credit. Supabase and Clerk have their own plans.
- **Ask first.** Never buy credits, change the billing account or plan, or switch auto-reload on for the user: it is their money. Before launch the user turns auto-reload on with a monthly charge limit, because an empty balance stops every key at once. Real people's text reaches Gemini only through the app and the Edge Function; checks, evaluations and tests send made-up text only. The billing account id, a card's digits and the Google Cloud project id never go in a committed file.

### `process-entry` requirements

The function did not exist before step 19 (the architecture notes spoke of a "working draft", but none was ever in this repo), so these were built in, not fixed. Each stays a requirement for every change to the function; the step that finished it is in brackets.

1. **Verify the JWT signature** (step 19, `_shared/auth.ts`). Against the public keys of the Clerk instance, RS256 only, with `iss`, `exp` and `sub` required, a `sid`, the top-level `role` equal to `authenticated`, a session that is not `pending`, and `azp` checked when `CLERK_AUTHORIZED_PARTIES` is set. The user id used anywhere comes only from this result. The app sends Clerk's normal session token: there is no template and no shared secret.
2. **Scope every service-role query** (step 19, `lifecycle.ts`). The service role bypasses RLS, so every query on `journal_entries` and `daily_affirmations` filters by the verified `userId` (a test fails on one that does not), and a `user_id` in the request body is never read.
3. **Validate input** (step 19, `validate.ts`). A POST of at most 64 KB; a lowercase UUID v4 `clientEntryId`; text of 10 to 5,000 characters; or a recording of 3 to 302 seconds, at most 12 MiB, at exactly `{userId}/raw/{clientEntryId}.m4a`; no audio in the body. The entry reaches the model inside `<journal_entry>` tags, with any such tag the person typed removed, and the instruction says everything in it is content and never instructions.
4. **Enforce structured output** (step 19, `schema.ts`, `gemini.ts`, `output.ts`). The schema asks; `output.ts` checks every value and every limit again, cleans the text (no tags, no emoji, curly apostrophes, no "Amen" in the stored prayer) and truncates nothing. At most nine model calls per request (four for the crisis screening, four for the reflection and, step 20, one for a replacement passage): a busy service is tried again after about 2, 4 and 8 s and an unusable reply once more (the vendor note on heavy demand). The screening ends 60 seconds after the request started, and every call 90 seconds after it.
5. **Run crisis screening first** (step 21, built in `_shared/safety/`; the rules are under Crisis Screening Rules). Nothing is made for an entry that was not screened. The order is: claim, read the recording, screen, then (step 22) the free-tier limit, then the reflection from the screened words, so a crisis returns before the limit and is never paywalled or counted. A crisis entry is saved as the flag and the person's words, nothing else, with no affirmations, and the reply is the crisis response. A content block is treated as a crisis and is never an error shown to the person, so there is no `CONTENT_BLOCKED` code any more.
6. **Hydrate scripture exactly** (step 20, `_shared/bible/` and `process-entry/scripture.ts`). The model returns only where a passage is: a USFM book id from the 66-id list (a schema enum built from `books.ts`, the one list), the chapter and the verses. The server checks that shape against the book table, reads the chapter from the Free Use Bible API (the Berean Standard Bible only, in the simplified format), repairs the spacing the API gets wrong (whitespace only: not one letter or mark changes), joins the verses (a line break where either neighbour is poetry, a space otherwise) and stores the text with `scripture_verified` true. A passage that does not exist (a chapter or a verse the BSB lacks, more than six verses, or more than 1,200 characters) costs one small follow-up request that sees the reflection's title and feeling and never the person's words; if that fails too the reflection is saved with no passage. A chapter that cannot be read (the API down, slow or answering with something else) is not a wrong passage: the reference is kept with no text and `scripture_verified` false, and `confirm-scripture` checks it again later. A crisis entry never reaches this code. There is no fallback verse, nothing is typed or recalled by the model, no verse is ever hardcoded, and the devotional never quotes scripture.
7. **Keep storage private** (decided and built in step 18). `journal_media` is a private bucket (25 MB, audio types only) with one folder per user. The database stores object paths (`audio_path`, `devotional_audio_path`), never URLs, and the app gets short-lived signed URLs (step 22). The app may read its own folder and upload only into `{userId}/raw/`; the Edge Function writes `{userId}/devotionals/` through the service role.
8. **The raw audio is saved before the function is called.** The app uploads it to `journal_media` first (step 23); the function only reads it, and a failed run keeps the file and the text.
9. **Reminder times** are not needed on the server: the phone schedules the notifications from the profile (step 28), and `daily_affirmations.scheduled_for` stays unset. Nothing in the function hardcodes 08:00, 13:00 or 20:00.
10. **Enforce the free tier server-side** (step 22). Free users get 3 devotionals a week (`FREE_DEVOTIONALS_PER_WEEK` in `_shared/limits/constants.ts`, mirrored in `src/constants/app.ts`); a trial or Pro account has no limit. The check goes after the crisis screening and before the model call that writes the reflection. The week runs from Monday 00:00 to the next Monday on the clock of the profile's time zone (`_shared/limits/weekly.ts`, `Intl` only; a zone `Intl` does not know is read as UTC and logged). An entry counts when it is not a crisis and it has its script, and so does an entry still being made that was claimed earlier than the one being checked (so two requests at once cannot both be the third and the fourth); a crisis entry, a failed one and the entry being checked itself never count. `checkDevotionalLimit` asks its two questions together (one round trip to a far database, not two), and two entries are put in order by their creation time to the millisecond, the larger id coming later. Over the limit the entry is saved as failed with `LIMIT_REACHED` (an audio entry keeps its transcript) and the answer is a 402 whose body also carries `entryId` and `usage` (`used`, `limit`, `resetsAt`), which opens the Paywall; a crisis is never counted or blocked. Known limit: the count is by when an entry was created, so a free account's blocked entry from an earlier week that is sent again later is made and not counted (counting by when it was made needs a column, a later migration). A narrower race remains and was left alone: two requests whose entries are created within a millisecond of each other, the later one's row becoming visible only after the earlier one's check, could still both pass; it costs one free devotional and a lock was not worth adding.
11. **Return consistent errors** (step 19, `_shared/errors.ts`): `{ error: { code, message, requestId } }`, the 12 codes and statuses of `ProcessEntryErrorCode`, with the messages from one map (`_shared/errors.ts` also holds `NOT_FOUND`, 404, which only `confirm-scripture` answers: each function's contract lists its own codes). The 402 also carries `entryId` and `usage` beside `error` (step 22, `AppError.details`: values the function built itself, never logged). Never a stack trace, a provider's words, SQL or anything the person wrote.
12. **Long work.** One request does it all: the model calls end by 90 seconds, the platform stops a function at 150 (free plan), and a claim lapses after 3 minutes so a request that died can be sent again. Speech (requirement 13) starts only in the first 100 seconds of a request, its Google calls end by 135 and its upload by at most 5 seconds after that (`speechTiming` in `process-entry/constants.ts`: the review's text let a 20-second upload start at 135, which is 155 and past the platform's 150), and it is skipped otherwise, so the answer goes out by about 145 seconds even when everything is slow, before the platform's 150: moving work to a background job would be a restructuring, to ask about first.
13. **Speak the devotional** (decided in the step 22 review, built in step 22; `_shared/tts/`). Speech is a best-effort step after the reflection is saved as `ready`, so nothing about it can lose a reflection. The script is built on the server from the saved devotional (`script.ts`): an opening line, the paragraphs, the passage with a spoken reference only when `scripture_verified`, and the closing prayer ending in "Amen."; every field is cleaned first, and a tag or a bracketed span is removed together with what is inside it, so the model cannot add a pause or a sound; over 4,800 bytes the passage goes first, then the prayer, and the devotional is never cut. Cloud Text-to-Speech reads it (Chirp 3 HD, `markup`, `LINEAR16`: a lossless WAV since 2026-10-24, see its vendor note), the function reads the length from the WAV's header (`wavDuration.ts`) and saves it as `{userId}/devotionals/{entryId}.wav` (`audio/wav`, about 48 KB a second) in `journal_media` (service role, replacing any earlier file), storing only the path and the duration. **No URL is returned or stored:** the app asks Supabase for its own signed link (`createSignedUrl(path, 3600)`, allowed by the bucket's select policy for its own folder), refreshes it before it expires and never keeps it. A failure leaves the entry `ready` with no audio (and `error_code` `TTS_FAILED` when a call failed, cleared when audio is added later); with no speech secrets set the step is simply off. A retry of a finished entry that has its devotional and no audio runs speech only (no screening, no limit, no model call), and its update only fills an empty audio path, so two retries cannot both win. The stage is `process-entry/speech.ts` (`speechStage`: it never throws and returns the entry as it now stands, with the log words), and a refusal by Google or Storage is marked with `markSpeechFailed`, guarded like `saveAudio` (a ready reflection with no audio, never a crisis entry, and it returns the marked row so the answer says `TTS_FAILED`). Ambient sound is never mixed in on the server. The log holds one word about speech (`tts`) and numbers, never the script, the verse or a link.

### Edge Function Rules

- `userId` comes only from `verifyClerkRequest`. Nothing else may supply it.
- One entry per `(user, clientEntryId)`. A retry of a finished entry returns it (no model call, no limit counted); a failed one, or one whose claim has lapsed, is claimed again by exactly one request (a compare-and-set update); one that is running answers 409.
- A reflection is `ready` only after its affirmations are saved: `ready` is the last write of a good run.
- Recordings are read from `journal_media`. No audio travels in a request body.
- `contracts.ts` and `src/types/index.ts` change together. `src/types/contractParity.ts` fails `npm run typecheck` when they differ, and `fixtures/parity.json` is checked against both the function's `response.ts` and the app's `toJournalEntry`.
- The log is one JSON line per request and only the allow-list in `_shared/log.ts`: the request id, a short hash of the user id, status, an error code or category, the input kind, durations, attempt and token counts (with `fallbackAttempts`, how many went to `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL`), the model name, and (step 20) what became of the passage as one word (`scripture`: verified, replaced, unverified or dropped) with the time spent reading the Bible (`bibleMs`), and (step 21) what the crisis screening answered as one word (`screen`: clear, crisis, no_speech or failed) with its time and attempts (`screenMs`, `screenAttempts`), and, when an entry ended as a crisis, the one word that says which check decided (`crisisSource`: phrase, model, uncertain or blocked), and (step 22) what the speech step did as one word (`tts`: ok, off, skipped:time, skipped:script, bad_config:<SECRET_NAME> or failed:<Google's or storage's short words>) with its time, calls and billed characters (`ttsMs`, `ttsAttempts`, `ttsChars`) and the stored file's length (`audioMs`), and what the free tier's check found (`limit`: exempt, free_ok or free_blocked, with `:bad_timezone` when the profile's zone was not valid; `limitUsed`: the count it found), 30 fields in all. Never a token or its claims, an email, entry text, audio, model output, the script that was read aloud, scripture (not even which book or chapter), the categories of concern the screening named, a phrase that matched, a link, a storage path, a key, the service account's address, SQL, or a provider's error message (those can quote the entry).
- `confirm-scripture` (step 20) takes `{ entryId }` and answers `{ scripture }` (`ConfirmScriptureResponse`). The user id comes only from the token and every query filters by it, so another person's entry and an entry that does not exist both answer the same 404 `NOT_FOUND`. It never calls the model and never stores a passage it did not read from the Bible: a successful read saves the text and sets `scripture_verified`, a Bible that cannot be read changes nothing, and a reference that cannot be read or cannot exist is removed. An entry changed in the last minute, or looked up by this instance in the last minute, is answered as it is. A crisis entry, an unfinished one and one with no passage have nothing to confirm and are never written to.
- `GEMINI_MODEL`, `GEMINI_SCREENING_MODEL` and `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL` have no default in code (the screening falls back to `GEMINI_MODEL` only when its own is empty, and with no fallback model every attempt goes to the first one), and every Gemini request sets `store: false`.
- A secret is a name in `supabase/functions/.env.example` and a value only in `supabase/functions/.env.local` (git ignores it) and in the hosted project. Nothing prints a value.
- The user deploys: login, `secrets set` and `functions deploy` are theirs to run, and the implementer never holds a management token. A change to a function is not live until they deploy it, and each function is deployed on its own (`functions deploy process-entry`, `functions deploy confirm-scripture`).

---

## Crisis Safety Rules (NON-NEGOTIABLE)

Gracehush will receive entries about self-harm, abuse, and despair.

- The Edge Function must flag entries showing risk of self-harm, suicide, abuse, or immediate danger, and return `is_crisis: true`. It does so in a dedicated screening that runs before anything else is made for the person (Crisis Screening Rules).
- For crisis entries:
  - Show the Reflection **Crisis Support state** instead of the devotional.
  - Do not generate or schedule affirmations.
  - Do not autoplay audio.
  - Use the copy from the design (`#view-crisis` in `prompt_material/05-reflection.html`): "It sounds like you’re going through something really heavy. You don’t have to carry this alone." It outranks a step prompt’s own wording (step 13 puts it in `copy.crisis.title`).
  - Show the resources on the Crisis Support state itself, from `src/constants/crisisResources.ts` (988 Suicide & Crisis Lifeline, Crisis Text Line, 911, and a link to international helplines), with tap-to-call and tap-to-text actions. They work offline: the app fetches nothing to show or open them, and only a web link needs a connection.
  - No haptic, sound, pulsing or animation on the crisis state, no analytics event and, in the app, no log line about a crisis entry or the Support Resources screen (step 34 says the same), and never log a transcript. (The Edge Function's request log holds one word about the screening, as Crisis Screening Rules says; the app logs nothing.) (Step 13 removed the mock `submitEntry`'s `outcome` log line for this reason: it printed "crisis". Step 25's real submit must not log the outcome either.)
- Never make claims about confidentiality, cost or what a hotline will do in the app’s own words. The design’s "confidential" lines are not built: the app states availability ("24/7") only, and a service’s own page may say the rest.
- Hotline data must be easy to update and should be reviewed before every release: open the three official pages (988lifeline.org, crisistextline.org, findahelpline.com), check every number and URL, and only then change `CRISIS_RESOURCES_LAST_VERIFIED` (shown in the Support Resources footer; last checked 2026-10-20, when the 988 chat page was found at `chat.988lifeline.org`, which the old `/chat/` address redirects to). That chat page serves only the U.S. and its territories (from another country it shows "Access Restricted", and automated clients get a 403), which fits V1's en-US scope.
- If you are unsure whether a change affects crisis handling, stop and ask.

---

## Crisis Screening Rules (step 21)

The server decides whether an entry is a crisis before it makes anything for the person. The code is `supabase/functions/_shared/safety/`, and `process-entry/handler.ts` is the flow.

- **Order.** Verify the token, validate the body, ensure the profile, rate limit (429), claim the entry, read the recording if there is one, **screen**, then the free-tier limit (step 22, the `TODO(22)` in `handler.ts`: after the screening, never before it), then the reflection from the screened words alone (never from the recording again), then the passage, the save and the affirmations. A crisis returns at the screening, so it never reaches the limit, the reflection, the Bible or speech, and it is never paywalled or counted toward a limit. A retry of a finished entry, a crisis included, returns it as it is and screens nothing.
- **No screening result, no reflection.** If the screening cannot be had within its 60 seconds (the service is down, busy or slow on every attempt, up to four with growing pauses, or it answers in a shape that cannot be used twice) the entry is failed with `AI_UNAVAILABLE` and can be sent again. Silence in a recording is `NO_SPEECH`. Nothing is ever generated for an entry that was not screened.
- **Uncertain and blocked are crises.** The model answers `none`, `uncertain` or `crisis` and is told to choose the higher level when unsure. `uncertain` and a safety-filter block both make a crisis entry, and a crisis outranks everything else a reply says, a contradicting `no_speech` included.
- **Three layers**, each able to decide alone. (1) The phrase backstop (`phrases.ts`): explicit first-person statements, checked on a written entry before the model is called (a match needs no model, so it works while the model is down and cannot be talked out of) and on a recording's transcript after it. (2) The screening call (`screen.ts`), with the five adjustable safety filters all `off` so that it can read what a person in distress writes. (3) The reflection's own `is_crisis` flag, and a safety block on the reflection request. Layer 3 is for what the first two miss, and the log shows when it was the one that caught an entry.
- **Not repeatable, so fail safe.** Gemini 3.x takes no temperature (see the Gemini safety note), so the screening can answer differently twice. The phrase layer and the failing-safe mapping are what make the whole dependable.
- **What a crisis entry stores:** `is_crisis` and the person's words (a written entry's own text, or the screening's transcript, or none when a recording was blocked before it was read). No category, source, reason or phrase is stored anywhere. No notification, email, webhook or outreach, no mark on the profile, and no change in how long the reply takes. It never gets affirmations (`TODO(28)` stays for the schedule). If the crisis cannot be saved the answer is a plain 500 with the reason `crisis_save_failed`, the entry is failed and can be sent again, and a devotional is never the fallback.
- **The request log** is described under Edge Function Rules. It holds the screening's answer as one word, its source as one word and the hashed user id, and never the words, a phrase that matched, the categories or model output. AGENTS.md's "no log line about a crisis entry" is about the app. If the one-word server marker is not wanted, it is four fields in `log.ts` (`screen`, `screenMs`, `screenAttempts`, `crisisSource`).
- **The phrase list is server-only:** never sent to the client, shown or logged, and it holds no method or means. Christian speech is handled on purpose. "Take my life" (a hymn line) is not in the list, "die to self" and "end my life of sin" are removed from the text before the phrases are looked for, and "I do not want to live" counts only with "anymore", "any longer" or "another day", because "I do not want to live in fear" is a prayer. A negation such as "I'm not suicidal anymore" still matches, on purpose.
- **No method or means** is written anywhere: code, prompts, tests, comments or copy.
- **Evaluation.** `_shared/safety/eval/cases.json` holds 30 made-up entries (10 crisis, 5 uncertain that also expect a crisis, 15 clear), and `run_eval.ts` runs them by hand against the real API (never in CI or a deploy). Run it after ANY change to `screenPrompt.ts`, `phrases.ts`, the screening model or `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL` (which screens every second attempt, so it is evaluated on its own, with `GEMINI_SCREENING_MODEL` set to it): every crisis case must come back as a crisis (100% recall), no case may go unanswered, and if more than 3 of the 15 clear cases are flagged, stop, show the ids and ask before changing the wording. It prints ids and labels, never the text. `cases_test.ts` keeps the set honest offline: a clear case must not hold a phrase, and several crisis cases must be left to the model. The evaluation sends only these made-up cases, whatever the key's tier.

---

## Local Cache & Offline Rules

- Every new entry is saved locally **first** (status `pending`), then synced.
- Sync queue states: `pending` → `uploading` → `processing` → `ready` | `failed`.
- Show the Offline banner and "Waiting to sync" chips from the Journal design while offline.
- Retry failed syncs with backoff when connectivity returns. Never silently drop an entry.
- The local cache holds sensitive text. Recommend encryption (for example SQLCipher via expo-sqlite) and ask before enabling it. Always wipe it on sign-out.
- The server is the source of truth once an entry is `ready`.

---

## Audio Rules

**Recording**

- Tap to start, tap to stop. 5:00 maximum with a visible timer.
- AAC / `.m4a`. Request the microphone permission only after the Permissions primer.
- Handle interruptions (calls, app backgrounding) without losing the recording.

**Playback (react-native-track-player)**

- Set up the player once at app start. Register the playback service and background audio modes in `app.json`.
- Use the real track duration, not a hardcoded `60` or `90`.
- Artwork comes from `images.playerArtwork`, not a remote Unsplash URL.
- Signed URLs expire, so refresh the URL before playing if needed.
- Capabilities: play, pause, seek, jump ±15s, playback rate (0.8x / 1x / 1.2x).
- The player UI reads one Zustand store, `src/store/playerStore.ts` through `usePlayback` (step 12). Step 26 swaps its mock internals for the real player and keeps the state and action names. A devotional is loaded only when the user acts on a player, never because its reflection was viewed (that would stop the one playing and, with real audio, fetch a signed URL for every entry looked at). `stop()` is a full reset that sign-out calls. `load(track, ambient)` is synchronous and the store never fetches: the caller passes the profile, the same entry does nothing (so reopening a reflection never resets its position), and another entry stops the one playing and starts at 0, paused, keeping the session's speed and ambient choice (the first load after the app starts, or after `stop()`, copies the profile's ambient sound and volume). The clock is wall time times the rate, so a late JS timer does not make it lag. A track comes from `getPlaybackTrack(entry)` (`data/selectors.ts`), which is null for a crisis entry, an unfinished one, and a devotional with no audio or no measured length: a crisis entry never reaches a player. Profile's ambient default (step 15) is the sound the next devotional starts with: after a successful save Profile calls `reseedAmbient()` (it clears the "seeded" flag and changes no state), so the next `load` of a different devotional copies the profile again, a devotional that is playing keeps its sound and volume, and Profile never calls `setAmbient` or `setAmbientVolume`. Without it the store would ignore a new default until the app restarted.

**Spoken devotional (step 22, on the server)**

- The function builds the speech script, has Cloud Text-to-Speech read it and stores the lossless WAV (`LINEAR16`, 24 kHz mono 16-bit, about 5.5 MB for two minutes) at `{userId}/devotionals/{entryId}.wav` in the private `journal_media` bucket. Only the path and the measured duration are stored, never a URL (Backend requirement 13). Step 26 plays that WAV through its signed link (PCM WAV is native on both platforms) and keeps a played devotional on the phone, so each one is downloaded once.
- The app never receives a link from the function. It mints a signed URL for the path itself, `supabase.storage.from("journal_media").createSignedUrl(path, 3600)`, keeps it in memory only and refreshes it before it expires. A devotional with no audio path (speech off, failed or skipped) is the "audio isn't ready yet" state; step 26 can ask the function to add the audio by sending the same request again.
- The ambient loop is never part of this file: it is mixed on the phone (step 27).

**Ambient soundscape**

- Google TTS does not produce ambient sound. Play ambient loops on the client, in parallel with the devotional, from `assets/audio/`.
- track-player plays one queue, so ambient needs a second audio channel. Propose the approach and ask before adding a library.

---

## Notification Rules (expo-notifications)

- Schedule affirmations locally, using the user's saved reminder times and the device time zone.
- Give each notification a stable identifier (`gracehush-morning`, `gracehush-afternoon`, `gracehush-evening`). Cancel only those IDs. **Do not use `cancelAllScheduledNotificationsAsync()`**, because it wipes unrelated notifications.
- Reschedule after each new `ready` entry, after reminder times change, and on app launch.
- Respect the "Daily Affirmations Push" toggle in Profile.
- Never schedule affirmations for crisis entries.
- Tapping a notification opens the related Reflection.
- There is no "your reflection is ready" notification in V1 (decided in the step 11 follow-up, 2026-10-20; it answers manual setup item 4 of prompt 11). A local notification cannot know when the server finishes, so it would need a server push, which needs the Apple developer account (last, see Instructional Memory) and does not run in Expo Go on Android. The processing copy only promises that the reflection will be "waiting in your Journal". Revisit if step 19 makes processing asynchronous (Backend / Edge Function Rules, item 12).
- Notification content must never include sensitive transcript text. Use only the affirmation headline and body.

---

## Subscription Rules (RevenueCat)

- RevenueCat is the source of truth for entitlements. Use one entitlement: `pro`.
- Read offerings and prices at runtime. Show trial terms exactly as configured.
- The Paywall must include Restore Purchases, the trial and renewal terms, Terms of Use, and Privacy Policy (App Store and Play requirements).
- Sync `subscription_tier` to `users_profile` server-side via a RevenueCat webhook, never from the client.
- Gate Pro features in the UI with a `useEntitlement()` hook, but always enforce limits on the server too.
- **What Pro is in V1 (decided in the step 16 review, 2026-10-21):** unlimited audio devotionals. Free users get `FREE_DEVOTIONALS_PER_WEEK` a week (Backend rule 10). The Paywall and Profile's Pro card may advertise only a benefit that is a real gate in this file. The design's other four rows (ambient soundscapes, a "never expires" history, the answered prayer timeline and a choice of devotional voices) are not gates today: the first and third are free for everyone, the history has no retention rule, and the voice is one server-side setting. To advertise one, first make it a gate here, then add its row.
- Offer a free trial only when the package has one **and** the store confirms this user is eligible; never advertise a trial that cannot be confirmed, and never show a savings percentage that the prices do not support.
- The Paywall never says "cancel with one tap", "no commitments" or that a failed purchase "has not been charged": cancellation happens in the App Store or Google Play settings, and a dropped connection can follow a charge. The disclosure names where, and a failed purchase points to Restore purchases.
- **The step 16 purchase service is a mock that fails closed** (`src/services/purchases.ts`, `TEMP(30)`): outside `__DEV__` there is no offering and a purchase answers `NOT_ALLOWED`, so a build can never hand out Pro for free. **Do not ship a build that takes payments between step 16 and step 30**, and run the release gate under "Before a store build" (Linting and Validation) before any build that anyone else can install: it stays red while this mock exists.
- **Launch pricing (adopted 2026-10-22, when the user approved the step 16 follow-up recommendations):** $9.99 a month and $59.99 a year, with a 7-day free trial on the yearly plan only (the monthly plan has none). That is the 50% saving the Paywall computes. These are the products to create in App Store Connect, Play Console and RevenueCat at step 30: the user sets the prices there, they are never typed into the app, and `data/mock/offerings.ts` keeps them only as placeholders. Changing a price later is a store and RevenueCat edit, not a release. Test a 14-day trial with RevenueCat Experiments once there is traffic (a longer trial may convert better, and it also raises forgot-to-cancel refunds). Before the yearly price is locked, check what one devotional costs in Gemini and Cloud Text-to-Speech with the real voice, because the yearly plan caps what a daily listener pays. Measured on 2026-10-24 with the real voice (Aoede at 0.88): Chirp 3 HD is $30 per million characters after the first million each month, and a real reflection's script is 1,688 characters (the passage included), so about $0.051 of speech, plus about $0.0017 of Gemini for the screening and the reflection: about $0.052 a devotional, and the first million characters a month cover about 590 of them. A daily listener therefore costs about $19 a year to serve, against the $59.99 yearly price before the stores' share. The estimate before the real run was $16 to $19 (1,300 to 1,700 characters); one reflection is one sample, and step 32 may change the length.

---

## Database Rules

The database is managed in Supabase. Step 17 created the project and its first schema by hand: the user ran `supabase/schema.sql` once in the SQL editor, then `supabase/selftest.sql` (11 checks). Step 18 turned the schema into migrations: `supabase/migrations/20261022120000_baseline_users_profile.sql` (a copy of `schema.sql` under a short header; `supabase/tests/run-in-pglite.mjs` refuses to run if the two drift apart) and `supabase/migrations/20261022120100_gracehush_v1_schema.sql` (the `journal_entries` and `daily_affirmations` tables, the new `users_profile` columns, the private `journal_media` bucket with its two policies, every grant and row level security). A third, `supabase/migrations/20261024120000_journal_media_accepts_wav.sql` (2026-10-24), adds `audio/wav` to the bucket's accepted types, with check 46 in `rls_selftest.sql` and a broken variant in the runner, and it must run after the v1 migration (which sets the bucket's whole list whenever it is run). The first two files are idempotent, were written in the step 18 review and were proven offline in PGlite (a real Postgres engine compiled to WebAssembly, because Docker is not installed on this machine): every migration applied twice, `selftest.sql` and `supabase/tests/rls_selftest.sql` (45 checks) passing twice, and 17 deliberately broken variants (a grant too wide, row level security off, a public bucket, a missing constraint and so on) each failing exactly the checks they should, under both settings of the dashboard option "Automatically expose new tables". **Nothing in the repo and nothing the implementer runs can reach the hosted database**: there is no CLI link, no database password and no management token. The user applies a migration by pasting the file into the dashboard's SQL editor and runs the self-tests there, which are the judge; the implementer never runs SQL against the hosted project.

The project was created with "Automatically expose new tables" OFF (Supabase makes that the default for every project on 2026-10-30; existing tables keep their grants), so the Data API cannot see a table until it is granted: every table needs explicit grants, `service_role` included (the RevenueCat webhook, `process-entry` and account deletion reach the tables through it). The v1 migration revokes first and grants back column by column, because Postgres ignores a column-level REVOKE while a table-wide GRANT exists. EXECUTE on functions is not affected by that setting.

- All schema, RLS and storage policy changes go in `supabase/migrations/` as timestamped, idempotent SQL files. Each new rule gets a check in `supabase/tests/rls_selftest.sql` and a broken variant in `run-in-pglite.mjs`; the user applies the file in the SQL editor and runs both self-tests there.
- Never edit the hosted schema by hand (the one exception was `supabase/schema.sql` in step 17, which step 18 baselined). Never disable RLS.
- Ask before creating any migration.
- **Types.** `src/types/supabase.ts` is generated from the real database and never edited by hand; regenerate it after every migration. The dashboard offers a TypeScript download at https://supabase.com/dashboard/project/_/api?page=tables-intro (it opens Integrations, Data API, Docs; the button is "Generate and download types", under the "Generating types" heading: confirmed in step 18, which used it), or, in Git Bash (PowerShell 5.1 writes UTF-16), run `npx supabase login` once and then `npx supabase gen types typescript --project-id <ref> --schema public > src/types/supabase.ts`. `src/types/database.ts` re-exports the row types and narrows what the app may send, so a write the database would refuse is a compile error.
- **What the signed-in app may write (decided in step 18).** `users_profile`: INSERT `id`, the three reminder times, `timezone`, `affirmations_enabled`, `ambient_sound` and `ambient_volume`; UPDATE the same without `id`. `journal_entries`: no INSERT and no DELETE; UPDATE `is_active_prayer`, `is_answered`, `answered_at`, `answered_notes` and `prayer_category`. `daily_affirmations`: no INSERT and no DELETE; UPDATE `is_favorite`. Everything else is written by the server (`service_role`): the plan (`subscription_tier`), `is_crisis`, `processing_status`, `error_code`, the transcript, scripture, devotional and audio columns, `prayer_title` and `prayer_summary`; entries and affirmations are created only by `process-entry`. Storage: `journal_media` is private; the app reads its own folder and uploads only to `{userId}/raw/{clientEntryId}.m4a` (no overwrite, no delete); the server writes `{userId}/devotionals/{entryId}.wav` (`audio/wav`, accepted by the bucket since the migration of 2026-10-24).

### Schema: built in step 18, and what is still open

Built in `20261022120100_gracehush_v1_schema.sql` (these are the real names; the hand-written types of step 06 had guesses):

- `journal_entries`: `client_entry_id` (unique with `user_id`, so a retried submit never creates a second row), `processing_status` (pending, uploading, processing, ready, failed), `error_code`, `is_crisis`, `audio_path` (the raw recording), `devotional_audio_path` and `devotional_duration_ms`, `scripture_verified`, `devotional_title`, `closing_prayer`, `answered_notes` (at most 1,000 characters), `updated_at`, and the prayer fields `prayer_title`, `prayer_category` (six values) and `prayer_summary` (at most 200). There is no `devotional_audio_url`: no public URL was ever stored, so nothing is deprecated or backfilled.
- Rules in the table itself: a crisis entry has no devotional, audio, scripture or prayer (`journal_entries_crisis_check`, `crisis_not_prayer`); a prayer is answered on a date and only then (`answered_fields_match`, `answered_note_length`: the rules prompt 29 proposed, built here while the table is empty); verified scripture has its reference and its text (`scripture_verified_check`); an entry has one affirmation per slot, owned by the entry's user, and none at all for a crisis entry (a trigger).
- `users_profile`: `timezone`, `ambient_sound`, `ambient_volume`, `affirmations_enabled`, `updated_at`. Onboarding completion stays on the device and the devotional voice is one server-side setting, so neither is a column.
- `daily_affirmations`: `is_favorite`, `is_delivered` and `scheduled_for`. The app can write only `is_favorite`; step 28 may leave the other two unused.

Still open: storage file deletion on account delete (step 31); `prayer_title` is written by `process-entry` since step 19 (the Gemini schema asks for a prayer title, a summary and a category); the `embedding VECTOR(768)` column and spiritual memory are **V2**, do not build them unless asked.

---

## State Management Rules

Use Zustand for global client state:

- onboarding progress and reminder times
- current playback state (track, position, speed, ambient)
- sync queue status and connectivity
- entitlement snapshot for UI gating
- app settings

Use local component state for temporary UI state (segmented tabs, sheet visibility, form inputs).

Do not mirror server data in Zustand if it already lives in the local SQLite cache. Read it through hooks.

Persist settings with AsyncStorage only when they don't belong in the profile table. Never persist journal text in AsyncStorage.

**Onboarding store (step 07).** `src/store/onboardingStore.ts` persists `hasCompletedOnboarding`, `completedAt`, `reminders` (`"HH:mm"`, 24-hour, keep that format), `permissionsPromptedAt`, `remindersSyncedAt` and `remindersDirty` under the key `gracehush.onboarding` (version 2). The current step index and any permission status are not persisted: the OS is always the source of truth for permissions, and relaunching mid-flow starts at Welcome with the saved times. Every persisted store uses `src/store/safeStorage.ts`, validates every field in its own `merge` (zustand's default merge is shallow), and is gated with `useStoreHydrated` wherever the first render depends on it (`AppShell` holds the splash until it is true). No journal text or other sensitive value goes into AsyncStorage: this store holds only flags, times and timestamps. Since step 17 `services/profileSync.ts` keeps `reminders` in step with `users_profile` (since step 18 in the same request as the three settings and the time zone). Version 2 added `remindersDirty` (an edit the server has not seen) and three actions: `markRemindersSynced`, `applyServerReminders(times)` (the server's times replace the device's and the edit flag clears) and `discardUnsyncedReminders` (sign-out: an unsent edit belongs to the account that made it). `migrate` treats a version 1 save whose time differs from the default and was never synced as unsent, so an existing install keeps its times. The rule: equal times need no move, an unsent edit is pushed, otherwise the server's times are applied.

**Data layer (step 06).** Screens read data only through `src/data/journalRepository.ts` (directly or through a hook), never from `src/data/mock/`. The mock scenario lives in the dev store (step 09): `getMockScenario()` reads `useDevStore.getState().scenario`, `setMockScenario` is gone, and the scenario buttons in `DevToolsPanel` call `setScenario`. `db.ts` rebuilds the fixtures when the scenario changes, and screens subscribe with `useDevStore((state) => state.scenario)`. Mock rows are DB-shaped, live in `mockDb` (profile, entries, affirmations) and go through the mappers in `src/data/mappers.ts`. `getMockScenario()` returns `'default'` outside `__DEV__`. Models are plain JSON (ISO strings, `null` rather than `undefined`) because step 24 caches them as JSON. A `pending` or `uploading` row is local-only, and a crisis entry never carries an emotion, devotional, scripture or prayer. Formatting lives in `src/utils/format.ts`. `Scripture.text` is `null` unless the verse is verified. **Submit (step 10).** `submitEntry(req, onStage)` in the repository is a mock (`TEMP(25)`): it takes the one-shot outcome from `takeMockSubmitOutcome()`, reports the stages 1.2s apart (`ready`: receiving, reflecting, preparing; `crisis` and `failed`: receiving, reflecting; `limit`: receiving), and answers `ready` or `crisis` with the id of a new entry, `failed` with `AI_UNAVAILABLE`, `limit` with `LIMIT_REACHED`. `addMockEntry(kind, req)` in `db.ts` (`TEMP(25)`) is what makes the mock database grow: it clones the anxious (ready) or crisis fixture under `entry-new-{clientEntryId}`, is idempotent on `client_entry_id` like the real server, quotes typed text as the transcript, adds no prayer, and for `ready` also clones the three affirmations, so Journal, its affirmation card and Reflection all work after a submit in every scenario (a scenario switch rebuilds the fixtures and drops the clones). Only stage names and outcome codes are logged, never an id, a path or text.

**Dev store (step 09).** `src/store/devStore.ts` is a non-persisted, flat Zustand store for development only: `scenario` with `setScenario`, `nextHomeLoadFails` with `setNextHomeLoadFails`, the one-shot flag that makes the next Journal Home load fail once (the hook clears it as it fails), and (step 10) `nextSubmitOutcome` with `setNextSubmitOutcome` (`MockSubmitOutcome`, default `"ready"`), which `takeMockSubmitOutcome()` reads and resets in the same call, and (step 11) `nextReflectionLoadFails` with `setNextReflectionLoadFails`, the same one-shot flag for the Reflection load (`useReflection` clears it as it fails), and (step 13) `nextCrisisLinkFails` with `setNextCrisisLinkFails`, a one-shot flag that makes the next crisis call, text or web link "unopenable" so the fallback sheet can be reached on a device that has a phone app (`useCrisisActions` clears it as it fails the link; it is ignored outside `__DEV__`), and (step 14) `nextPrayerMutationFails` with `setNextPrayerMutationFails`, a one-shot flag that makes the next prayer change (a Mark Answered save, a note edit or a move back to Active) fail once with `NETWORK` (`takeMockPrayerMutationFailure()` in `scenario.ts` reads and clears it in the same call, and answers `false` outside `__DEV__`; step 15 reuses this flag for the next profile save, data export and account deletion and adds no third), and (step 15) `mockProfileVersion` with `bumpMockProfileVersion` (the mock plan lives in the mock database, outside any store, so Profile and the dev panel reload when it changes) and `notificationStatusOverride` with `setNotificationStatusOverride` (`null`, `"granted"`, `"denied"` or `"undetermined"`: what Profile treats as the notification permission, because Expo Go on Android cannot import `expo-notifications` and always answers `"undetermined"`; `useProfileSettings` reads it only in `__DEV__`). Step 16 added `nextPurchaseOutcome` with `setNextPurchaseOutcome` (`MockPurchaseOutcome`: "success" (the default), "cancelled", "pending", "failed", "not-allowed" or "already-owned"; one-shot like the submit outcome: `takeMockPurchaseOutcome()` in `scenario.ts` reads it and puts it back to "success", and answers "success" in a release build), `trialEligible` (default true: whether the store would offer this user a free trial; buying a trial turns it off for the session), `restoreFindsPurchase` (default false) and `offeringUnavailable` (default false: the store has no offering, which is how the paywall's unavailable state is reached), each with its setter. Step 17 added `profileSource` with `setProfileSource` (`"supabase"` or `"mock"`, default `"supabase"`): where the profile comes from, read through `getProfileSource()`, which answers `"mock"` when `.env.local` has no Supabase address and always answers `"supabase"` in a release build. Nothing in a release build sets any of these: `getMockScenario()` ignores the store there and the purchases mock fails closed. Later steps (10, 14, 17, 24, 25) add their own `next…` flags and fields to it, and their controls to `DevToolsPanel`. `scenario.ts` imports the store at runtime, and the store imports only the `MockScenario` type from `scenario.ts` (`import type`), so there is no runtime cycle.

---

## TypeScript Rules

Use TypeScript strictly.

Avoid `any`, including in Edge Functions. Type Gemini, HelloAO, and TTS responses explicitly.

Keep app models and the process-entry contracts (`TimeSlot`, `JournalEntry`, `ProcessEntryRequest`, `ProcessEntryResponse`, `ProcessEntryErrorCode`) in `src/types/index.ts`, and mirror the contracts in `supabase/functions/process-entry/contracts.ts` (step 19; types only, no imports). `src/types/contractParity.ts` makes any difference between the two copies a type error. Database row types come from the generated `src/types/supabase.ts` (never edited by hand, step 18) and are re-exported by `src/types/database.ts`, which also narrows what the app may send to the columns it is granted. Gemini output types are server-only and live with the Edge Function.

Keep types simple and readable.

Type navigation with `RootStackParamList` (`src/navigation/types.ts`) and screen props with `NativeStackScreenProps<RootStackParamList, "Name">`. No `any`.

Ambient declarations (`*.svg`, `expo/types`) live in committed files under `src/types/`. Do not rely on the generated `expo-env.d.ts`: Expo CLI deletes it, and typecheck then fails on `import "./global.css"`.

---

## Feature Implementation Rules

When the user asks to build a feature:

1. Read this file first.
2. Identify the files to change.
3. Keep changes focused.
4. Do not rewrite unrelated code.
5. Follow existing patterns.
6. Ensure the feature works end-to-end (client → Edge Function → DB → client). A feature that calls a model reads "Gemini connection and credit" (Backend / Edge Function Rules) first: the credit is active and the key is already connected.
7. Check that loading, empty, error, and offline states are handled.
8. Fix errors before finishing.

---

## Code Simplicity Rules

Avoid overengineering.

Refactor only when needed.

No premature abstractions, generic "manager" classes, or custom frameworks.

---

## Component Creation Rule

Only create reusable components when necessary.

Ask if unsure.

---

## Linting and Validation

Run:

```bash
npm run lint
npm run typecheck
```

Check the real exit codes and read the full output. Never pipe these commands through `tail` or `head`, which hides failures. Lint warnings count: fix the cause instead of disabling the rule.

Typecheck and lint cannot catch Metro, NativeWind, or import-resolution problems, so also bundle the app:

```bash
npx expo export --platform android --platform ios --output-dir <a temp folder outside the repo>
```

To confirm that new classes compile, add `--no-bytecode --no-minify` and search the bundle for the class name.

For Edge Function changes, also run these from the repo root. No Docker is needed: Deno and the Supabase CLI run through `npx`.

```bash
D="npx --yes deno@2.9.6"; C="--config supabase/functions/process-entry/deno.json"
$D check --no-lock --node-modules-dir=none $C supabase/functions/process-entry/index.ts supabase/functions/confirm-scripture/index.ts supabase/functions/process-entry/live_check.ts supabase/functions/_shared/safety/eval/run_eval.ts supabase/functions/_shared/tts/audition.ts
$D check --no-lock --node-modules-dir=none --config supabase/functions/confirm-scripture/deno.json supabase/functions/confirm-scripture/index.ts
$D lint $C supabase/functions/process-entry supabase/functions/confirm-scripture supabase/functions/_shared
$D fmt --check --line-width=120 supabase/functions
$D test --no-lock --node-modules-dir=none $C --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read supabase/functions/process-entry supabase/functions/confirm-scripture supabase/functions/_shared
node supabase/tests/process-entry/parity.cjs
```

The tests run the real handlers, `jose` and `supabase-js` against a fake Clerk key set, Gemini, the Bible API (real chapters from `_shared/bible/fixtures/`), Google's token endpoint and Text-to-Speech (a real RSA key, a real WAV), PostgREST and Storage (`process-entry/testing/fakes.ts`; Storage takes an upload as the real bucket does, binary and with its type, size and duplicate rules, and the database clock never gives two rows the same time). The test command names every function folder and `_shared` because Deno only finds tests under the folder of the `--config` file. The lint command names them for the same reason: with `--config` pointing into `process-entry`, `deno lint supabase/functions` checked only that folder (24 files) until step 21 noticed, and the three folders together are 51 files (72 after step 22; a planted `var` in a new `_shared` file is caught by the long form and was missed by the short one). `_shared/tts/audition.ts` writes one WAV per voice and speed into `tts-audition/` for listening (by hand, with the service account's key; it was proven against a fake Google that answers the token endpoint, the voice list and a synthesize call). `process-entry/live_check.ts` calls the real Gemini API and the real Bible API by hand with made-up text, `_shared/bible/sweep_check.ts` reads all 31,086 verses of the Berean Standard Bible through the real parser, and the deployed functions are checked from the phone with the dev panel's "Process-entry check (dev)", which also compares the stored passage with the public Bible API. `supabase/functions` is excluded from the app's `tsc` (it is Deno code), and `expo lint` only lints `src`. Fix all errors before finishing.

The crisis screening is also evaluated by hand against the real API (it sends only the made-up cases in the file). Run it after any change to the screening prompt, the phrase list, the screening model or the fallback model (for that one, with `GEMINI_SCREENING_MODEL` set to it on the command line: `--env-file` does not replace a variable that is already set), and read the table: every crisis case must come back as a crisis (Crisis Screening Rules).

```bash
npx --yes deno@2.9.6 run --no-lock --node-modules-dir=none --config supabase/functions/process-entry/deno.json \
  --allow-net --allow-env --allow-read --env-file=supabase/functions/.env.local \
  supabase/functions/_shared/safety/eval/run_eval.ts [--only id,id] [--pause ms] [--audio file=expected]
```

Test on an Android development build when the change touches audio, notifications, purchases, or native modules. The iOS development build waits for the iOS developer account (see Instructional Memory). UI-only changes can be verified in Expo Go SDK 58.

### Before a store build

A build that anyone besides the developer can install (a Play Console upload on any track, TestFlight, the App Store) waits until this prints nothing (Git Bash on this machine):

```bash
grep -rnE "TEMP\(|TODO: real" src
```

Every `TEMP(NN)` is a mock that a numbered step replaces (purchases, the player, the recorder, the repository, the account service), and the `TODO: real …` constants are the Privacy Policy and Terms addresses and the support email that the Paywall and Profile link to. On 2026-10-22 it printed 17 lines after step 17 (it was 19 before: the two `TEMP(17)` comment lines left `journalRepository.ts`): 14 `TEMP(` markers (steps 23, 24, 25, 26, 29, 30 and 31) and the 3 constants. It is a floor, not a checklist: it cannot see a missing key, a store listing or a form. A development build for the developer's own phone (step 05b, step 35) may still carry the markers.

### Committing and publishing (the repository is public)

The repository is https://github.com/danieldazong/gracehush (remote `origin`; GitHub redirects its old name, `gracehush-app`). Only `main` exists: "dev/main" has meant `main` each time the user said it (2026-10-22 and 2026-10-23), and a `dev` branch is made only if the user asks. Commit and push only when asked. Never force-push unless the user asks for it (then always `--force-with-lease=main:<the sha you expect>`). If GitHub has moved, `git fetch` and `git merge --ff-only origin/main`: the user edits the README there.

- **No secret and no identifying value in a committed file.** Not a key or token, not the contents of `.env.local` or `supabase/functions/.env.local` (both git-ignored; the committed `.env.example` files hold names only), and not the Supabase project ref, the Clerk instance domain (a publishable key is `pk_test_` plus that domain in base64, so the domain is the key), the length of a key, a local path or a personal email address. Documents write `<project-ref>` and `<dev-instance>` (as in `https://<dev-instance>.clerk.accounts.dev`) from the start; the real values stay in the git-ignored files. The fakes in the tests (`sb_secret_test_key`, `test-gemini-key`, `https://a.clerk.accounts.dev`) are intentional.
- **Audit before every push, and again on the final commits.** Read every value from both `.env.local` files, derive the project ref (from `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`) and the Clerk domain (the publishable key without `pk_test_`, base64-decoded, minus the trailing `$`; also `CLERK_ISSUER`), and search each as a literal in every file git would commit and in every commit (`git grep -I -F`); print names and counts, never a value. Then scan the lines being added, untruncated (AGENTS.md has lines of 9,000 characters), for key shapes (`sb_secret_`, `sbp_`, JWTs, `AIza`, `AQ.`, `pk_` and `sk_`, `whsec_`, private key blocks, connection strings with a password, bearer tokens), emails, local paths and long hex or mixed-case strings, and look at every new or changed binary file. After the push, fetch a few files from `raw.githubusercontent.com` and check them the same way. A pattern with a lookahead does not work in `grep -E`: make a check find something known before trusting a "none".
- **Stage with explicit paths, never `git add -A`**: it adds `.claude/skills/` and `.commandcode/` (copies of `.agents/skills`). `LICENSE` stays untracked until the user chooses a license. Among env-like files only `supabase/functions/.env.example` and the root `.env` and `.env.example` may be staged, and `git check-ignore` must list both `.env.local` files.
- **Commit messages** are Conventional Commits for developers who have never seen this project: an imperative subject of at most 72 characters with no step or prompt number, a body wrapped at 72 (`awk 'length > 72'` finds long lines), and the attribution line the harness asks for. Split a large change into commits that each build: typecheck an earlier commit alone in a temporary `git worktree` with `node_modules` linked, then remove the worktree.
- **On this machine** `~/.gitconfig` has a placeholder identity, so commit with `-c user.name="danieldazong"` and `-c user.email=` set to the account's GitHub noreply address (the one on the existing commits), and push with `git -c http.version=HTTP/1.1 -c http.postBuffer=524288000 push` (a plain push fails with HTTP 408). `gh` is not installed, and Git Credential Manager already holds the GitHub login.

### Verifying on this machine (Windows)

- An Android emulator can run from the installed SDK (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Android\Sdk`, API 37 x86_64 image, WHPX acceleration). `avdmanager` is not installed, so the AVD `gracehush_test` was written by hand. Install Expo Go 58.x on it (the `Expo-Go-58.0.5.apk` from `expo/expo-go-releases`; the emulator still has 57.0.9, which cannot open the SDK 58 project, and installing over it keeps its data, which holds the user's own session: see the Step 17 trap) and drive it with `adb` (`uiautomator dump` for accessibility labels, `input tap`, `exec-out screencap`). Test at 320dp, 430dp, and font scale 1.3.
- iOS cannot be run or built here (no Mac), so `npx expo run:ios` never works and no prompt or reply should tell the user to run it. iPhone development builds will go through EAS in the last step (`prompts/35-ios-developer-account.md`, see Instructional Memory), where the interactive commands (`eas login`, `eas device:create`, the first `eas build`) are the user's to run. Say plainly that iOS was not verified, and ask the user to confirm on their iPhone in Expo Go.
- Sign-in needs the Clerk publishable key in `.env.local`: without it the app throws on launch by design, so nothing can be run on the emulator or the iPhone until it exists. Email sign-in runs in Expo Go. Google on Android is verified on the Android development build (step 05b). Apple, and Google on iOS, wait for step 35 and are reported as not verified on iOS. In Expo Go, Apple shows the calm "isn't available" message by design, and Google opens Clerk's browser flow. That flow can be driven on the emulator up to Google's own login page (Chrome shows its first-run screen on a fresh emulator: "Stay signed out"); never enter a real Google account there, so the sign-in itself is the user's check.
- The user often has their own `expo start` on port 8081. Run yours on 8082, check a process's command line before stopping it, and never stop theirs.
- Keep one-off scripts, screenshots, and exports in a temp folder outside the repo. Shut the emulator down (`adb emu kill`) and stop your own Metro when done.
- Their Metro hot-reloads your edits onto their device. Keep any temporary experiment behavior-neutral for iOS, mark it `TEMP-EXPERIMENT`, and revert it before finishing.
- Permissions on the emulator: `adb shell pm clear host.exp.exponent` resets Expo Go (it then shows its first-run developer-menu hint again: tap Continue, then close the menu), then `pm revoke` or `pm grant` `android.permission.RECORD_AUDIO` and `android.permission.POST_NOTIFICATIONS` for the state to test, and read the result with `dumpsys package host.exp.exponent`. The OS microphone prompt can be driven through `uiautomator dump` (its buttons are "While using the app", "Only this time" and "Don’t allow", with a typographic apostrophe). Expo Go on Android also asks its own per-project confirmation ("… You've already granted permission to another Expo experience. Allow gracehush to also use it?", DENY or ALLOW) the first time a project uses a permission that is already granted to Expo Go, so a granted permission can still show a dialog there. It is not an OS prompt and a development build never shows it. Notification prompts cannot be tested in Expo Go on Android at all (see the `expo-notifications` SDK note).
- uiautomator dumps XML-escape labels (`Allow &amp; Continue`), and `adb shell input text` can double the first character typed into a freshly focused field: check the field, then fix it with Home (`input keyevent 122`) and Forward-Delete (`input keyevent 112`).
- Android navigation modes (step 08): `adb shell cmd overlay enable-exclusive --category com.android.internal.systemui.navbar.threebutton` switches to 3-button navigation, and `--category com.android.internal.systemui.navbar.gestural` switches back. `adb shell cmd overlay list --user 0` shows which navbar overlay is `[x]`. The `navigation_mode` setting does not update on this emulator image (it keeps reading 2), so confirm the mode with a screenshot or the overlay list. The bar's bottom inset is 24dp in gestural mode and 48dp in 3-button mode.
- Dark mode (step 08): `adb shell cmd uimode night yes` and `night no` switch the system theme (`cmd uimode night` prints the current one). The app restarts on each change, and on this machine it can take a minute to come back.
- Expo Go's floating "Tools" button sits at the top-right and covers whatever is under it (the header avatar), so taps there miss. `input swipe` and `draganddrop` do not move it. Press, hold and drag with raw events in one shell call: `adb shell "input motionevent DOWN 971 239; sleep 1; input motionevent MOVE 975 500; input motionevent MOVE 980 900; input motionevent MOVE 985 1300; input motionevent MOVE 990 1500; input motionevent UP 990 1500"` (1080x2400 screen). It returns to the top-right after a cold start, and it belongs to Expo Go, not the app.
- `uiautomator dump` also lists views TalkBack ignores (opacity-0 layers and `no-hide-descendants` children), so to see what an accessibility service sees, use `uiautomator dump --compressed`. Android reports `uppercase` text already uppercased (`DEVELOPER`), so match labels case-insensitively. A screenshot can take 7 to 10 seconds while Metro and the emulator are busy: to catch a short animation mid-way, slow it down with a temporary `TEMP-EXPERIMENT` change that is Android-only, then compare pixel colors across frames with `pngjs`, instead of trying to time the capture.
- Setting the clock (step 09 review): to test anything that depends on the time of day, set the emulator's clock, not its time zone. A running Hermes process ignores a zone change (`cmd alarm set-timezone` changes `date`, but the app keeps its old zone until it restarts), while a clock change reaches `Date.now()` at once. Run `adb shell settings put global auto_time 0`, then `adb shell cmd alarm set-time <epoch ms>` (compute it in Node, for example `Date.UTC(y, m, d, 7, 5, 0)` for 08:05 in UTC+1), send the app to the background (`input keyevent 3`) and bring it back (`am start -a android.intent.action.VIEW -d exp://127.0.0.1:8082 host.exp.exponent`): the `AppState` listener fires.
