**Step 11 follow-up — four fixes and three decisions (2026-10-20).**

- Decisions (the user approved all the professional recommendations made after the step 11 report): (1) `SegmentedControl`'s item `View` gets `collapsable={false}`: one prop, a bug fix in a component the step 11 prompt said not to touch (see the vendor note). (2) Scripture is quoted by `quoteVerse`, which balances the verse's own curly double quotes and never edits the verse (Screen Specifications, 5). The first idea, adding only the mark a verse lacks at each end, was dropped: a verse that ends with its own inner quote would still end `””`. The rule counts the marks with a depth scan instead. (3) `useReduceMotion` is one app-wide answer (vendor note). (4) `rosewood` is `#96555D` (colour table). The first proposal, `#9B5860`, was replaced for headroom (4.56 on blush against 4.81). Decided without code: (5) the large-text wrap of the tab labels ("Affirmations" at 360dp and all three labels at 320dp, both at font scale 1.3) waits for step 14, which reuses `SegmentedControl` with count badges: one adaptive layout there (stack the tabs at large text) fixes both, and nothing is clipped or lost meanwhile; (6) the temporary crisis view needs no action: no build leaves the user's machines before step 13 (the iOS account is last), so keep step 13 right after step 12 and do not reorder; (7) no "your reflection is ready" notification in V1 (Notification Rules).
- Changed: `src/components/SegmentedControl.tsx` (the prop, with a comment), `src/hooks/useReduceMotion.ts` (rewritten, same signature), `src/utils/format.ts` (`quoteVerse`), `src/components/ScriptureCard.tsx` (uses it), `src/screens/journal/ReflectionScreen.tsx` (the share message uses it), `src/constants/copy.ts` (`share.message` now takes the quoted verse, and a comment on `quote`), `global.css` and `src/constants/theme.ts` (`rosewood`), the three comments that cited "rosewood on blush is 4.41:1" (`EmailSheet.tsx`, `JournalFilterSheet.tsx`, `SpeakSection.tsx`) and AGENTS.md. Nothing was installed (`package.json` and the lockfile are untouched).
- Verified: `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npx expo install --check` (up to date) and `expo export` for Android and iOS (once unminified with `-c`, once plain on the final code) exit 0. On the Android emulator (Expo Go 57.0.9, 390dp, Metro on 8082): the Reflection tabs show a round pill after Affirmations, after Closing Prayer and back on Devotional, and New Entry's toggle after Write and back on Speak (item bounds unchanged: 102.6×44.1 in a 52.0 track, and 77.7×26.0 and 73.3×26.0); Matthew 11:28–30 shows one pair and its share text (read from Android's share sheet) ends `light.”` then "— Matthew 11:28–30 (BSB)" then "Shared from Gracehush"; Philippians 4:6–7 is unchanged; the rendered text of the date (on linen), the scripture reference (on blush) and the pill label (on the translucent pill) is exactly 150,85,93, which is `#96555D`; `useReduceMotion` was measured with temporary logs in `FadeIn` and in the hook (details in the vendor note), all reverted and compared with `cmp` (a search finds no `TEMP-EXPERIMENT` left). **Step 12 — Now Playing sheet and the shared player store (2026-10-20).**

- Decisions (from the step prompt, none asked again): nothing was installed (`package.json` and the lockfile are untouched; `expo install --check` is up to date). `#view-nowplaying` in `prompt_material/05-reflection.html` is the design source, and tokens win over it. Speed is three chips (0.8x, 1x, 1.2x) and ambient is a chip row that starts with "Off". **Load on action, not on view**: nothing loads until the user plays on the mini player or opens Now Playing. `load(track, ambient)` is synchronous with the profile passed in, `stop()` is a full reset, speed and ambient survive another track, and the clock is wall time times the rate. Haptics: `selection` for a changed speed or ambient chip only. The scrubber has a thumb. The artwork has no text. There is no mini player outside Reflection in V1 (a global one is a new feature: ask first). The footer line is development-only. Nothing on the screen animates by itself. Duration field: `Devotional.durationMs` everywhere, never a hardcoded length.

- Icons (iOS / Android, all through `Icon`): close `chevron.down` / `keyboard_arrow_down`; play `play.fill` / `play_arrow`; pause `pause.fill` / `pause`; replay `arrow.counterclockwise` / `replay`; back 15 `gobackward.15` / `replay` and forward 15 `goforward.15` / `replay` mirrored (Android draws the 15 as a 10px `Text` inside the arrow, with `allowFontScaling={false}`); ambient `waveform` / `graphic_eq`; quieter `speaker.fill` / `volume_down`; louder `speaker.wave.3.fill` / `volume_up`. No name differs from the prompt, and `satisfies SymbolViewProps["name"]` type-checks them.
- **Beyond the prompt's text (each found while building or on the emulator):** (1) the page does not scroll while a finger is on a scrubber (`scrollEnabled`, see the vendor note): the responder props alone let Android's `ScrollView` take a drag that drifts about 8dp vertically. (2) `needsOffscreenAlphaCompositing` on the dimmed volume row (see the vendor note): the thumb showed the fill's end through it. (3) The draft store was hardened: `play()` is a no-op while playing (a redundant play restarted the interval and lost up to 250 ms), `seekTo` ignores an idle player and a non-finite value (the draft turned an idle player into "ended"), and a seek while playing resets the tick reference (the time before the seek was counted twice). (4) The ambient chips are labelled "Ambient sound, Soft Rain" for screen readers (the prompt said only "labelled from `copy.ambient.labels`", and "Off" alone is ambiguous). (5) `getPlaybackTrack` also rejects a non-finite duration. (6) The error view's message ("We couldn’t load this devotional.") and the "Close" text link under "Try again" were not in the prompt's strings.
- Differences from the HTML, all kept: no blurred circles, white 95% circle or "Gracehush Sanctuary" label (white on the gradient is 3.36:1 there), and the 2-stop `gradients.brand`; the logo sits in the Onboarding frosted tile scaled to 96; the title is 24 Medium `plum-deep` (HTML regular `plum`) with "Gracehush • reference" under it (HTML: the reference only); the scrubber has a 16dp thumb and a 44dp touch area (HTML: an 8dp bar with no thumb); the skip icons are the plain 15 glyphs (the HTML's `replay_10` with a "15" under it shows two numbers); the unselected chips are `mauve` on white with a petal border (HTML `muted`, and a linen fill for the ambient ones); the ambient chips wrap (HTML scrolls them sideways); the Close row says "Close" (HTML "Close sheet") and its handle is iOS-only and centered (HTML: between the button and a spacer); `shadow-xs` on the card is skipped; times use `formatDuration` ("0:34", not `formatTimer`'s "00:34").

**Step 13 follow-up — premium pass on the crisis screens (2026-10-21).**

- Decisions (the user sent an iPhone screenshot of the crisis view, asked what would make it look more premium and modern, I recommended six changes with exact values and they answered "Goahead to Implement your professional recommendation"): (1) the four contact rows are one white list card with hairlines between them (a bordered box per row looked busy); (2) "Talk to someone now" sits directly under the top card and is 56 tall (`PrimaryButton size="large"`), so the one action is the second thing on the screen at every text size, and the list becomes "Ways to reach someone"; (3) the top card is calm blush with no red bar (a left bar reads as an error banner), a heart in a white 44dp circle and a Fraunces 22/30 headline (it was 19/26: the headline is the emotional centre); (4) the tiles are 40dp rounded squares (radius 12) with visible fills: crisis at **12%**, not the 14% I proposed, because 12% is the tint the chips already use (the icon on it measures 4.63:1), and `linen-deep` for the others; (5) the crisis header shows no date; (6) Support Resources groups its rows under "988 Suicide & Crisis Lifeline" (Call 988, Text 988, Chat online, each "Available 24/7") and "Other support", so the service name is not repeated three times. This **supersedes** the step 13 entry's layout (the red bar, the list before the button, a bordered box per row) and the Stitch design for those parts; crisis red is now on the heart, the 988 and 911 tiles and icons, the 911 detail line and the button.
- Found first: the screenshot showed none of the step 13 styles (no red bar, no tile fills, a small headline, a black 911 line): the user's Metro was started before the new files existed, and a hot reload does not recompile the stylesheet for class names that first appear in new files. **Restart Metro with `npx expo start -c` (and reopen Expo Go) after any step that adds new class names**; the emulator run uses a cleared cache and shows the intended design.

- Decisions (from the step prompt, none asked again): `prompt_material/06-prayers.html` is the design source (all three views) and tokens win over it; no month groups (one continuous timeline, one `FlatList` for both tabs); counts come from `getPrayerCounts` and `copy.prayers` builders; changes wait for the repository (about 500 ms, `TEMP(29)`) and are not optimistic, so a failed save keeps the typed note; `answeredDate` is a local `"YYYY-MM-DD"` and the mock stores 12:00 local on it; the Mark Answered sheet is a blush `BottomSheet` and the discard `ConfirmDialog` is rendered inside its children; Android's overflow menu is a `BottomSheet` list and iOS's is `ActionSheetIOS` (no red option); the user stays on Active after a save and a persistent banner offers "View in Answered"; no new haptic; no offline UI. No name differs from the prompt.
- **Differences from the prompt text, each found while building or on the emulator:** (1) The scripture reference is a `flex-1` text in a `min-w-[120px] flex-1` group, not the prompt's natural-width text: an italic `Text` sized by its own measure lost everything after its first word on Android (see the vendor note). (2) The `SegmentedControl` badge `View` has `collapsable={false}`, like the item, because it gains a background only when selected. (3) A stacked `SegmentedControl` track is `rounded-hero`: with the full pill radius the selected pill pokes out of a 100 to 140dp tall track. (4) On the timeline, the items after the first start their rail at their top (`top-0`) and the first at the node's centre (`top-4`), so the rail meets the one through the gap and nothing breaks 2dp above a node. (5) The usePrayers state also carries `counts` (`getPrayerCounts` of the loaded list), so the screen never counts for itself. (6) The spoken label of a card is one `copy.prayers.card.a11y(parts)` call that skips empty parts and does not add a full stop to a part that already ends in one (a quoted note ends in `.”`). The answered date and "After N days" are read as the plain texts of the timeline row above the card, not repeated in the card's label. (7) The note counter reads "950 / 1,000" like New Entry's, not "950 / 1000". (8) `Prayer from Oct 2` (`getPrayerTitle`) is a shared helper because the card, the sheet and the iOS action sheet all need it. (9) The repository's failure `message` for the mock is `copy.prayers.errors.save`, or `.date` for an invalid day, and the sheet picks its inline line by the result's code, as the prompt says.

**Step 15 — Profile & Settings (2026-10-21).**

- Decisions (from the step prompt, none asked again): `prompt_material/07-profile.html` is the design source (the settings view and both dialogs) and tokens win over it. The Pro upsell is nested in the free plan card; a trial or Pro user sees the plan state and "Manage subscription". Devotional Voice is not built (no data or function to choose). Daily Reminders and Ambient Soundscape expand in place, so there is no sub-screen and no picker inside a `BottomSheet`. The reminder times stay editable whatever the Daily Affirmations switch says and live in the onboarding store; the mock `getProfile()` returns them, so the Journal's current slot and Reflection's Affirmations tab follow an edit now. `updateProfile` takes `affirmationsEnabled`, `ambientSound` and `ambientVolume` only, and the switch and chips are optimistic with a rollback of only the fields that save touched. One dev flag, `nextPrayerMutationFails` (its button is now "Fail next save (dev)"), fails the next prayer change, profile save, export and delete. `getUsage()` is untouched. The ambient default applies to the next devotional: Profile calls `reseedAmbient()` after a successful save and never `setAmbient` or `setAmbientVolume`. Sign out, export and both delete steps are one `ConfirmDialog` instance (the content follows the step, the visible flag is separate); a failure stays inside it and the confirm button is the retry. The export copy promises no PDF, email or encryption. About gains Privacy Policy and Terms of Use rows (the stores expect both in the app), and the support row is "Support & Helpline Resources" in plum. Crisis red appears on the Delete Account row and in the two delete steps only. Offline Reflections shows real counts (`getSyncSummary`). `selection` haptics for the Daily Affirmations switch and for a changed ambient chip, and none for anything else here. The name line is the full name, then the first name from the email, then "Friend", and never an email. Loading is a skeleton in the shape of the sections; the error state still shows what needs no profile data (the account header, About, the footer and Sign Out). No toasts: export and restore use persistent inline lines. Nothing was installed (`package.json` and the lockfile are untouched).

- **Differences from the prompt text, each found while building or on the emulator:** (1) `ConfirmDialog`'s card is capped at 90% of the window height and scrolls inside that: the Pro delete dialog is taller than a 320dp, font scale 1.3 window, and as a centered card it lost its icon and title off the top. (2) The ambient volume row wraps: at 320dp and font scale 1.3 the track between two icons and the "Vol 50%" readout was 73dp wide, so the wrapper is `min-w-[96px] flex-1` and the readout drops under it (the track is 150dp). (3) The reminders summary joins each time to its AM or PM with a non-breaking space; it wrapped as "Afternoon 3:00 / PM" at 320dp. (4) The Offline Reflections tile is a plain cloud, because a check-marked cloud beside a "pending" icon would contradict it; the trailing icon carries the state. (5) The Privacy Policy and Terms of Use rows have a leading `lock` and `description` icon (the prompt named only the trailing external mark). (6) Moving from delete step 1 to step 2 clears the dialog's error, so a failed "Manage subscription" link from step 1 does not follow the user to the final confirmation. (7) The notice lines: a new "Restore purchases" or "Manage subscription" replaces the other's line, every notice clears when the tab loses focus, and a successful save clears the two save lines. (8) `simulateWrite` is exported, because `services/account.ts` waits with it too. (9) The two `TODO(24)` markers the prompt asks for (the outbox count, the sign-out body) were missing after the build and were added in the final review. (10) `ProfileScreen.tsx` is about 800 lines even with the two panels moved out, past the prompt's 450-line guide: the rest are the private pieces the prompt lists (notices, header, loading and error views, notification card, delete extras, footer, dialog logic).

**Step 16 — Paywall UI (2026-10-22).**

- Decisions (from the step prompt, none asked again): `prompt_material/08-paywall.html` is the design source (one view, no hidden states) and tokens win over it. The Paywall lists the one Pro benefit AGENTS.md defines, unlimited audio devotionals, from `copy.paywall.features` (one row per entry; the free count comes from `FREE_DEVOTIONALS_PER_WEEK`). The HTML's other four rows, its glow and its "No commitments. Cancel with one tap." line are not built, and the old draft's "You haven't been charged." is not claimed either (a failed purchase points to Restore purchases). Every price, savings percent, per-month price, trial length and renewal line comes from the offering or from `utils/pricing.ts`: the chip shows only when the prices support a saving, and a trial is advertised only when the package has one and the store confirmed this user is eligible. Close is never disabled, not even during a purchase; Restore, the plan options and the button are. Crisis support is never paywalled (the screen loads the entry in the same `Promise.all` as the offering and replaces itself with Reflection). The footer is pinned only in a window at least 700dp tall at a font scale of at most 1.3. The purchase service is a mock that fails closed. A `selection` haptic fires only when the selected plan changes. Nothing was installed (`package.json` and the lockfile are untouched).
- Added: `src/components/paywall/PlanOption.tsx` and `PaywallFooter.tsx` (extracted because the screen passed 300 lines), `src/hooks/usePaywall.ts`, `src/services/purchases.ts` (`TEMP(30)`), `src/data/mock/offerings.ts`, `src/utils/pricing.ts`; the paywall types in `types/index.ts`; `copy.paywall` (and a shared `daysLabel` at the top of `copy.ts`); `MockPurchaseOutcome` and `takeMockPurchaseOutcome` in `scenario.ts`; four dev-store fields; `getMockSubscriptionTier` in the repository; and the "Paywall (dev)" group in `DevToolsPanel`.

**Step 16 follow-up — pinned block, button-label breaks, release gate and launch pricing (2026-10-22).**

- Decisions (the user pasted the open items from my step 16 report and asked for a professional recommendation on each; I answered item by item, and they replied "Implement all the professional recommendations"): (1) The pinned block holds only the button and its renewal terms; "Maybe later" and the Terms and Privacy row are the last rows of the scrolling page. This **supersedes** the step 16 entry's footer (the button, the terms and the links as one pinned block); the pin rule itself (a window at least 700dp tall, font scale at most 1.3) is unchanged. (2) The long label is fixed with line-break strategies on the shared label (`textBreakStrategy="balanced"`, `lineBreakStrategyIOS="push-out"`). **This revises my own earlier suggestion**: the word joiner (U+2060) is dropped, because an invisible character inside an accessibility label cannot be checked on iOS here, and "per year" for "/year" is 25.5dp wider (215.1 against 189.6dp, from the DM Sans file), which would wrap labels that fit today. (3) The Privacy Policy, Terms and support address stay the user's to provide; `docs/legal-brief.md` holds the facts for them. React Native 0.86 has `experimental_accessibilityOrder`, which is experimental and leaves out anything not listed, so it was not used; and I did not add a screen-reader switch that unpins the block, because it would give screen-reader users a different layout from everyone else and cannot be checked on iOS here. The cost is two or three more swipes between the plans and the button for a screen-reader user. (c) My recommendation said the first view would show both plans at 845dp: it does for the profile source, but at 360dp wide (800dp tall) it shows the Yearly card whole and 35.3dp of Monthly, and while a purchase notice is showing (about 50dp) it shows 58.1dp of Monthly even at 390dp. (d) My report said a 360dp phone at font scale 1.3 with a used trial was the realistic wrap case: the label fits there by 1.3dp (244.7dp of text in a 246dp slot), so what wraps is a wider price or a narrower window, such as the 320dp case. (e) iOS's `push-out` avoids a lone word on the last line and does not balance the lines like Android's strategy, so the iPhone's wrap may differ from the one measured here.

**Step 17 — Supabase and Clerk (2026-10-22).**

- Decisions (from the step prompt, approved in its review, none asked again): (1) Supabase Third-Party Auth with Clerk: the app sends Clerk's normal session token (`getToken()` with no template), so no JWT template exists and no secret is shared. (2) One package, `@supabase/supabase-js`. (3) The base schema is applied by hand, once, by the user (`supabase/schema.sql`, then `supabase/selftest.sql`); this step ran no SQL, used no management token and ran no `npx clerk config patch`. (4) The app can never write the plan: the grants are per column and the typed `Database` has no `subscription_tier` or `created_at` in its insert and update types. (5) The profile is local-first: only `profileSync` talks to the server (at sign-in, on foreground and 800 ms after a reminder edit) and `getProfile()` reads what the device already knows. (6) A development build with no Supabase values runs on the mock profile; a wrong value always stops the app and a missing one stops a release build. (7) The client and the error mapper are `src/services/supabaseClient.ts` and `src/utils/supabaseErrors.ts` (AGENTS.md has no `src/lib/`). (8) `getSupabase()` is `null` while nobody is signed in and the token callback throws `NoSessionError`, so no anonymous request can leave the app. (9) `db: { retry: false }`, so an offline read fails at once. (10) An unsent reminder edit belongs to one account: sign-out drops it, the sync never runs for a user who changed while a request was away, and the settings kept on the device are keyed by Clerk user id. (11) The row types are `type` aliases, and the splash also waits for the new prefs store.
- Installed with `npx expo install`: `@supabase/supabase-js` 2.118.0 (one line in `package.json`; the lockfile gained 8 packages and no existing entry changed: `postgrest-js`, `auth-js`, `realtime-js`, `storage-js` and `functions-js` at 2.118.0, `phoenix` 0.4.5 and `iceberg-js`). No `react-native-url-polyfill` was added: `@clerk/expo` already depends on it (4.0.0) and imports its `/auto` entry, so the `URL` globals on a device come from it, which is also what Supabase's Expo quickstart documents.
- Added: `src/utils/jwt.ts`, `src/constants/env.ts`, `src/utils/supabaseErrors.ts`, `src/services/supabaseClient.ts`, `src/data/profileRemote.ts`, `src/services/profileSync.ts`, `src/store/profilePrefsStore.ts` and `src/components/SupabaseAuthBridge.tsx`. They are the review's text: seven were byte-identical to the harness-proven drafts, and `supabaseClient.ts` differs from its draft only by the `TODO(19)` comment the prompt added (and the reword in difference 1).
- Changed: `App.tsx` (the splash also waits for the prefs store, `SupabaseAuthBridge` is mounted, and the start-up check), `.env.example` (two names), `src/types/index.ts` (`RepoResult` has `UNAUTHORIZED`; the `ProfilePatch` comment), `src/types/database.ts` (`UserProfileRow` is a `type` alias, plus `UserProfileInsert`, `UserProfileUpdate` and `Database`), `src/store/onboardingStore.ts` (version 2), `src/store/devStore.ts` and `src/data/mock/scenario.ts` (`profileSource`, `getProfileSource`), `src/data/journalRepository.ts` (`getProfile` and `updateProfile`; CRLF kept, 346 lines), `src/services/account.ts`, `src/components/dev/DevToolsPanel.tsx` (the profile source buttons and the Supabase check; CRLF kept, 534 lines), `src/screens/profile/ProfileScreen.tsx` (the `TODO(17)` comment only), `package.json` and the lockfile. No screen, hook, copy string or layout changed, and no migration, storage policy, Edge Function or dashboard setting was touched.

(2) The review's draft of `src/types/index.ts` had a `RepoFailureCode` alias that nothing uses and left the `ProfilePatch` comment alone; the prompt's text (the inline union and the new comment) was followed instead. (4) `npx expo install --check` and `npx expo-doctor` are 20 of 21, not clean: Expo's server now expects `expo` ~57.0.31, `@expo/ui` ~57.0.26 and `expo-router` ~57.0.29 (installed: 57.0.30, 57.0.25 and 57.0.28). This step did not cause it (the lockfile diff only adds the eight packages), and an upgrade is outside "one new dependency", so it was not upgraded then (the user approved it later: see the last update of this entry).
- Measured on this machine with the installed packages (a fetch that always rejects, one read through the client): **7.0 s and 4 attempts** with the library's default retry, **0.0 s and 1 attempt** with `db: { retry: false }`. Android dev bundle 10 s with a warm cache (13.2 MB, 2,081 modules); `expo export` 150 s for Android, 62 s for iOS and 30 s for Android unminified. **Not measured:** the sign-in to Journal time and Profile's time offline with the real client (both need the Supabase project).
- Read in the installed source (`SupabaseClient.ts`, `lib/fetch.ts`): the client calls the token callback once when it is created, and a rejection there only logs `console.warn('Failed to set initial Realtime auth token:', e)` (a development LogBox toast; nothing is sent); `omitApiKeyAsBearer` is set only on the Edge Functions fetch, while the REST fetch still falls back to the API key as the bearer when the callback returns `null`, which is why this app's callback throws (it matters for `supabase.functions.invoke`, if a later step uses it); `atob`, which the dev panel's token check needs, is installed on Hermes by `@clerk/expo`'s base64 polyfill.
- Verified: `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` exit 0 on the final code. The typing proof of section 3: a file with the four lines under `@ts-expect-error` compiles with the valid writes beside them, and with the directives stripped it fails with exactly the four expected errors (`subscription_tier` is not a known update property, `created_at` is typed `never`, the insert needs an `id`, and `journal_entries` is not a table); the proof files are deleted. `expo export` for Android (plain), iOS and Android unminified exit 0 (run before one comment-only edit, which cannot change a bundle); the unminified bundle holds `X-Client-Info` 5 times, no `process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE*` lookup is left in it and with no values both constants compile to `""`. **Step 18 — Schema migration (2026-10-22).**

- Decisions (from the step prompt, approved with it; none asked again): (1) No Docker, no CLI link, no database password: the SQL was proven offline in PGlite, the user applied it in the dashboard's SQL editor and the self-tests judged it there; the implementer ran no SQL against the hosted project. (2) The v1 migration creates `journal_entries`, `daily_affirmations`, the private `journal_media` bucket and five `users_profile` columns; nothing is backfilled and there is no `devotional_audio_url`. (3) The app cannot insert or delete an entry or an affirmation (the Edge Function does, as `service_role`); it updates only the prayer columns and `is_favorite`. (4) Explicit grants for every role, column by column, safe under either setting of "Automatically expose new tables". (5) Rules live in the tables: a crisis entry carries no devotional, audio, scripture or prayer; a prayer is answered on a date and only then, its note at most 1,000 characters; verified scripture has its reference and text; one affirmation per slot, owned by the entry's user, and none for a crisis entry; `client_entry_id` is unique per user. (6) `journal_media` is private, 25 MB, audio types; the app uploads only to `{userId}/raw/{clientEntryId}.m4a` (`upsert: false`) and the server writes `{userId}/devotionals/`. (7) The generated types are never hand-edited; `database.ts` re-exports the rows and narrows what the app may send. (8) The settings sync: the server column is the truth; the phone keeps `snapshot` and `unsent` per user, an unsent edit wins and otherwise the server wins, one request carries the reminder times, the settings and the zone, and version 1's `prefs` migrate to `unsent`. (9) The time zone is sent when the row is created and whenever it differs, is never shown and has no unsent state. (10) `getProfile()` opens when the first sync read the row but failed to send something. (11) Three renames: `scheduleProfileSync`, `cancelProfileSync`, `watchReminderEdits`. (12) The checks stay in the repo under `supabase/tests/`. (13) No `types:db` script.

**Step 21 — Crisis screening (2026-10-23).**

- Decisions (the user's "Implement it" came with a prompt that had not been through the review pass, so the reconciliation with the code and with the live API was done here, and the differences are listed below): (1) The server decides whether an entry is a crisis before it makes anything. The order is: claim, read the recording, **screen**, then (step 22) the free-tier limit, then the reflection from the screened words alone, then the passage, the save and the affirmations. The flow is in `handler.ts` (the prompt says `index.ts`, which only serves). (2) Three layers, each able to decide alone: a phrase backstop, a screening call, and the reflection's own `is_crisis` flag plus a safety block on the reflection request. `uncertain` and a block are crises, a crisis outranks a contradicting `no_speech`, and a screening that cannot be had means no reflection (the entry is failed with `AI_UNAVAILABLE` and can be sent again). (3) A recording goes to the screening once, which transcribes it and judges the words in one reply; the reflection only ever sees text, so its prompt and schema lost the audio, transcript and `no_speech` parts (`PROMPT_VERSION` is `gen-v1.2`; the screening is `screen-v1`). (4) `CONTENT_BLOCKED` no longer exists: a block is a crisis, never an error shown to the person (removed from `_shared/errors.ts`, both contract copies, `response.ts` and `mappers.ts`; no migration, because `error_code` has no check constraint). (5) The five adjustable safety filters are all `off` for the screening only (the real API accepted exactly those five). (6) The screening model is `GEMINI_SCREENING_MODEL`, else `GEMINI_MODEL` (`gemini-3.1-flash-lite`); the user may set a separate one later. (7) The request log gains `screen`, `screenMs`, `screenAttempts` and `crisisSource` (one word each). (8) Nothing was deployed, no secret changed, no migration, no UI change, no package.
- Added: `supabase/functions/_shared/safety/` with `phrases.ts` (18 templates that expand to 105 phrases, a normalizer, 44 exempt idioms), `screenPrompt.ts` (`screen-v1`), `screen.ts` (`screenEntry`, `askModel`, `interpret`, `parseScreening`, the schema, the five settings), `eval/cases.json` (30 made-up entries), `eval/run_eval.ts` (by hand, real key) and the tests `phrases_test.ts`, `screen_test.ts` and `eval/cases_test.ts`.
- Changed: `process-entry/handler.ts` (the new flow; the step 19 safety net and its `TODO(21)` are gone; the `TODO(22)` moved to after the screening), `gemini.ts` (`GeminiBlocked` instead of `CONTENT_BLOCKED`, optional `safetySettings` and `attemptTimeoutMs`), `prompt.ts`, `schema.ts` and `output.ts` (text only), `lifecycle.ts` (`saveCrisisEntry` fails as `crisis_save_failed`), `scripture.ts` (a blocked follow-up means no passage), `contracts.ts`, `response.ts`, `src/types/index.ts` (CRLF kept) and `src/data/mappers.ts`, `_shared/errors.ts`, `_shared/log.ts`, `confirm-scripture/handler.ts` (the four new log fields, empty), `live_check.ts` (screen, then reflect), `testing/fakes.ts` (a separate screening queue that answers "clear" when empty, and a call-order record), the tests of every file above, `.env.example`, and `src/components/dev/processEntryCheck.ts` (a second crisis sentence that only the model can find). Deleted: nothing.
- Differences from the prompt text, each with its reason: (1) `index.ts` is `handler.ts`. (2) The prompt's `safetySettings`, `promptFeedback` and `finishReason` belong to `generateContent`; the Interactions API has `safety_settings`, lowercase values, and a block is an HTTP 400 with a code (see the Gemini safety note), so the "blockReason or finishReason" check is `isBlockedBody`, which step 19 already had. (3) "Temperature 0" was not applied: AGENTS.md and a test forbid sampling parameters, and the docs advise against them on Gemini 3.x. (4) `screenEntry(input, config)` takes the key, model, thinking level, deadline and stats as a second argument, because nothing in `_shared` reads the environment; a recording is `{ kind: "audio", bytes }` (the transport base64-encodes it and always sends `audio/m4a`), not `base64` plus a mime type; and `failed` carries a log-safe `reason` so a bad model name and a timeout can be told apart. (5) No `FORCE_GENERATION_CRISIS` switch and no `functions serve` (there is no Docker, and step 20 decided against environment switches in production code): case g is a Deno test through the fake world, so there is no development switch to guard. (6) The phrase list is 18 templates (a group such as `(kill|hurt|harm)` stands for its alternatives) that expand to 105 phrases, with contractions spelled out and filler words dropped first, rather than 15 to 25 literal strings, and it has an exempt list for Christian speech; see Crisis Screening Rules. (7) The documented lint command only checked `process-entry`; it now names the three folders (see the Linting section). (8) The eval also prints seconds and the failure reason, asks the model alone about the cases the backstop decided, and takes `--only`, `--pause` and `--audio`. (9) The two markers the prompt asks for are in the comment of `saveCrisisEntry` (`TODO(22)`: crisis entries never count toward the free-tier limit, so the weekly count of step 22 must skip `is_crisis` rows; `TODO(28)`: never schedule affirmations for a crisis), and the limit's own `TODO(22)` in `handler.ts` says it goes after the screening.
- Real checks (2026-10-23, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, thinking low, a free-tier key, made-up text only). **Evaluation, two full runs of the 30 cases:** run 1 answered a crisis for 14 of the 15 crisis cases and had no answer for `crisis-10` (the free-tier model failed that call; it answered a crisis on the next run, and the run printed no reason yet); run 2 answered a crisis for **15 of 15**, with all 30 answered and **0 of 15 clear cases flagged** in both runs (the idioms, the past struggle and the Christian devotion case `clear-12` included). The six cases the backstop decided were also asked of the model alone and it said crisis five times and uncertain once (run 1), so the backstop is a net and not the only catch; the five uncertain cases came back as uncertain or crisis; the two injection cases (`crisis-08`, `crisis-09`) were flagged. A third partial run had `clear-01` fail with `gemini_http_503:service_unavailable` after about 40 s (two attempts of about 20 s each): the free-tier model under load, which the code answers as designed (no reflection, entry failed). **Recordings** (made-up speech from Windows' own voice, encoded to `.m4a` with ffmpeg, never a real person's voice): a clear prayer was clear (158 characters heard), an explicit sentence a crisis from the model (104 characters heard, exactly the sentence), a quiet one a crisis from `uncertain` (83, exact), 6 s of silence `no_speech`, and a 4 min 37 s recording (2.1 MiB, about the 5:00 maximum) was clear in 10.2 s with the whole text. `live_check.ts --audio` ran all seven cases through screening, reflection and the real Bible and every one behaved as expected (the instruction-injection entry still got a normal reflection). **Cost and time:** a screening call is about 420 to 500 tokens in, under 100 out and 100 to 200 thinking (about $0.0003); one full evaluation was 34 attempts, 13,822 tokens in, 1,032 out and 2,981 thinking (about $0.01). A clear written entry took 1.1 to 3.9 s (two of fifteen took about 10 s); an entry it flagged took 4.7 to 23 s (the model thinks longer about risk, and a total over 20 s, such as the 23 s case, means the first attempt failed or timed out and the retry answered). So a written reflection now starts a few seconds later than before and a flagged one can start 20 s later; the billed key should be timed again before step 25.
- Verified: `deno check` of both entry points, `run_eval.ts`, `live_check.ts` and the new tests, `deno lint` of the three folders (51 files, clean; a planted `var` in a new file was caught), `deno fmt --check` (62 files), **222 Deno tests pass** (171 before; the screening, phrase, eval-set, handler, gemini, lifecycle and scripture tests are new or changed), parity 7 of 7, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` exit 0 (the contract-parity type check passes with `CONTENT_BLOCKED` removed on both sides), the profile-sync harness 88 of 88, and the release gate still prints 17 lines. **Mutations:** 32 deliberate breakages of the safety logic in a scratch copy (a failed screening treated as clear, uncertain treated as clear, a block treated as a failure, no phrase check before the model or on a transcript, `no_speech` outranking a crisis, the settings not sent or one left on, the model's own flag ignored, a blocked reflection rethrown, a crisis keeping its affirmations or its title, the log carrying the person's words, a retried block, phrase matching inside words, fillers not dropped, the exempt idioms removed, "take my life" or "live in fear" made crisis phrases, and more), each run with `--no-check` so that only a test could catch it: **all 32 are caught by named tests and none survives** (one breakage, "take my life" as a crisis phrase, was first caught only by an unrelated check on the list's form, so it was redone and is now caught by the prayer test).

**Step 21 follow-up — Gemini under heavy demand (2026-10-23).**

- What happened: the user deployed `process-entry` (27 files, `--use-api`), four requests with no valid token answered 401, 401, 405 and 204 with the function's own bodies (the new build was live), and the phone check on the test account ended "10 passed, 4 FAILED" at about 14:56 UTC. The written entry (44.2 s), the quiet crisis (25.4 s) and the instructions entry (43.2 s) got `503 AI_UNAVAILABLE`, and "same id again" failed only because the first entry had not finished (its second request was processed again, which is the designed path for a failed entry). Every case that needs no model passed: the four refusals, the three `confirm-scripture` cases with the public-Bible match, the crisis that a phrase decided (1.3 s) and the recording (a retry of a finished entry, 1.4 s). The cause was Google's free tier and not the code (the vendor note on heavy demand has the probes): the function did what the rules say, no screening answer and so no reflection, and each entry was failed and can be sent again. What was too weak was the retry: one more try after a fixed 1.5 s, where Google asks for exponential backoff (step 19's open item (3) had already flagged it).
- Decisions (the user's "Fix all what need Fixs"; nothing about what counts as a crisis changed): (1) Retries as Google's troubleshooting page asks: up to four attempts per call, with about 2, 4 and 8 s between busy failures (a quarter either way), never a pause that leaves under 8 s, and an unusable reply asked for once more at once. (2) The screening ends 60 s after the request started (`screenTiming.deadlineMs`), so the reflection keeps 30 s of the 90. (3) An optional `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL`: every second attempt of the screening and of the reflection goes to it, and empty keeps every attempt on the first model. It screens entries, so it was evaluated like a screening model before being recommended. (4) Our own timeout is logged as `gemini_timeout` and a dropped connection as `gemini_network`. (5) The log line gains `fallbackAttempts` (23 fields). (6) The dev check skips "same id again" when the first entry did not finish, and explains `AI_UNAVAILABLE` in one line. Unchanged: the 20 s screening attempt, the 45 s reflection attempt, the 90 s deadline, the replacement passage's single attempt, the order of the flow, both prompts and the phrase list.
- Changed: `process-entry/gemini.ts` (`MAX_ATTEMPTS` 4, `MAX_INVALID_REPLIES` 2, `backoffMs`, `retryBackoffMs` 2,000, `fallbackModel`, `fallbackAttempts` in `GeminiStats`, `gemini_timeout`), `handler.ts` (the secret, the screening's own deadline, the fallback for both calls, the log field), `_shared/safety/screen.ts` (`ScreenConfig.fallbackModel`, `screenTiming.deadlineMs`), `_shared/log.ts`, `confirm-scripture/handler.ts` (the field, always 0), `live_check.ts` (it mirrors the function: the fallback and the screening's deadline), `_shared/safety/eval/run_eval.ts` (the screening's 60 s; it judges one model and never uses the fallback), `.env.example`, the tests of each, and `src/components/dev/processEntryCheck.ts`.
- The fallback, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (free key, made-up text only): evaluation run 1, on the old retry code, gave 14 of 15 crisis cases as a crisis with `uncertain-02` unanswered (two 20 s timeouts) and 0 of 15 clear cases flagged; run 2, on the new code, gave **15 of 15, 0 of 15, and all 30 answered** (`uncertain-02` came back uncertain, and `clear-10` took 56.0 s, which the old code would have failed). The model alone called all six phrase cases crises in both runs, and it spends no thinking tokens at `low`. `live_check.ts` with it as the only model: three reflections ready (169 to 178 words; Psalm 127:2, Psalm 103:2–3 and Psalm 116:7 verified), the instructions entry a normal reflection, the quiet crisis uncertain and the explicit one decided by the phrase list; its one replacement request got no answer from the free tier, and four direct tries of the same request then picked Philippians 4:6–7 each time (2.5 to 28.9 s). Price on the pricing page: $0.30 in and $2.50 out per million tokens against $0.25 and $1.50 for 3.1 Flash-Lite, paid only for the attempts that go to it.
- Verified: 233 Deno tests (222 before; the new ones cover the four attempts, the doubling and the jitter, a pause that leaves no time, an unusable reply asked for again at once, the shared attempt budget, the fallback's turn, model and request, no fallback or one that names the main model, the timeout reason, the screening's own deadline and the reflection keeping the request's), `deno check`, `deno lint` (51 files), `deno fmt --check` (62), parity 7 of 7, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` exit 0, the release gate 17 lines, and the secrets audit of every changed file. **Mutations:** a control run that must pass, then 20 breakages of the new rules in a scratch copy with `--no-check`: 19 caught by named tests; the 20th (a fallback equal to the main model counted as one) changed nothing, because the attempt is compared with the main model by name, so the redundant check it removed was taken out of the code and the four fallback mutations were run again (all caught).
- Hosted results (2026-10-23): the user deployed `process-entry` again and set `GEMINI_FALLBACK_MODEL=gemini-3.5-flash-lite` with `secrets set`. The upload list copied from the terminal showed 26 files and not `_shared/bible/hydrate.ts`, a copy artifact: that file is a static import, the function boots only with it, and it then verified two passages. Four requests with no valid token answered 401 (`auth:missing`), 401 (`auth:malformed`), 405 and 204 with the function's own bodies (2.4 s cold, then under 1 s). The phone check on the test account, 16:49 to 16:51 UTC, ended **"Done: 16 passed."** The free tier was still busy, and the log (every line with the 23 allowed fields and `promptVersion` `gen-v1.2`, no text, no token and no id but the request's, `user` e9dbd2d2) shows the fallback at work: the written entry was screened by the fallback at the second attempt (`screenAttempts` 2, `fallbackAttempts` 1, 22.6 s) and written by the main model at its first attempt (120 thinking tokens, which only 3.1 Flash-Lite spends), 30.1 s in all with its passage verified; the quiet crisis was found by the fallback after the main model failed (`crisisSource` uncertain, 482 tokens in, 41 out, no thinking, 24.3 s); and the instructions entry needed the fallback for the screening (39.5 s) and for the reflection (`attempts` 4, `fallbackAttempts` 2, no thinking tokens), 63.9 s in all with its passage verified. The crisis that a phrase decided took 0.9 s with no model call, the four refusals had their reasons (`text_short`, `text_long`, `audio_path`, `audio_base64`), and the same id and the recording were answered from their finished rows with no model call. So the main model failed four of its five first attempts in those two minutes and the fallback answered all four times it was asked; with the old code each of those four calls would have had one more try on the same busy model. `confirm-scripture` was not redeployed (it did not change) and verified an older reflection's 1 Peter 5:6–7 (148 characters, the same letters and marks as the public Bible).
- Not verified: which failure the main model gave each time (a 503 or our 20 s timeout: a request that ends well logs no reason for the attempts that failed); a billed key, and the paid tier under demand; the app waiting for a slow request (step 25: the slowest answer here was 63.9 s, the function can take about 95 s, and the dev check's timeout is 130 s).

(2) The replacement passage keeps a single attempt, so under heavy demand a reflection can be saved without a passage. (3) Step 25: the app's request timeout must outlast the function's slowest answer, and the New Entry error view should link to Support Resources (step 21's open item (2)). (4) Step 32 compares models with the fallback in mind. (5) Nothing of step 21 is committed yet, and the phone is still signed in as the test account.

**Google billing for Gemini (2026-10-23).**

- Where it stands: the user subscribed and reported the same day that the Google subscription is active. The user had set up a Cloud Billing account from AI Studio, and its $30 activation payment ("Postpay – Google Cloud Services") went through that evening: that payment is the account activation, which is why the key reads Tier 1, and it is not Gemini credits (AI Studio's Transactions list read "You don't have any transactions yet" and the Credit balance $0.00 an hour later). AI Studio's API Keys page now shows the gracehush key (the only key in the project, the same row that read "Set up billing, Free tier" earlier that day) on "My Billing Account", **Tier 1**, on Google's Prepay plan, where the Gemini API serves a request only while the credit balance is above $0.
- What the API said: while the credit balance was still empty, every model answered HTTP 402 `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, "Your prepayment credits are depleted" (probes at 21:39 and 21:43 UTC with made-up text, 0.3 s each, `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` and `gemini-3.8-flash`). That is the Prepay plan's own refusal, and it showed that the key was already billed. `gemini.ts` treats a 402 as final (no retry) and logs `gemini_http_402:RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, so an empty balance fails each entry at once with `AI_UNAVAILABLE`. At 21:43 UTC, minutes after the user's report, the key was still refused, and the AI Studio Billing page then showed **Credit balance $0.00** (plan Prepay, auto-reload off, "Paid 1" with a $250 tier cap, and the banner "A credit balance above $0 is required to resume service"): the billing account is active and the project is on the paid tier, but the Gemini credits had not been bought yet (they are bought with **Buy credits** on that page). The user then bought the Gemini credits (the Cloud console's Prepay card read **Credit balance $20.00**, "$20.00 added on Oct 23"), and at 22:37 UTC the first calls were served: 4 of 4 (HTTP 200), through the Interactions API for `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` and `gemini-3.8-flash` and through the older `generateContent` endpoint for the first, so the paid API is active. **Auto-reload was found On** (the page read "Auto-reload: On"), and the user switched it off at about 22:55 UTC (their report; a re-test at 22:56 UTC still served 4 of 4 calls): off is the setting while developing, so that no more can be spent than was added ($20), and before launch it goes back on with a monthly charge limit. The Gemini side of the billing is complete: the key is on the paid tier with $20 of credits and auto-reload off, and nothing else is open on it.
- Paid-tier numbers (2026-10-23, 22:37 to 22:49 UTC, made-up text only, thinking low, the function's real request bodies): **timing** (`probe503.ts`: three screening calls and one reflection per model, 12 of 12 served, none refused or busy): `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` screening 1.1 to 2.0 s and a reflection in 2.8 s (625 tokens in, 634 out, 116 thinking); `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` screening 0.7 to 1.1 s and a reflection in 2.8 s (no thinking tokens); `gemini-3.8-flash` screening 2.7 to 2.8 s and a reflection in 6.8 s (the same model took 63 to 77 s for a reflection on the free tier). **Screening evaluation** (`run_eval.ts`, 30 cases, every call answered on its first attempt): all three models gave 15 of 15 crisis cases as a crisis, 0 of 15 clear cases flagged and no case unanswered (`gemini-3.1-flash-lite` 37.8 s of model time, 14,800 tokens in, 1,111 out, 3,346 thinking; `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` 26.4 s, 1,117 out, no thinking; `gemini-3.8-flash` 81.3 s, 1,108 out, 2,047 thinking). **Cost at that day's paid prices**: a screening call about $0.0003 on `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`; a written reflection about $0.0013 on `gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, $0.0017 on `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` and $0.0026 on `gemini-3.8-flash` (until 2026-12-31, double after); the whole check, probes and the three evaluations, about five cents of the $20.
- What it changes: (1) a project with billing enabled is under Google's "Paid Services" terms, which do not use prompts and responses to improve its products (see the Gemini API terms note), so a real person's prayer may now reach the function and step 25 no longer waits on billing. (2) The timing and the screening evaluation on the paid tier are done (the numbers above), and no model or secret was changed: whether `gemini-3.8-flash` replaces `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` for the reflection is a choice for step 32 (the user's call, with the price and the speed above). (3) When the balance reaches $0 every key of the billing account stops at once, so before launch turn on auto-reload with a monthly charge limit and watch the balance. (4) Prepay credits pay for the Gemini API only: Cloud Text-to-Speech (step 22) is paid by the Cloud Billing account itself, which must be active and not a free trial (prompt 22's step A checks it). Checked on 2026-10-23 at about 22:20 UTC: the Cloud console's "How you pay" page shows the header "Paid account" and "You currently pay in 2 different ways": the "Postpay – Google Cloud Services" card holds the user's $30.00 payment as a credit ("No balance due", a $100 payment threshold, last payment Oct 23, manual), so Cloud Text-to-Speech has an active account behind it, and the "Prepay – AI Studio" card reads Credit balance $0.00 with **Buy credits**: the Gemini API still answers 402 until those credits are bought. (5) The billing account id, a card's digits and the Google Cloud project id stay out of every committed file. (6) The standing billing reminder is gone from Instructional Memory, because the user said billing is done.

**Step 22 — Spoken devotional and the free tier's limit (2026-10-23).**

- Decisions (the review's twelve, approved with the prompt and not changed by the build; (13) to (15) are the build's): (1) Cloud Text-to-Speech answers 401 to an API key, so the function signs in as a service account (`GOOGLE_TTS_CREDENTIALS_B64`; the token endpoint is a constant in the code and is never read from the key file). (2) Speech is optional, with three states (`readTtsConfig`: `ready`, `off` and `bad`, which names the secret), so the code, the limit and the deploy never wait for the billing: only the sound does, and it switches on with `secrets set` and no new deploy. (3) Only Chirp 3 HD voices, `markup` input and MP3 out; the language code comes from the voice name; the rate is `TTS_SPEAKING_RATE` or 0.92. (4) The script is `buildSpeechScript` of the saved row (the passage only when verified). (5) The answer carries a path and a duration and no link, and nothing stores a URL. (6) Speech runs after `ready` and is best effort: a failed call or upload sets `error_code` `TTS_FAILED`, a later success clears it, and the same request sent again runs speech only. (7) The limit is checked after the screening and before the reflection; over it the entry is saved failed with `LIMIT_REACHED`, a recording keeps the words that were heard in it, and the answer is a 402 with `entryId` and `usage`. (8) The week is Monday 00:00 on the profile's clock. (9) The speech budget is `speechTiming` and the log has 30 fields. (10) The developer check prints the plan and has the audio, retry and limit cases. (11) The known limit (the count is by creation time) is accepted. (12) No migration, package, switch in production code or `any`. **Build:** (13) The speech step is its own file, `process-entry/speech.ts`: it takes a job and gives an outcome back (it knows nothing of the request's context), never throws, and is tested on its own. (14) Once an entry is saved nothing marks it failed: `saved` is set right after `saveReadyEntry` and `saveCrisisEntry` and the `catch` skips `failEntry` then, so a database that fails while the answer is built leaves a good reflection (or a saved crisis) as it was. (15) The upload may end at most 5 s after the speech deadline.
- Added: `process-entry/speech.ts` (`speechStage`, `uploadCapMs`) and `speech_test.ts` (7 tests), `_shared/limits/checkLimit_test.ts` (21), `_shared/errors_test.ts` (3) and `_shared/tts/audition.ts`. The review's files (`_shared/tts/*`, the week code in `_shared/limits/`, `spokenReference`) were found unchanged and are not repeated here.
- Changed: `process-entry/handler.ts` (the flow of the prompt's section 2.1), `lifecycle.ts` (`readProfile`, `saveTranscript`, `needsSpeech`, `saveAudio` and `markSpeechFailed`; the two `TODO(22)` comments are gone), `constants.ts` (`speechTiming`), `contracts.ts` (`LimitUsage` and `ProcessEntryLimitError`), `_shared/errors.ts` (`AppError.details`, which `errorResponse` puts beside `error`), `_shared/log.ts` (seven fields, 30 in all), `_shared/limits/weekly.ts` (`checkDevotionalLimit`), `confirm-scripture/handler.ts` (the seven fields, empty), `process-entry/live_check.ts` (`--speak out.mp3`), `testing/fakes.ts`, `handler_test.ts` (22 new tests: a to u of the prompt, with the plans and the zones), `lifecycle_test.ts` (6 new) and both log-field tests; in the app `src/types/index.ts` (the two types, and the comment above `ProcessEntryResponse` no longer promises a signed URL), `src/types/contractParity.ts` (two lines), `src/components/dev/processEntryCheck.ts` (the plan line, the audio cases, the retry and the limit cases) and the markers (`NewEntryScreen.tsx`: `TODO(25)` for the 402's `entryId`; `PaywallScreen.tsx`: a plain statement that the server screens before it counts). AGENTS.md and its copy. No package, migration, secret, policy or screen changed. The fake world now answers Google's token endpoint and Text-to-Speech (a real RSA key and a real MP3 of 5,064 ms), takes a Storage upload as the real bucket does (binary, the type list, the 25 MiB limit, a duplicate without `x-upsert` refused), knows `neq`, `gt`, `lte`, `is` and `not.` filters, and keeps a strictly increasing database clock: the order of two entries made together is their creation time, and the real database never hands out one time twice.
- Differences from the prompt text, each with its reason: (1) **The time budget.** The prompt says the answer is sent before 150 s, but speech may end at 135 s and its upload was capped at 20 s after that, which is 155 s. The upload is now capped at the smaller of 20 s and 5 s past the speech deadline (`uploadCapMs`, tested, and mutation 22 is caught), so the worst case is about 145 s. (2) **Case p** is tested on `checkDevotionalLimit` itself with a pinned clock (`checkLimit_test.ts`: 15 scenarios, the tiers, the third-or-fourth rule, two zones, a bad zone and a failing query), not through the handler; the handler tests j, m, o, q and r cover the same rules end to end, and `weekly_test.ts` stays about the arithmetic of the week. (3) **Case u, the crisis arm.** After `saveCrisisEntry` the handler makes no database call (a crisis answer is built from the row and has no affirmations to read), so no database failure can reach the flag there: `saved u2` proves the handler needs no database after a crisis is saved, and `saved u` (a failing affirmations read after a reflection and its audio were saved) proves the flag. The flag is set after the crisis save as well, for code added later. (4) `checkDevotionalLimit` takes an optional fifth argument, `now`, so that the tests pin the clock; the four-argument call that prompt 30 names still works. (5) `saveAudio` returns the row or null, and `markSpeechFailed` returns the row it marked or null (so that the answer says `TTS_FAILED`); the prompt gave neither a return value. (6) `ttsChars` is the script's characters only for a call that worked (what Google bills for sure) and 0 after a failure. (7) A speech-only retry with speech off or half-set is answered as it is and logs `tts` null: only a request that runs the step logs `off` or `bad_config`. (8) The check's closing line counts skips (`Done: 14 passed, 3 skipped.`) and reads as before when none is skipped. (9) Test names carry words (`speech b)`, `limit j) m)`, `log s)`, `saved u)`) because the file already uses the letters a to m for other cases.
- Real checks (2026-10-23; no Google Text-to-Speech credential existed, and the Gemini key's prepaid credits were empty until about 22:37 UTC): the price page was read again in the build and matches the prompt's section 2.8 (Chirp 3 HD: 0 to 1 million characters free a month, then $30 per million; billing must be enabled; spaces, newlines and tags are counted). `audition.ts` was run for real against a fake Google that answers the token endpoint, the voice list and a synthesize call: with `--voices Aoede,Leda,Achernar --rates 0.88,0.95` it found two of the three voices (Achernar was left out of the fake's list and was skipped), made four files named `Aoede-0.88.mp3` and so on with one sign-in shared by the four calls, the markup held the reading ("A reading from Psalm 127, verse 2.") and ended in "Amen.", and `--one` (a full name or the last word), a rate outside 0.25 to 2.0 and a missing key each ended with exit 2 and a plain message; its sample script is 1,445 characters. `live_check.ts --speak` was run against the fake world (a fake Gemini and Google, the real Bible chapters): speech on gave one token request, one synthesize call and `1589 characters (with the passage), 20492 bytes, 5.1 s long`, speech off printed "not set up". **The real Gemini API** answered HTTP 402 `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` ("prepayment credits are depleted") to every model call until the user's credits arrived (`live_check.ts` got it, and a one-word probe still did at 21:49 UTC; see the "Google billing for Gemini" entry above). Then `live_check.ts --speak` ran for real at 22:51 UTC (`gemini-3.1-flash-lite`, made-up text, under a cent) and **every case behaved as expected**: three reflections ready, of 176, 187 and 170 words with prayers of 53, 49 and 46 words, and their passages (Psalm 127:1–2, Psalm 103:2–4 and Psalm 34:18) verified against the Bible API (420, 29 and 28 ms); the crisis sentence decided by the phrase list with no model; the quiet crisis found by the model as `uncertain` in 1.1 s; a passage that does not exist replaced by Philippians 4:6–7; each reflection 4.2 to 5.0 s with two attempts (the screening and the reflection); and `--speak` printing "speech: not set up" because no speech secret exists. The words to read aloud come to about 240 to 290 (the devotional, the prayer, the verse and four short lines), which is roughly 1.7 to 2 minutes at Chirp's pace, against AGENTS.md's "about 60 seconds": an estimate from word counts, made before any MP3 existed (the real numbers are in the "Real Google checks" bullet below). **Mutations:** 34 deliberate breakages in a scratch copy of `supabase/functions` (never the repo, no `.env.local`), each run with `deno test --no-check` over six test files so that only a failing test counts: **34 caught by named tests, 0 survived, 0 did not run.** They are the prompt's 21 (two of them tried two ways: the limit counting crisis or failed entries, itself or a lapsed claim; the in-flight order; trial and Pro exempt; the limit before the screening; a crisis getting a 402; the 402 body; the held-back recording's words and its retry; speech for a crisis; the speech-only retry screening; `saveAudio`'s empty-path filter; a speech failure failing the request; an unverified passage read; a link in the response; `TTS_FAILED` not cleared; the start time; the log carrying script text; `failEntry` after the save) and 11 of this build's (the upload cap, an off-by-one, `resetsAt`, the `markSpeechFailed` and `saveTranscript` guards, `ttsChars`, an unknown plan failing open, no upsert, no `TTS_FAILED` mark, `unexpected` for `storage`, a wrong entry id in the 402). One more was run to check a claim and **survived, as an equivalent mutant:** removing only `is_crisis = false` from the count changes nothing, because a crisis row never holds a devotional (the table's crisis check) and the `devotional_script is not null` filter already keeps it out, so mutation 1 drops both. The contract comparison bites (`contractParity.ts(22,10)` TS2344 with one extra field on the server's `ProcessEntryLimitError`, the file restored byte for byte), a planted `var` in a new `_shared` file is caught by the three-folder lint, the signed-URL words appear in one comment only, and `log.ts` holds the only `console.` call of the functions.
- Real Google checks (2026-10-24; **the MP3 facts in this bullet are history: the follow-up entry after this one made the devotional a WAV that same day**; after the user did steps B and C in the Cloud console: the API on, the service account `gracehush-tts` with no roles, and a key; the implementer did step D when the user said "run the commands your self"). **(1) The key, by name and shape.** One script, run from a scratch folder outside the repo, appended `GOOGLE_TTS_CREDENTIALS_B64` to the local `supabase/functions/.env.local` (git ignores it: checked with `git check-ignore`), keeping the old content byte for byte and printing yes or no answers only. The saved line decodes to the key file's own `type`, `client_email` and `private_key`, the function's own `parseServiceAccount` reads it as a service account, `readTtsConfig` answered `bad (TTS_VOICE_NAME)` until the voice was written and `ready` after, and Google's token endpoint accepted the key (a token came back and was not printed). **Step 22 follow-up — the clean voice: a lossless WAV instead of the MP3 (2026-10-24).**

- What happened: the user listened to the first real devotional (`real-reflection.mp3`, Chirp 3 HD Aoede at 0.88) and said the voice was all right but had "a little bit of silent robotic voice effect", not a clean human voice, and no real feeling; "fix that". The two complaints had two causes, so they were separated with A/B files of the same text and voice: the MP3 at 32 kbps (the only MP3 Google writes) and the slowed rate, against the lossless LINEAR16 at both rates. The user heard the WAV files as "clean and realistic" and chose `ab-chirp-0.88-wav.wav`: the voice they had chosen, lossless. So the robotic edge was the MP3's compression, and the fix is the format, not the voice.
- Decisions (the user's "go ahead and implement your professional recommendation"): (1) The function asks for `LINEAR16` and stores the WAV as `{userId}/devotionals/{entryId}.wav`, `audio/wav`; the voice, the rate, the script, the time budget and the best-effort rules are unchanged. (2) One additive migration lets the bucket accept `audio/wav`; nothing else in the bucket or its policies changes. (3) The length comes from the WAV header (`wavDuration.ts`), exact to the sample; the MP3 frame walker, its tests and its four fixtures are gone (dead code is deleted, not kept). (4) The size is accepted as the cost: about 48 KB a second, 5.5 MB for a two-minute devotional, twelve times the MP3. The alternatives were all refused or worse: a 64 kbps MP3 (refused by the API for these voices), Opus (unplayable on iPhones and not in the bucket), a 16 kHz WAV (a third smaller, but not the sound the user chose), and a server-side AAC transcode (a V2 job if download volume ever costs). Supabase's price page (read 2026-10-24): the Free plan holds 1 GB of files and 5 GB of downloads a month (about 180 devotionals and 900 plays), Pro 100 GB and 250 GB (then $0.0213 and $0.09 a GB). Step 26 keeps a played devotional on the phone, so each is downloaded once. (5) The feeling is left as it is: Gemini-TTS, the engine that takes a style prompt, was probed and parked (the vendor note has the facts and the prices); the user chose the lossless Chirp file, which reads scripture with no language model between the text and the voice.
