**Step 22 follow-up 2 — Expo SDK 58 upgrade (2026-10-24).**

- What happened: the user's iPhone stopped opening the project ("Project is incompatible with this version of Expo Go. The installed version of Expo Go is for SDK 58.0.0. The project you opened uses SDK 57"). They had changed nothing: the App Store updates Expo Go by itself, an iPhone can only run the newest Expo Go, and Expo's own message says an older one cannot be installed. SDK 58 reached npm on 2026-09-29 (`expo@58.0.0`), Expo's announcement is dated 2026-10-15 and said the new Expo Go would reach both stores within a few weeks (its Android APK, 58.0.5, is on `expo/expo-go-releases`; Google Play was not checked). The Android emulator's hand-installed Expo Go 57.0.9 still opens an SDK 57 project, so only the iPhone broke. The ways back to a working iPhone were: upgrade the project, test only on the Android emulator until the development build, or bring the paid Apple developer account forward (a development build); the standing instruction keeps that account last.
- Decision (the user's): upgrade to SDK 58. I proposed it as the recommended path and asked for a clear "apply". The user answered, with the error screenshot, "It says i should Upgrade the Project to SDK 58.0.0", which I took as the go-ahead (their own words: the system's background-task notices that arrived meanwhile were not treated as consent). I first proved the whole upgrade in a scratch copy outside the repo, leaving the working tree and the user's own dev server alone, then applied it. The user stopped their dev server twice when asked, because it holds files that `npm ci` replaces.
- Packages: the versions come by hand from SDK 58's `bundledNativeModules.json` (the same result as `expo install --fix`), plus `expo ~58.0.19` and `@types/react ~19.3.0` from SDK 58's template. 35 package ranges changed, the main ones being React Native 0.86.3 to 0.88.0, React and React DOM 19.2.3 to 19.3.0, `react-native-reanimated` 4.5.1 to 4.7.1, `react-native-worklets` 0.10.1 to 0.13.0, `react-native-screens` ~4.26.0 to ~4.30.0, `react-native-safe-area-context` ~5.7.0 to ~5.10.1, `react-native-gesture-handler` ~2.32.0 to ~3.3.0 (no app code imports it), `expo-router` ~57.0.29 to ~58.0.26 (unused), `@expo/ui` ~57.0.26 to ~58.0.23, `expo-notifications` ~57.0.24 to ~58.1.6 and `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` 9.1.0 to 9.2.1. Left as they were: `@clerk/expo` 4.13.0 (its peer range is expo >=54 <59), NativeWind 5.0.0-rc.0 and `react-native-css` 3.1.0-rc.0 (neither caps React Native), `@supabase/supabase-js`, React Navigation 7.x, zustand, TypeScript 6.0.3, ESLint 9.39.5, Tailwind 4.1.12 and `lightningcss` 1.30.1 (the override stays). The lockfile gained Expo's own `noxcturnal` (github.com/expo/noxcturnal, needed by `@expo/metro-config` 58). Node 24.15 on this machine meets SDK 58's requirement (22.13 or newer on the 22 line, 24.3 or newer on the 24 line, or 26).
- Source changes (7 files, no change of behavior): React Native 0.87 made its TypeScript API strict (see the new vendor note), so `useRef<Text>`, `useRef<TextInput>` and `useRef<ScrollView>` became `TextInstance`, `TextInputInstance` and `ScrollViewInstance` in `CrisisSupportPanel`, `EmailSheet`, `NewEntryScreen`, `WriteSection` (its `inputRef` prop) and `ProfileScreen`, and `JournalFeedScreen` and `PrayersScreen` pass `header ?? undefined` and `emptyState ?? undefined` to their list. The type check found 15 errors in those 7 files before the fixes. `NewEntryScreen.tsx` also carries step 22's own comment change, so a commit split needs `git add -p`.
- npm trap: with the new date picker, `npm install` fails with ERESOLVE (its optional peer `react-native-windows` conflicts with React Native 0.88). What worked: `npm install --legacy-peer-deps` once, then a plain `npm install` to heal the lockfile. The result passes `npm ci` in strict mode. A lockfile made with `--legacy-peer-deps` alone fails `npm ci` ("not in sync"), because strict mode also installs the peers `@react-native/metro-config`, `metro-config`, `metro-runtime` and `flow-parser`.
- Verified in the scratch copy first and again in the real project after `npm ci` (908 packages, 7 minutes): `expo-doctor` 20 of 20; `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` exit 0; `expo export` for Android (1,978 modules) and iOS (2,005 modules) with Hermes, and **the two bundle hashes are identical in the scratch copy and the real project**; the 88 profile-sync checks and the contract parity check (7 of 7) pass. `expo export` printed once "Error while reading cache, falling back to a full crawl: Unable to deserialize cloned data": it is the old SDK 57 Metro cache read by the new Metro, and it rebuilds itself. `npm ls` shows two extraneous WebAssembly packages (`@napi-rs/wasm-runtime` and `@tybys/wasm-util`) and the `lightningcss` override, which are harmless. The Deno side (the Edge Functions) does not use these packages and was not touched.
- Checked against SDK 58's list of breaking changes (Expo's announcement), in `src`: no use of `InteractionManager`, the `Touchable` root export, `NativeMethods`, the `Modal` `animated` prop or deep imports from `react-native/Libraries`; `StatusBar` comes from `expo-status-bar`; `@expo/ui/community/datetime-picker` still exists in `@expo/ui` 58.0.23. SDK 58's CLI no longer has the `@react-navigation` guard and `expo-router` 58 no longer depends on React Navigation, so `EXPO_ROUTER_DISABLE_RN_NAVIGATION_CHECK` in `.env` is now unneeded but left in place, harmless. Behavior changes that can matter later: `expo-notifications` shows foreground notifications by default (step 28), `@expo/ui`'s `Host` aligns to the top on iOS and its stacks changed their default spacing (the two date-picker sheets use only `DateTimePicker`), `expo-sqlite` drops libSQL (step 24 does not need it) and R8 is on by default in Android release builds.
- Not verified: everything that runs. The iPhone in Expo Go 58 is the first real test (NativeWind 5 rc on React Native 0.88, every screen, both date pickers, the keyboard-synced layouts, haptics, Fast Refresh). The Android emulator was not run: it needs the SDK 58 Expo Go (`Expo-Go-58.0.5.apk` was downloaded) and the host had about 1.2 GB of memory free. Whether the Expo Go guard for `expo-notifications` on Android is still needed is not known (the module error was seen on Expo Go 57.0.9 only).
- Open items: (1) the user restarts the dev server with `npx expo start -c` (its cache is from SDK 57) and opens the project on the iPhone; anything that breaks at runtime is the next job. (2) The Android emulator needs Expo Go 58 before it can run the project again: install the APK, and mind the Step 17 trap (its Expo Go still holds the user's own session and unsent reminder times). (3) Each new SDK repeats this for an iPhone in Expo Go (about every three months), so the development build of step 35 is also what ends it; the React Native 0.87 and 0.88 release notes were not read beyond Expo's list. (4) Nothing of the upgrade is committed (only when the user asks): one commit for `package.json`, `package-lock.json` and the 7 source files, kept apart from step 22's work. (5) The step 22 hosted check on the phone (the free run, then the pro run) still waits for the iPhone to open the app again.

**Step 22 follow-up 3 — the fonts after the SDK 58 upgrade (2026-10-24).**

- What happened: on the iPhone (Expo Go 58) every text drew in the iOS system font. Colours, sizes and spacing were right, so NativeWind's classes compiled, and the compiled styles named `Fraunces_600SemiBold` and the others (checked in the iOS bundle). The user's screenshots from before the upgrade, Fraunces on every heading and card, were the target.
- How it was found: nothing on this machine was wrong (the dev server serves all eight TTFs through the exact URL expo-asset 58 builds, with the same bytes and hash, on all three URL implementations the app can be running; expo-font 58's iOS alias code resolves a single-face alias as 57 did; the eight font files are static), so the running app on the phone was read through the dev server's Hermes inspector (`/json/list`, then `Runtime.evaluate` over a WebSocket, read-only): `ExpoFontLoader.getLoadedFonts()` was `[]`, `Asset.fromModule` threw `Cannot read property 'getAssetByID' of undefined`, react-native's exports held `AssetRegistry` while NativeWind's wrapper (the module expo-asset's `react-native` import resolved to) did not, and loading the eight fonts through the real registry registered all eight, so download, cache and CoreText registration are fine. The cause is the NativeWind vendor note above.
- Changed: `metro.config.js` (the resolver bypass for `node_modules/expo-asset/`) and `App.tsx` (a development-only `console.warn` when `useFonts` reports an error; the gate's behaviour is unchanged). The temporary font readout added to `DevToolsPanel.tsx` during the diagnosis was removed (the file is byte-identical to before). No dependency, token or screen changed.
- Verified: in the dev bundle expo-asset's `Asset.js` now depends on `react-native/index.js` while `PrimaryButton.tsx` and `JournalFeedScreen.tsx` keep the react-native-css wrapper, and the compiled font families are unchanged; `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` exit 0; `expo export` for iOS (71 s) and Android (73 s) exit 0, and in the iOS export expo-asset's module depends on the react-native index module. A first version of the bypass matched the origin path with a regex written through a shell heredoc that lost a backslash, so it never matched a Windows path (found with a trace in the resolver and a bundle of expo-asset alone); the file now compares with `path.join(__dirname, "node_modules", "expo-asset") + path.sep`.
- Not verified: the permanent fix on the iPhone. The user's next screenshot (the Journal with the Filter sheet open, 4:56 PM) shows Fraunces and DM Sans, but it does not prove the fix: the dev server on port 8081 (started 15:30) predates the new `metro.config.js` (16:40), and Expo's CLI only watches that file to print a warning, it does not restart Metro (`FileNotifier` in `@expo/cli` 58.1.15); and the diagnosis had registered the eight fonts under the app's own aliases inside the running Expo Go process through the inspector. Those registrations are process-wide native state (CoreText's process scope, and a file-level alias table in expo-font's Swift code, read in `FontFamilyAliasManager.swift`, not tested), so they probably survive a JS reload and would hide a failing `useFonts` until Expo Go is force-quit. The test that proves the fix: restart the dev server with `npx expo start -c`, force-quit Expo Go on the iPhone, reopen the project, and look for Fraunces headings on the Journal with no `[fonts] …` warning in the terminal. Everything before that is proven: the registry reaches expo-asset in the new bundle, and the fonts register on the phone once it does.
- Open items: the bypass goes when react-native-css forwards `AssetRegistry` (worth an upstream issue: every NativeWind v5 app on SDK 58 that loads fonts with `useFonts` is affected); then the step 22 phone check (the free run, then the pro run).

**Step 09 follow-up 2 — the Filter sheet's chips are centered (2026-10-24).**

- What happened: the user sent an iPhone screenshot of the Journal's Filter sheet and said the filter chips were not centered horizontally in the card. The chip row was `flex-row flex-wrap gap-2` with no `justify-*`, so both wrapped rows (All, Anxious, Weary, Grateful and Grieving, Hopeful, Prayer requests) sat against the left edge, with roughly 85 to 90dp of empty space on the right and none on the left (read from the screenshot, about 1.28 px per dp).
- Decision: `justify-center` on the chip row, so each wrapped row is centered in the sheet. The title ("Filter reflections", left-aligned), the "Show n reflections" button and Reset are unchanged.
- Changed: `src/screens/journal/JournalFilterSheet.tsx` (one class). `justify-center` is used all over the app, so a hot reload carries it. Nothing was installed.
- Verified: `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` exit 0. Not verified: the look on the iPhone, which was not attached to the dev server when this was done, so the chip positions could not be measured (centering each flex line with `justify-content: center` is standard Yoga behaviour, not tested here).
- Open items: the other wrapped chip rows (the ambient chips on Now Playing and on Profile) are left-aligned and were not touched; the sheet's title stays left-aligned.

**Step 22 follow-up 4 — hosted rollout of the WAV audio and the weekly limit, and the first phone check (2026-10-24).**

- What was done on the real project, all by the user in the dashboard and the terminal (the implementer never holds a management token): the bucket migration `20261024120000_journal_media_accepts_wav.sql` was pasted into the SQL editor ("Success. No rows returned") and `rls_selftest.sql` was run with a summary query appended to it: 46 checks, 46 passed, none failed (the editor's "Potential issues detected" dialogs, caused by the self-test's temporary table, are answered with "Run without RLS", as in step 18); `secrets set --env-file supabase/functions/.env.local` and `functions deploy` for `process-entry` and for `confirm-scripture` (both with `--use-api`) ran without error; and probes with no sign-in answered each function's own 401, 401, 405 and 204 (the CORS preflight), so the new code is live.
- The first phone check (run 1), as the free test account `gracehush17b+clerk_test@example.com` (17:17 on the phone's clock): the first line read `account plan: free` (so the app ran the new check) and the last `Done: 13 passed, 5 skipped.`, with no FAIL, which is the expected result for a free account that is already over its weekly limit. SKIP: the written entry and the instructions entry (each would have got the 402), same id again, the speech retry, and the blocked entry after an upgrade (these need a Pro account). PASS: `confirm-scripture` for an entry that does not exist (404, 1.0 s), a malformed id (400, 0.7 s) and a reflection waiting for its passage (Psalm 127:1–2 verified in 1.4 s; the phone's own read of the public Bible matched: 232 characters, same letters and marks); text too short (422), text too long (413), someone else's recording (400) and audio in the body (400); the made-up crisis sentence (1.3 s) and the quiet one that only the model finds (2.9 s), both a crisis entry with no devotional and no affirmations; the recording (a retry of a finished entry, 0.7 s); the free limit (a new entry got `402 LIMIT_REACHED`, the entry was saved, `usage` 9 of 3, `resetsAt` Monday 2026-10-26, 2.4 s; the 9 is the real count, because earlier test runs made 9 devotionals this week before the limit existed); and a crisis sentence over the limit was answered as a crisis (200), never a 402.
- Observation, not a failure: the recording retry took 0.7 s. With speech on, the server adds audio to a ready entry that has a devotional and none yet when the same request is sent again (about 15 s), so speech may be off, may have failed fast, or that old entry may not qualify. The Pro run's `devotional audio` line and the function log's `tts` field settle it (`off` means the speech secrets are missing from the hosted project).
- Not yet done: run 2 as Pro. Set the test account's `subscription_tier` to `pro` in the Table Editor (its Clerk User ID is the row's `id`), then run the check once, at least 10 minutes after run 1 because of the 10-entries-in-10-minutes rate limit. It proves the spoken devotional end to end (audio of 20 s to 5 minutes, the app's own signed link answering `audio/wav` and starting with RIFF/WAVE, the public address refused) and makes the entry that run 1 held back. Then the function log export (30 fields, `tts`, `audioMs`, `ttsChars`, the limit words) is read for anything sensitive.

**Publishing the SDK 58, font, audio and filter work (2026-10-24).**

- Decisions (the user asked to commit and push all edits to GitHub "dev/main", with commits named properly and in a standard way so that other developers can read the repository): only `main` exists, so it was `main` again. Conventional Commits, an imperative subject of at most 72 characters with no step or prompt number, a body wrapped at 72 columns that says what changed and why, and one concern per commit, each one typechecked alone in a temporary worktree. No force-push.
- The six commits, in order: `chore(deps): upgrade to Expo SDK 58 and React Native 0.88`; `fix(fonts): give expo-asset the real react-native under NativeWind`; `fix(journal): center the filter chips in the filter sheet`; `feat(db): accept WAV audio in the journal media bucket`; `feat(functions): speak devotionals and enforce the weekly free limit`; and this documentation commit. `NewEntryScreen.tsx` mixed an SDK type fix with an unrelated comment update, so it was split by hunk (a patch applied to the index) between the first and fifth commits. Left out on purpose, as before: `.claude/skills/`, `.commandcode/` and `LICENSE`.
- The personal payment-processing detail of the Google billing set-up was left out of the billing log entry and the step prompt before anything went public (none of it was ever in a pushed commit); the amounts of credit and the plan facts stay. The step prompt also got a banner saying the devotional is a WAV now (its MP3 details describe the first build), and step G's "plays an MP3" sentence was corrected.
- Audit before committing: 16 literal values (every value of both `.env.local` files, the project ref, the Clerk domain and instance name, the issuer host, the Text-to-Speech service account's address and key lines, and the Cloud project id) searched in every staged tree, 17 shapes (each proven on a planted sample) on every added line, the lockfile's package sources, the commit messages, and the two WAV fixtures' chunk lists (only `fmt ` and `data`, no embedded metadata): no findings.
- Verified before committing: `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` exit 0; `deno check` of both functions, `deno lint` (72 files), `deno fmt --check` (83 files), 348 tests and the parity check (7 of 7) pass; each of the first five commits typechecks alone. The offline PGlite re-run of the migration runner was declined by the user and not run (the hosted self-test passed 46 of 46).

## Final Reminder

Before every feature implementation:

- Read this file
- Follow it strictly
- Build clean, simple, maintainable code
- Replicate the UI exactly when designs are provided, but ignore Stitch preview artifacts
- Protect the user's privacy, the accuracy of scripture, and the safety of people in crisis
- Record any decision you add or change in this file, then copy it to `prompts/AGENTS.md`
